Quasi-canonical AFL and Arithmetic Transfer conjectures at parahoric levels
Chao Li, Michael Rapoport, Wei Zhang

Abstract
. In the first part of the paper, we formulate several arithmetic transfer conjectures, which are variants of the arithmetic fundamental lemma conjecture in the presence of ramification. The ramification comes from the choice of non-hyperspecial parahoric level structure. We prove a graph version of these arithmetic transfer conjectures, by relating it to the quasi-canonical arithmetic fundamental lemma , which we also establish. We relate some of the arithmetic transfer conjectures to the arithmetic fundamental lemma conjecture for the whole Hecke algebra in our recent paper [ 14 ] . As a consequence, we prove these conjectures in some simple cases. In the second part of the paper, we elucidate the structure of an integral model of a certain member of the almost selfdual Rapoport–Zink tower, thereby proving conjectures in [ 11 ] and [ 15 ] . This result allows us to verify the hypotheses of the graph version of the arithmetic transfer conjectures in a particular case.



1.
Introduction

Inspired by the Jacquet–Rallis approach
[
9
]
to the global Gan–Gross–Prasad conjecture,
the third author proposed a relative trace formula approach to the arithmetic Gan–Gross–Prasad conjecture. In this context, he formulated the
arithmetic fundamental lemma
(AFL) conjecture
[
27
]
. The AFL conjecturally relates the special value of the derivative of an orbital integral to an arithmetic intersection number on a
Rapoport–Zink formal moduli space of
p
-divisible groups
(RZ-space) attached to a unitary group. The AFL formula takes the following form.

Let
p
be an odd prime number. Let
F_{0}
be a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and let
F/F_{0}
be an unramified quadratic extension. Let
W_{1}
be a non-split
F/F_{0}
-hermitian space of dimension
n+1
and let
W_{1}^{\flat}
be the perp-space of a vector
u_{1}\in W_{1}
of unit length (the
special vector
). Let
G^{\prime}=\Res_{F/F_{0}}(\mathrm{GL}_{n}\times\mathrm{GL}_{n+1})
and
G_{W_{1}}=\mathrm{U}(W_{1}^{\flat})\times\mathrm{U}(W_{1})
. Then the following identity holds for all matching regular semi-simple elements
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})

and

g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})
,

\left\langle\Delta,g\Delta\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n,n+1}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}(\gamma,{\mathbf{1}}).

Here
q
denotes the cardinality of the residue field of
F_{0}
. On the RHS,
\operatorname{\partial Orb}(\gamma,{\mathbf{1}})
is the special value of the derivative of the weighted orbital integral of the unit element in the spherical Hecke algebra
\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime\flat}\times K^{\prime}}
of the natural hyperspecial compact subgroup
K^{\prime\flat}\times K^{\prime}
of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(F)\times\mathrm{GL}_{n+1}(F)
. We note that, in contrast to
[
27
]
, the natural transfer factor
\omega(\gamma)
of
[
19
]
has been incorporated in the definition of
\operatorname{\partial Orb}(\gamma,{\mathbf{1}})
. On the LHS appears the intersection number of the diagonal cycle
\Delta
of the product RZ-space
\mathcal{N}_{n,n+1}=\mathcal{N}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
with its translate under the automorphism of
\mathcal{N}_{n,n+1}
induced by
g
. Here, for any
n
,
\mathcal{N}_{n}
is the Rapoport–Zink moduli space of framed
basic
principally polarized
p
-divisible groups with action of
O_{F}
of signature
(1,n-1)
. Both sides of the identity only depend on the orbits of
\gamma
, resp.
g
, under natural group actions.

The AFL conjecture is now known to hold in general, cf. W. Zhang
[
29
]
, Mihatsch–Zhang
[
17
]
, Z. Zhang
[
30
]
. These proofs are global in nature. Local proofs of the AFL are known for
n=1,2
(W. Zhang
[
27
]
), and for minuscule elements (Rapoport–Terstiege–Zhang
[
20
]
, He–Li–Zhu
[
6
]
).

It is essential for the AFL conjecture that one is dealing with a situation that is unramified in every possible sense, i.e., the quadratic extension
F/F_{0}
defining the unitary group is unramified, and the special vector has unit length, and the function appearing in the derivative of the orbital integral is the characteristic function of a hyperspecial maximal open compact subgroup. The AFL has to be modified when these unramifiedness hypotheses are dropped. In the context of the
fundamental lemma
(FL) conjecture of Jacquet–Rallis, this question leads naturally to the
smooth transfer
(ST) conjecture, proved by the third author in the non-archimedean case
[
28
]
. In the arithmetic context, this question naturally leads to the problem of formulating
arithmetic transfer
(AT) conjectures. A number of such AT conjectures are formulated in
[
19
]
. These conjectures are proved in a small number of cases, cf.
[
18
]
,
[
19
]
.

The limiting factor to formulating such conjectures is the geometric side of the conjecture. Indeed, for formulating an AT conjecture (at least in the naive sense), one has to make sure that the ambient Rapoport–Zink space is regular (otherwise, the Serre definition of the intersection multiplicity is not applicable), and this strongly limits the possibilities, see
[
7
]
. In the present paper, we impose on the quadratic extension
F/F_{0}
to be
unramified
but allow the polarization in the RZ-moduli problem to be non-principal. More precisely, let
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r]}
be the formal moduli space of formal
O_{F_{0}}
-modules with action of
O_{F}
of signature
(1,n-1)
and a compatible polarization of type
r
, i.e., the kernel is killed by
\varpi
(the uniformizer of
F_{0}
) and is of order
q^{2r}
. Thus
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}=\mathcal{N}_{n}
. In
[
19
]
, we considered on the geometric side the natural closed embedding of
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}
into
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[1]}
and its graph
\Delta
in the product
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[1]}
. We then formed the intersection product of
\Delta
with its translate under the (regular semi-simple) automorphism
g
of
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[1]}
and related it to the derivative of an orbital integral at a matching element
\gamma
. As highlighted above, the intersection product makes sense, since
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[1]}
is regular. More generally, in
[
30
]
, Z. Zhang considers for any
r\geq 0
the natural embedding of
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r]}
into
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r^{\prime}]}
, where
r^{\prime}=r
or
r^{\prime}=r+1
and forms an intersection on
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r^{\prime}]}
. However, this last product is not regular, unless
r=0
; therefore, when
r\geq 1
, this product is replaced in loc. cit. by a blow-up. Using this blow-up, an AT identity is formulated and indeed proved.

In the present paper, we want to replace the pair
(r,r^{\prime})=(0,1)
by the pair
(1,0)
, or more generally by
(r,0)
for arbitrary
r>0
. The product
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[0]}
is regular so that intersection products make sense on this space. A problem arises however from the fact that there is no natural embedding of
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r]}
into
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[0]}
. Rather, one has to replace this embedding by a diagram linking
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r]}
to
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[0]}
,

(1.0.1)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 11.22803pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.12445pt\raise-11.68251pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 81.57603pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 10.09908pt\raise-11.68251pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 11.22804pt\raise-25.14429pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-11.22803pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 42.48384pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 79.73965pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[0]}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

In the generic fiber (i.e., for the corresponding rigid-analytic spaces), the morphism
\pi_{1}
is part of the RZ-tower corresponding to suitable open compact subgroups of a unitary group of dimension
n
. In other words,
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
is an integral model of a certain member of the RZ-tower. When
r
is even, the new space
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
is simply the RZ-space
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r,0]}
(corresponding to a parahoric subgroup in a quasisplit unitary group) and the map
\pi_{1}
is then just the obvious transition map. When
r
is odd, the new space
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
(corresponding to a non-parahoric in a non-quasisplit unitary group) is very mysterious. One of our main results concerns the structure of
\widetilde{}\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1]}
. The following theorem is a simplified version of Theorem
14.6.2
in the text.

Theorem 1.0.1
.

(i)

The formal scheme
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
is regular of dimension
n
.

(ii)

The morphism
\pi_{1}
is finite flat of degree
q+1
, étale away from the
closed balloon locus

\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}
, and totally ramified along
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}
. The closed balloon locus
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}
is a Cartier divisor which is a disjoint sum of copies of
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
, enumerated by the self-dual lattices in the split
F/F_{0}
-hermitian space of dimension
n
.

(iii)

The morphism
\pi_{2}
is proper and factors through the Kudla–Rapoport divisor
\mathcal{Z}(u)
of
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
corresponding to the
special vector

u
of valuation one. The resulting morphism
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}\mathcal{Z}(u)
is a blow-up in a zero-dimensional reduced subscheme
\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\rm cent}
and the exceptional divisor
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],{\rm exc}}
in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
is a reduced Cartier divisor which maps isomorphically to
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}
under
\pi_{1}
.

Here, the blow-up is meant in the generalized sense of EGA, i.e., a blow-up in an ideal sheaf with support in
\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\rm cent}
.

Let us illustrate this theorem in the case
n=2
. In this case, we have

•

\mathcal{N}_{2}^{[1]}
is isomorphic to the Drinfeld half plane (this is the
alternative interpretation
of the Drinfeld halfplane in
[
12
]
). The special fiber is a union of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
’s with dual graph a
(q+1)
-valent tree. These
\mathbb{P}^{1}
’s are of two kinds: even and odd; if two such
\mathbb{P}^{1}
’s intersect, they are of different parity.

•

\mathcal{N}_{2}^{[1],\bullet}
consists of all even
\mathbb{P}^{1}
’s. The preimage under
\pi_{1}
of an even
\mathbb{P}^{1}
is nonreduced (a “fat”
\mathbb{P}^{1}
with multiplicity
q+1
). The preimage of an odd
\mathbb{P}^{1}
under
\pi_{1}
is a Fermat curve of degree
q+1
.

•

The special fiber of
\mathcal{Z}(u)
consists of Fermat curves of degree
q+1
intersecting at points in
\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\rm cent}
(the centers of the blow-up morphism
\pi_{2}
), and, conversely, all such intersection points are contained in
\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\rm cent}
. Each Fermat curve contains
q+1
intersection points and
q+1
Fermat curves pass through each intersection point.

•

The preimage of a point of
\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\rm cent}
under
\pi_{2}
is an exceptional divisor of
\pi_{2}
and can be identified with the underlying reduced scheme of a fat
\mathbb{P}^{1}
corresponding to a specified even
\mathbb{P}^{1}
in
\mathcal{N}_{2}^{[1]}
.

Figure
1
illustrates the morphisms
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
in (
3.8.2
) (for
n=2
and
r=1
) on the special fibers locally around a blow-up point of
\mathcal{Z}(u)
.

Figure 1.
n=2

Theorem
1.0.1
was conjectured in the unpublished manuscript
[
11
]
of Kudla and the second author and was used in the extension by the first and the third author of the Kudla–Rapoport intersection conjecture to the
almost self-dual
case in
[
15
]
.

Returning to the case of general
r
, we obtain two closed embeddings,

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\subset{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}},

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\subset{{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}.

The first is given by
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
. The second is the graph of
\pi_{2}
. On the RHS of both inclusions, there is a (compatible) action of
G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})=\mathrm{U}(W_{1}^{\flat})(F_{0})\times\mathrm{U}(W_{1})(F_{0})
. Here the perp-space is taken for the special vector
u_{1}\in W_{1}
of length
\varpi^{\varepsilon}
, where
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(r)\in\{0,1\}
is the parity of
r
. Correspondingly, there are two intersection numbers arising in this context, given as follows,

\displaystyle{\rm(i)}

\displaystyle\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}

\displaystyle:=\chi(\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1},{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\cap^{\mathbb{L}}g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}),

\displaystyle{\rm(ii)}

\displaystyle\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}

\displaystyle:=\chi({\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1},{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\cap^{\mathbb{L}}g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}).

Here
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})
. The first expression makes sense, since
\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}
is regular. The second expression makes sense if
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
is regular. We conjecture (in a more precise way, cf. Conjecture
3.5.1
) that this is always the case. This conjecture holds for
r=1
and when
r
is even.

Here the variant (i) leads to our AT conjecture of type
(r,0)
, by which we indicate the type of the vertex lattices defining the parahoric level of the relevant RZ spaces. The variant (ii) leads to the graph version of our AT conjecture. Let us first state our result on the graph version, which is reasonably complete (Corollary
6.2.3
, Proposition
6.3.1
, Theorem
8.1.1
). In the statement below, there appear compact open subgroups
\widetilde{K}_{n}^{[r]}\subset K_{n}^{[r]}
of
\mathrm{U}(W_{0}^{\flat})
(the first a non-parahoric for odd
r
, the second a maximal parahoric) and
K_{n+1}
of
\mathrm{U}(W_{0})
(a hyperspecial maximal parahoric). Here
W_{0}
denotes the split hermitian space of dimension
n+1
and
W_{0}^{\flat}
the perp-space for the special vector
u_{0}
of length
\varpi^{\varepsilon}
. Then
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
, resp.
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r]}
, resp.
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
are the members of the RZ tower corresponding to the open compact subgroups
\widetilde{K}_{n}^{[r]}
, resp.
K_{n}^{[r]}
, resp.
K_{n+1}
. On the
\mathrm{GL}
-side, we have analogous open compact subgroups
\widetilde{K}_{n}^{\prime[r]}\subset K^{\prime}_{n}\subset\mathrm{GL}_{n}(F)
and
K^{\prime}_{n+1}\subset\mathrm{GL}_{n+1}(F)
.

Theorem 1.0.2
.

Let
\varphi^{\prime}_{r}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
be as follows,

\varphi^{\prime}_{r}=\begin{cases}c_{r}c_{r}^{\prime}(q^{2(n+1)}-1)\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{K}_{n}^{\prime[r]}\times K^{\prime}_{n+1}}+c_{r}((-1)^{n+1}+1)\mathbf{1}_{G^{\prime}(O_{F_{0}})},&\text{when $r$ is odd}\\
c_{r}c_{r}^{\prime}\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{K}_{n}^{\prime[r]}\times K_{n+1}^{\prime}},&\text{when $r$ is even.}\end{cases}

Then
\varphi^{\prime}_{r}
is a transfer of
(c_{r}^{2}\cdot{\bf 1}_{\widetilde{K}_{n}^{[r]}\times K_{n+1}},0)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{0}})\times C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{1}})
.

Assume that
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[r]}
is regular. Then, if
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
,

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}_{r}\big)-\Orb\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}_{r,\mathrm{corr}}\big),

where

\varphi^{\prime}_{r,\mathrm{corr}}=\begin{cases}c_{r}\cdot(n+1)\,{\bf 1}_{G^{\prime}(O_{F_{0}})}\cdot\log q,&$n$\text{ is even, and $r$ is odd}\\
0,&$n$\text{ is odd or $r$ is even.}\end{cases}

Here
G^{\prime}(O_{F_{0}})
denotes a certain maximal compact subgroup of
G^{\prime}(F_{0})
. The integers
c_{r}
and
c^{\prime}_{r}
are related to the normalizations of measures, see (
6.1.11
). The proof of Theorem
1.0.2
is by reduction (via the factorization of
\pi_{2}
through the inclusion of the special divisor
\mathcal{Z}(u_{1})
of
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
) to the AFL when
r
is even, resp. to the quasi-canonical AFL when
r
is odd, see Theorem
8.1.1
. The topic of this latter variant of the AFL is another main theme of the paper, which we discuss next.

Let
\mathcal{Z}(u)\subset\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
be the Kudla–Rapoport divisor for a special vector of valuation
\varepsilon\in\{0,1\}
, cf.
[
10
]
. When
\varepsilon=0
, the structure of
\mathcal{Z}(u)=\Delta\simeq\mathcal{N}_{n}
is clear: it is a regular formal scheme of dimension
n
which is formally smooth over
\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
. When
\varepsilon=1
, the structure of
\mathcal{Z}(u)
is given by the following theorem (Theorem
14.5.2
).

Theorem 1.0.3
.

Let
u
be a special vector of valuation one. The formal scheme
\mathcal{Z}(u)
is regular of dimension
n
, and formally smooth over
\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
outside a zero-dimensional closed subset of
\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\rm red}
.

Note that
\mathcal{Z}(u)
is its own difference divisor, which implies the regularity of
\mathcal{Z}(u)
(regularity holds for any difference divisor
[
23
]
, cf. also
[
31
]
). When
n=1
, the formal scheme
\mathcal{Z}(u)
(in
\mathcal{N}_{2}
) is the quasi-canonical divisor of conductor one introduced in
[
10
]
, which is in turn closely related to the quasi-canonical lifting of level one of Gross.

The quasi-canonical AFL arises from the closed embedding,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}(u)\subset{\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}.

By the regularity of
\mathcal{Z}(u)
, there is the well-defined intersection number for
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{Z}(u),g\mathcal{Z}(u)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}

\displaystyle:=\chi(\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1},\mathcal{Z}(u)\cap^{\mathbb{L}}g\mathcal{Z}(u)).

Note that the AFL gives an analytic expression for this when
\varepsilon=0
. When
\varepsilon=1
, the corresponding statement is the following theorem (Theorem
7.2.1
). Recall the non-parahoric
\tilde{K}_{n}^{[1]}
of
\mathrm{U}(W_{0}^{\flat})(F_{0})
corresponding to the member
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[1]}
of the RZ-tower mentioned above. Also, let
K_{n+1}\subset\mathrm{U}(W_{0})(F_{0})
be the stabilizer of a selfdual lattice.

Theorem 1.0.4
.

Let
c_{1}^{\prime}=(q^{2}+1)(q^{2}-1)
. Consider the function

\varphi^{\prime}_{1}=c_{1}^{\prime}(q^{2(n+1)}-1)\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{K}_{n}^{\prime[1]}\times K^{\prime}_{n+1}}+((-1)^{n+1}+1)\mathbf{1}_{G^{\prime}(O_{F_{0}})}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime}).

Then
\varphi^{\prime}_{1}
is a transfer of
({\bf 1}_{\tilde{K}_{n}^{[1]}\times K_{n+1}},0)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{0}})\times C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{1}})
and, if
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle\mathcal{Z}(u),g\mathcal{Z}(u)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}_{1}\big)-\Orb\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}_{1,\mathrm{corr}}\big),

where

\varphi^{\prime}_{1,\mathrm{corr}}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{c^{\prime}_{1}}(n+1){\bf 1}_{G^{\prime}(O_{F_{0}})}\cdot\log q,&$n$\text{ is even, }\\
0,&$n$\text{ is odd.}\end{cases}

We now return to the AT problem, pertaining to the intersection number
\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}
above. Here we have only partial results. Quite generally, we have the following conjecture, see Conjecture
13.1.1
.

Conjecture 1.0.5
.

(i)

There exists a transfer
\varphi^{\prime}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
of
(c_{r}^{2}\cdot{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}\times K_{n+1}},0)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{0}})\times C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{1}})
such that, if
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}\big).

(ii)

For any transfer
\varphi^{\prime}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
of
(c_{r}^{2}\cdot{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}\times K_{n+1}},0)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{0}})\times C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{1}})
, there exists
\varphi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{corr}}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
such that if
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}\big)-\Orb\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{corr}}\big).

Under additional hypotheses, we can give candidates for the function
\varphi^{\prime}
in part (i), cf. Conjecture
9.2.1
. We recall from
[
14
, §3.6]
the base change homomorphism between spherical Hecke algebras,

\mathrm{BC}:\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n+1}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}}.

Recall from
[
14
, §4]
the atomic Hecke function in
\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}}
, defined as the convolution,

\varphi_{r}:=\vol(K_{n}^{[r]})^{-1}{\bf 1}_{K_{n}K_{n}^{[r]}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}K_{n}}.

Conjecture 1.0.6
.

Assume that
r
is even. Let
\varphi^{\prime}
be any element in
\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n+1}}
such that

\mathrm{BC}(\varphi^{\prime})=\varphi_{r}\otimes{\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}},

(then
\varphi^{\prime}
is a transfer of
(\vol(K_{n}^{[r,0]})^{-2}\,{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}\times K_{n+1}},0)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{0}})\times C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{1}})
).
If
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}\big).

When
r=0
, Conjecture
1.0.6
recovers the arithmetic fundamental lemma. For arbitrary even
r
, we show that Conjecture
1.0.6
follows from the AFL in
[
14
]
(see Conjecture
9.2.3
) for certain (non unit) elements in the spherical Hecke algebra, cf. Corollary
9.2.5
. Unfortunately, we know of no case of even
r\geq 2
, where Conjecture
9.2.3
is proved.

When
r
is odd, we only can give
\varphi^{\prime}
in the following special case, cf. Conjecture
11.2.1
. Define the analogous atomic Hecke function

\varphi_{0}^{[n+1]}:={\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}K_{n+1}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}}\in\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}},

where we note that
\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}}
are both spherical Hecke algebras(!).

Conjecture 1.0.7
.

Let
r
be odd and assume
r=n
. Let
\varphi^{\prime}
be any element in
\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n+1}}
such that

\mathrm{BC}(\varphi^{\prime})={\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}\otimes\varphi_{0}^{[n+1]}\in\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}}.

If
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}\big).

Again, as for even
r
, Conjecture
1.0.7
follows from the AFL in
[
14
]
(see Conjecture
9.2.3
) for certain (non unit) elements in the spherical Hecke algebra, cf. Corollary
11.2.3
. For
n=1
, Conjecture
9.2.3
holds, cf.
[
14
, Thm. 7.5.1]
. Hence we obtain the following theorem.

Theorem 1.0.8
.

Conjecture
1.0.7
holds when
n=r=1
.

It would be very interesting to construct an explicit candidate for
\varphi^{\prime}
in Conjecture
1.0.5
, at least in the case
r=1
but when
n
is arbitrary. We hope to return to this problem in future work.

There is another kind of AT problem, related to a diagram similar to (
1.0.1
), but relating this time
\mathcal{N}_{n}
to
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r]}
, cf. (
3.10.1
),

(1.0.2)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.47166pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 33.47166pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.36809pt\raise-10.29166pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.3978pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}_{2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 79.81967pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 9.00732pt\raise-10.29166pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.3978pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.47168pt\raise-26.14566pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 90.90637pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-9.47166pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{n}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 40.72748pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 77.98329pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r]}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

The geometry of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}
is in a sense the opposite of that of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
. When
r
is even and
r\leq n
, then
{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}
is very singular but contains a closed formal subscheme
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{n}^{[r],+}
which is isomorphic to
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0,r]}
, hence is regular with semi-stable reduction, cf. §
3.10
. There is another closed formal subscheme
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{n}^{[r],-}
about which we know very little (e.g., if it is regular); then
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{n}^{[r]}
is the union of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{n}^{[r],+}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{n}^{[r],-}
. When
r
is odd, then
{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}\simeq\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0,r-1]}\simeq\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[r-1]}
is regular with semi-stable reduction.

We consider the intersection number arising in this context, given as follows,

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r]}}:=\chi(\mathcal{N}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r]},{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}\cap^{\mathbb{L}}g{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}),\quad g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0}).

This leads to our AT conjecture of type
(0,r)
. We have the following general conjecture (cf. Conjecture
13.2.1
), analogous to Conjecture
1.0.5
.

Conjecture 1.0.9
.

Let
r
be such that
0\leq r\leq n+1
, with parity
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(r)
. Let
W_{\varepsilon}
be the hermitian space of dimension
n+1
with invariant
(-1)^{\varepsilon}
, and denote by
W_{\varepsilon+1}
the hermitian space of the same dimension
n+1
and with opposite invariant. As before, the perp-spaces
W_{\varepsilon}^{\flat}
and
W_{\varepsilon+1}^{\flat}
are formed using special vectors of length
\varpi^{\varepsilon}
. Also, recall the function
\varphi_{r}^{[\varepsilon]}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{U}(W_{\varepsilon})}
from (
10.2.3
).

(i)

There exists
\varphi^{\prime}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
with transfer
({\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[0]}}\otimes\varphi_{r}^{[\varepsilon]},0)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{\varepsilon}})\times C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{\varepsilon+1}})
such that, if
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[r]},g\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[r]}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r]}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}\big).

(ii)

For any
\varphi^{\prime}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
transferring to
({\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[0]}}\otimes\varphi_{r}^{[\varepsilon]},0)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{\varepsilon}})\times C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{\varepsilon+1}})
, there exists
\varphi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{corr}}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
such that, if
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[r]},g\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[r]}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r]}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}\big)-\Orb\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{corr}}\big).

When
r
is even, we can give candidates for the function
\varphi^{\prime}
in part (i), cf Conjecture
10.3.1
.

Conjecture 1.0.10
.

Let
0\leq r\leq n+1
, with
r
even. Let
\varphi^{\prime}
be any element in
\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n+1}}
such that

\mathrm{BC}(\varphi^{\prime})={\bf 1}_{K_{n}}\otimes\varphi_{r}\in\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}}.

If
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[r]},g\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[r]}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}\big).

Again, when
r=0
, Conjecture
1.0.10
recovers the arithmetic fundamental lemma. For arbitrary even
r
, we show that Conjecture
1.0.10
follows from the AFL in
[
14
]
(see Conjecture
9.2.3
) for certain (non unit) elements in the spherical Hecke algebra, cf. Corollary
10.3.4
. When
r
is even and
r=n+1
, then there is a close relation between Conjectures
1.0.10
and
1.0.7
. For even
r
with
2\leq r\leq n
, there are also variants of Conjecture
1.0.10
involving the closed formal subschemes
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{n}^{[r],+}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{n}^{[r],-}
of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{n}^{[r]}
. If
r
is odd, we do not have a candidate for the function
\varphi^{\prime}
, unless
r=1
in which case we recover the AT conjecture made in
[
19
, §10]
, see §
12
.

Thm.
12.0.2

{\bf 1}_{K_{n}}\otimes{\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}}

We note that there are two extreme cases
(r=n,0)
and
(0,r^{\prime}=n+1)
in the table; they are in fact equivalent under the duality isomorphisms (
3.10.6
).

Let us comment on the scope of our AT conjectures. We indeed seem to have exhausted all possible cases under a couple of natural constraints, as we explain now. In addition to the Kudla–Rapoport
\mathcal{Z}
-divisor, we also use the
\mathcal{Y}
-divisor, cf.
[
30
, §5.2]
(and see §
3.10
). The AFL conjecture concerns the diagram

(1.0.3)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 11.25697pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 35.25697pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 83.32338pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 11.25697pt\raise-25.29895pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 94.694pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-11.25697pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 43.51395pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 81.77092pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[0]},}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

where
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\simeq\mathcal{Z}(u_{0})
, for a special vector of unit norm. Our spaces and cycles are variants of this diagram built on the following two considerations:

1)

we would like the ambient space to be a product of RZ spaces of
maximal parahoric levels
and to be regular.

2)

the
\mathcal{Z}
-divisor, resp. the
\mathcal{Y}
-divisor
\mathcal{Z}(u)^{[r]}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Y}(u)^{[r]}
on
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r]}
should be isomorphic to a lower-dimensional RZ space of maximal parahoric level. More precisely, we have the following
exceptional isomorphisms

(1.0.4)

\begin{cases}\mathcal{Z}(u)^{[r]}\simeq\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r]},&v(u)=0,\\
\mathcal{Y}(u)^{[r]}\simeq\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r-1]},&v(u)=-1.\end{cases}

These are also called
exceptional special divisors
. It is conceivable that exceptional special divisors on
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r]}
are characterized by the property that they are regular formal schemes (besides the case
\mathcal{Z}(u)^{[0]}
with
v(u)=1
).

We are led to the formation of “pull-back” diagrams of exceptional special divisors along the natural projection maps
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r_{1},r_{2}]}\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r_{i}]}
from RZ spaces of (non-maximal) parahoric levels, where
0\leq r_{1},r_{2}\leq n+1
and
r_{1}\equiv r_{2}\mod 2
:

(1.0.5)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widetilde{}\mathcal{Z}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 11.07642pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 86.37408pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-57.37889pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 49.1755pt\raise-50.06836pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.60417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\square}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 86.37408pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r_{1},r_{2}]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 104.60103pt\raise-57.37889pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 194.66786pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 194.66786pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r_{2}]}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-62.37408pt\raise-66.75781pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}^{[r_{1}]}=\mathcal{Z}(u_{0})^{[r_{1}]}\text{ or }\mathcal{Y}(u_{0})^{[r_{1}]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.3741pt\raise-66.75781pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 88.67795pt\raise-66.75781pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 88.67795pt\raise-66.75781pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r_{1}]}.}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 206.20206pt\raise-66.75781pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Here
\mathcal{C}^{[r_{i}]}
are as in (
1.0.4
).
By
the symmetry interchanging
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
, it suffices to consider one of the two cartesian squares, say the bottom-left one. We would like to consider the cartesian product
\widetilde{}\mathcal{Z}_{1}
as our cycle and the product
\mathcal{C}^{[r_{1}]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r_{2}]}
as the ambient space. The regularity of the product space happens if and only if (at least) one of the two factors is smooth over
\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
. We distinguish two cases.

The case when
\mathcal{C}^{[r_{1}]}
is smooth
. Then either
\mathcal{C}^{[r_{1}]}=\mathcal{Z}(u_{0})^{[r_{1}]}\simeq\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}
with
v(u_{0})=0
and
r_{1}=0
, or
\mathcal{C}^{[r_{1}]}=\mathcal{Y}(u_{0})^{[r_{1}]}\simeq\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}
with
v(u_{0})=-1
and
r_{1}=1
. Summarizing these two possibilities and renaming
r_{2}
as
r
and recalling the parity
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(r)=r_{1}
, the lower left diagram in (
3.10.1
) becomes the cartesian diagram (
3.10.1
) defining the space
{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}
,

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 11.25697pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.25581pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.25581pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.64586pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 22.46503pt\raise-16.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.60417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\square}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 36.64586pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r,\varepsilon]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.18005pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-11.25697pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 11.25697pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.25697pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.25697pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[\varepsilon]}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

The case when
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r_{2}]}
is smooth
. Then
r_{2}=0
or
r_{2}=n+1
. There is a duality isomorphism
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r]}\simeq\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[n+1-r]}

[
30
, §5.1]
which interchanges the two exceptional special divisors in (
1.0.4
), cf.
[
30
, Prop. 5.7]
. To avoid repetitions, we thus assume
r_{2}=0
. Then
r_{1}
is even. We let
r=r_{1}
in the case
\mathcal{C}^{[r_{1}]}=\mathcal{Z}(u_{0})^{[r_{1}]}\simeq\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r_{1}]}
(and
v(u_{0})=0
), resp. let
r=r_{1}-1
in the case
\mathcal{C}^{[r_{1}]}=\mathcal{Y}(u_{0})^{[r_{1}]}\simeq\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r_{1}-1]}
(and
v(u_{0})=-1
), so that we always have
r+\varepsilon=r_{1}
. Then the lower left diagram in (
3.10.1
) becomes the cartesian diagram (
3.5.4
) defining the space
\widetilde{}\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r]}
,

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 11.22803pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.25581pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.25581pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.22803pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.14479pt\raise-16.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.60417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\square}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 35.22803pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{[r+\varepsilon,0]}_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.5396pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-11.22803pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 11.22804pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.96414pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 36.96414pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{[r+\varepsilon]}_{n+1}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Therefore the list of our cases of AT conjectures is exhaustive, if we only consider pull-backs of KR divisors, further subject to the natural conditions 1) and 2) above.
Moreover, from the above interpretation using the pull-backs of
\mathcal{Z}
- or
\mathcal{Y}
-divisors, our cycles
{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}
and
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
may be viewed as special cases of (yet to be defined) KR divisors on a RZ space
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r_{1},r_{2}]}
of non-maximal parahoric level. Finally, we observe that the image of the composition
{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r,\varepsilon]}\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r]}
is a KR divisor which admits a decomposition as a sum of two (Cartier) divisors. This decomposition leads naturally to closed formal subschemes
\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[r],\pm}
and to refinements of AT conjectures, explaining all cases in the table.

Note that there are more AT conjectures if we allow ourselves to take a certain class of resolutions of singularities, for example those formulated (and proved!) by Z. Zhang
[
30
]
.

Let us outline the layout of the paper. The paper consists of two parts. In the first part, we discuss the quasi-canonical arithmetic fundamental lemma, the graph version of the AT conjecture of type
(r,0)
and the AT conjectures, and the evidence for them. In the second part, we discuss the geometry of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
and the structure of the special divisor
\mathcal{Z}(u)
for a vector
u
of length
\varpi
. The two parts are independent of each other (of course, there are results in the case
r=1
in the first part which apply only due to the results in the second part).

In more detail, the layout of part 1 of the paper is as follows. In §
3
, the relevant RZ spaces are introduced and the intersection numbers appearing on the geometric side are defined. In §
4
, the analytic side is detailed (transfer, matching, etc.). In §
5
we recall the FL and the AFL. In §
6
, we construct functions which have the correct transfer for the graph version of the arithmetic transfer conjectures. Using these functions, we formulate and prove in §
7
the quasi-canonical FL and AFL. In §
8
, we deduce the graph version of the AT conjecture. The sections
9
–
13
are devoted to the AT conjecture. In §
9
we construct a function with the correct transfer and formulate the AT conjecture when
r
is an even integer. In §
11
we do the same for the case when
r
is odd and equals
n
. In §
13
, we formulate an AT conjecture in a vague form, for arbitrary
r
.

Part 2 of the paper starts with §
14
, in which the space
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[1]}
is introduced, and in which our results concerning its geometric structure are formulated. The last two sections are devoted to the proofs of these results.

We thank SLMath for its hospitality to all three of us during the Spring 2023 semester on “Algebraic cycles, L-values, and Euler systems” when part of this work was done. We thank the referee for his/her comments. WZ thanks the department of Mathematics at Harvard University for their hospitality where part of the paper was written during his visit in Spring 2024. CL was supported by the NSF grant DMS #2101157. MR was supported by the Simons foundation. WZ was supported by the NSF grant DMS #1901642 and the Simons Foundation.

2.
Notations

Let
p>2
be a prime. Let
F_{0}
be a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, with ring of integers
O_{F_{0}}
, residue field
k=\mathbb{F}_{q}
of size
q
, and uniformizer
\varpi
. Let
F
be the unramified quadratic extension of
F_{0}
, with ring of integers
O_{F}
and residue field
k_{F}
. Let
{\mathrm{val}}:F\mathbb{Z}\cup\{\infty\}
be the valuation on
F
. Let
|\cdot|_{F}:F\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
(resp.
|\cdot|:F_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
) be the normalized absolute value on
F
(resp.
F_{0}
). Let
\eta=\eta_{F/F_{0}}:F_{0}^{\times}\rightarrow\{\pm 1\}
be the quadratic character associated to
F/F_{0}
. Let
\sigma
be the nontrivial Galois automorphism of
F/F_{0}
. Let
{\breve{F}}
be the completion of the maximal unramified extension of
F
, and
{O_{\breve{F}}}
its ring of integers, and
\bar{k}
its residue field. Fix
\delta\in O_{F}^{\times}
such that
\sigma(\delta)=-\delta
.

Let
W
be a (non-degenerate)
F/F_{0}
-hermitian space with hermitian form
(\ ,\ )
. We write
{\mathrm{val}}(x):={\mathrm{val}}((x,x))
for any
x\in W
. Recall that a (non-degenerate)
F/F_{0}
-hermitian space is determined up to isomorphism by its dimension
n
and its discriminant
{\mathrm{disc}}(W)=(-1)^{{n\choose 2}}\det(W)\in F_{0}^{\times}/\mathrm{Nm}_{F/F_{0}}F^{\times}
(
[
8
, Theorem 3.1]
). We say
W
is
split
if
{\mathrm{disc}}(W)=1\in F_{0}^{\times}/\mathrm{Nm}_{F/F_{0}}F^{\times}
, and
nonsplit
otherwise.

Let
L\subseteq W
be an
O_{F}
-lattice of rank
n
. We denote by
L^{\vee}
its dual lattice under
(\ ,\ )
. We say that
L
is
integral
if
L\subseteq L^{\vee}
. If
L
is integral, define its
fundamental invariants
to be the unique sequence of integers
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
such that
0\leq a_{1}\leq\cdots\leq a_{n}
, and
L^{\vee}/L\simeq\oplus_{i=1}^{n}O_{F}/\varpi^{a_{i}}
as
O_{F}
-modules; define its
valuation
to be
{\mathrm{val}}(L)\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}
; and define its
type
, denoted by
t(L)
, to be the number of nonzero terms in its fundamental invariants
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
.

We say
L
is
minuscule
or a
vertex lattice
if it is integral and
L^{\vee}\subseteq\varpi^{-1}L
. Note that
L
is a vertex lattice of type
t
if and only if it has fundamental invariants
(0^{(n-t)},1^{(t)})
, if and only if
L\subseteq^{t}L^{\vee}\subseteq\varpi^{-1}L
, where
\subseteq^{t}
indicates that the
O_{F}
-colength is equal to
t
. The set of vertex lattices of type
t
(resp. of type
\geq t
, resp. all vertex lattices) in
W
is denoted by
{\mathrm{Vert}}^{t}(W)
(resp.
{\mathrm{Vert}}^{\geq t}(W)
, resp.
{\mathrm{Vert}}(W)
). We say
L
is
self-dual
if
L=L^{\vee}
, or equivalently
L
is a vertex lattice of type 0. We say
L
is
almost self-dual
if
L
is a vertex lattice of type 1. Since
F/F_{0}
is unramified, if
W
is split then
{\mathrm{val}}(L)
is even, any vertex lattice has even type and
W
contains a self-dual lattice; if
W
is nonsplit then
{\mathrm{val}}(L)
is odd, any vertex lattice has odd type and
W
contains an almost self-dual lattice.

Let
X
be a formal scheme. For closed formal subschemes
\mathcal{Z}_{1},\cdots,\mathcal{Z}_{m}
of
X
, denote by
\cup_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{Z}_{i}
the formal scheme-theoretic union, i.e., the closed formal subscheme with ideal sheaf
\cap_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{Z}_{i}}
, where
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{Z}_{i}}
is the ideal sheaf of
\mathcal{Z}_{i}
. A closed formal subscheme on
X
is called a Cartier divisor if it is defined by an
invertible
ideal sheaf.

When
X
is noetherian and
Y
is a closed formal subscheme, denote by
K_{0}^{Y}(X)
the Grothendieck group (modulo quasi-isomorphisms) of finite complexes of coherent locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules, acyclic outside
Y
(i.e., the homology sheaves are formally supported on
Y
). As defined in
[
29
, (B.1), (B.2)]
, denote by
\mathrm{F}^{i}K_{0}^{Y}(X)
the (descending) codimension filtration on
K_{0}^{Y}(X)
,
and denote by
\Gr^{i}K_{0}^{Y}(X)
its
i
-th graded piece. As in
[
29
, App. B]
, the definition of
K_{0}^{Y}(X)
,
\mathrm{F}^{i}K_{0}^{Y}(X)
and
\Gr^{i}K_{0}^{Y}(X)
can be extended to locally noetherian formal schemes
X
by writing
X
as an increasing union of open noetherian formal subschemes. Similarly, we let
K_{0}^{\prime}(X)
denote the Grothendieck group of coherent sheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules. Now let
X
be regular. Then there is a natural isomorphism
K_{0}^{Y}(X)\simeq K_{0}^{\prime}(Y)
. For closed formal subschemes
\mathcal{Z}_{1},\cdots,\mathcal{Z}_{m}
of
X
, denote by
\mathcal{Z}_{1}\cap^{\mathbb{L}}_{X}\cdots\cap^{\mathbb{L}}_{X}\mathcal{Z}_{m}
(or simply
\mathcal{Z}_{1}\cap^{\mathbb{L}}\cdots\cap^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{Z}_{m}
if the ambient space is clear) the derived tensor product
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\cdots\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Z}_{m}}
, viewed as an element in
K_{0}^{\mathcal{Z}_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}}(X)
.

For
\mathcal{F}
a finite complex of coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules, we define its Euler–Poincaré characteristic

\chi(X,\mathcal{F}):=\sum_{i,j}(-1)^{i+j}\length_{{O_{\breve{F}}}}H^{i}(X,H_{j}(\mathcal{F}))

if the lengths are all finite. Assume that
X
is regular with pure dimension
n
. If
\mathcal{F}_{i}\in\mathrm{F}^{r_{i}}K_{0}^{\mathcal{Z}_{i}}(X)
with
\sum_{i}r_{i}\geq n
, then by
[
29
, (B.3)]
we know that
\chi(X,\bigotimes_{i}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{F}_{i})
depends only on the image of
\mathcal{F}_{i}
in
\Gr^{r_{i}}K_{0}^{\mathcal{Z}_{i}}(X)
.

For two formal schemes
X,Y
over
\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
, write
X\times Y:=X\times_{\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}}Y
for short.

For an algebraic variety
Y
over a finite extension
F
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we write
C_{c}^{\infty}(Y)
for
C_{c}^{\infty}(Y(F))
.

Part I
AT conjectures and the quasi-canonical AFL

3.
The geometric side

In this section, we introduce the relevant RZ spaces and then define the intersection numbers appearing on the geometric side of the arithmetic transfer conjectures.

3.1.
Rapoport–Zink spaces
\mathcal{N}_{n}
of self-dual level

Let
S
be a
\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
-scheme. Consider a triple
(X,\iota,\lambda)
where

(i)

X
is a formal
\varpi
-divisible
O_{F_{0}}
-module over
S
of relative height
2n
and dimension
n
,

(ii)

\iota:O_{F}\rightarrow\End(X)
is an action of
O_{F}
extending the
O_{F_{0}}
-action and satisfying the Kottwitz condition of signature
(1,n-1)
: for all
a\in O_{F}
, the characteristic polynomial of
\iota(a)
on
\Lie X
is equal to
(T-a)(T-\sigma(a))^{n-1}\in\mathcal{O}_{S}[T]
,

(iii)

\lambda:X\rightarrow X^{\vee}
is a principal polarization on
X
whose Rosati involution induces the automorphism
\sigma
on
O_{F}
via
\iota
.

Up to
O_{F}
-linear quasi-isogeny compatible with polarizations, there is a unique such triple
(\mathbb{X},\iota_{\mathbb{X}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})
over
S=\Spec\bar{k}
, where
\mathbb{X}
is isoclinic. Let
\mathcal{N}_{n}=\mathcal{N}_{F/F_{0},n}
be the (relative)
unitary Rapoport–Zink space of self-dual level
, which is a formal scheme over
\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
representing the functor sending each
S
to the set of isomorphism classes of tuples
(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)
, where the
framing

\rho:X\times_{S}\bar{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{X}\times_{\Spec\bar{k}}\bar{S}
is an
O_{F}
-linear quasi-isogeny of height 0 such that
\rho^{*}((\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})_{\bar{S}})=\lambda_{\bar{S}}
. Here
\bar{S}\coloneqq S_{\bar{k}}
is the special fiber.

The Rapoport–Zink space
\mathcal{N}_{n}
is formally locally of finite type and formally smooth of relative dimension
n-1
over
\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
(
[
22
]
,
[
16
, Prop. 1.3]
).

3.2.
The hermitian space
\mathbb{V}_{n}

Let
\mathbb{E}
be the formal
O_{F_{0}}
-module of relative height 2 and dimension 1 over
\Spec\bar{k}
. Then
D\coloneqq\End_{O_{F_{0}}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{E}):=\End_{O_{F_{0}}}(\mathbb{E})\otimes\mathbb{Q}
is the quaternion division algebra over
F_{0}
. We fix an
F_{0}
-embedding
\iota_{\mathbb{E}}:F\rightarrow D
, which makes
\mathbb{E}
into a formal
O_{F}
-module of relative height 1. We fix an
O_{F_{0}}
-linear principal polarization
\lambda_{\mathbb{E}}:\mathbb{E}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{E}^{\vee}
. Then
(\mathbb{E},\iota_{\mathbb{E}},\lambda_{\mathbb{E}})
is a hermitian
O_{F}
-module of signature
(1,0)
. We have
\mathcal{N}_{1}\simeq\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
and there is a unique lifting (
the canonical lifting
)
\mathcal{E}
of the formal
O_{F}
-module
\mathbb{E}
over
\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
, equipped with its
O_{F}
-action
\iota_{\mathcal{E}}
, its framing
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}:\mathcal{E}_{\bar{k}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{E}
, and its principal polarization
\lambda_{\mathcal{E}}
lifting
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}^{*}(\lambda_{\mathbb{E}})
. Define
{\overline{\mathbb{E}}}
to be the same
O_{F_{0}}
-module as
\mathbb{E}
but with
O_{F}
-action given by
\iota_{{\overline{\mathbb{E}}}}\coloneqq\iota_{\mathbb{E}}\circ\sigma
, and
\lambda_{{\overline{\mathbb{E}}}}\coloneqq\lambda_{\mathbb{E}}
, and similarly define
\bar{\mathcal{E}}
and
\lambda_{\bar{\mathcal{E}}}
.

Denote by
\mathbb{V}_{n}=\Hom_{O_{F}}^{\circ}({\overline{\mathbb{E}}},\mathbb{X})
be the space of special quasi-homomorphisms. Then
\mathbb{V}_{n}
carries a
F/F_{0}
-hermitian form: for
x,y\in\mathbb{V}_{n}
, the pairing
(x,y)\in F
is given by

({\overline{\mathbb{E}}}\xrightarrow{x}\mathbb{X}\xrightarrow{\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}}{\mathbb{X}^{\vee}}\xrightarrow{y^{\vee}}{\overline{\mathbb{E}}}^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\lambda_{\mathbb{E}}^{-1}}{\overline{\mathbb{E}}})\in\End_{O_{F}}^{\circ}({\overline{\mathbb{E}}})=\iota_{\overline{\mathbb{E}}}(F)\simeq F.

The hermitian space
\mathbb{V}_{n}
is the unique (up to isomorphism) non-degenerate non-split
F/F_{0}
-hermitian space of dimension
n
. The unitary group
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V}_{n})(F_{0})
acts on the framing hermitian
O_{F}
-module
(\mathbb{X},\iota_{\mathbb{X}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})
(via the identification in
[
10
, Lem. 3.9]
) and hence acts on the Rapoport–Zink space
\mathcal{N}_{n}
via
g(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)=(X,\iota,\lambda,g\circ\rho)
for
g\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})
.

For any
0\neq x\subseteq\mathbb{V}_{n}
, define the
Kudla–Rapoport divisor
or
special divisor

\mathcal{Z}(x)\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{n}
to be the closed formal subscheme which represents the functor sending each
S
to the set of isomorphism classes of tuples
(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)
such that the quasi-homomorphism

\rho^{-1}\circ x\circ\rho_{\bar{\mathcal{E}}}:\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{S}\times_{S}\bar{S}\xrightarrow{\rho_{\bar{\mathcal{E}}}}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\times_{\Spec\bar{k}}\bar{S}\xrightarrow{x}\mathbb{X}\times_{\Spec\bar{k}}\bar{S}\xrightarrow{\rho^{-1}}X\times_{S}\bar{S}

extends to a homomorphism
\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{S}\rightarrow X
(
[
10
, Def. 3.2]
). Then
\mathcal{Z}(x)
only depends on the
O_{F}
-lattice
\langle x\rangle
spanned by
x
, and it is a Cartier divisor on
\mathcal{N}_{n}
and is flat over
\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
(
[
10
, Prop. 3.5]
). In the body of the paper, the index
n
of the RZ-space is in fact often replaced by
n+1
.

3.3.
Rapoport–Zink spaces
\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}
of maximal parahoric level

Let
r
be an integer such that
0\leq r\leq n
. Let
S
be a
\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
-scheme. Consider a triple
(Y,\iota,\lambda)
analogous to §
3.1
but the principal polarization
\lambda
is replaced by a polarization
\lambda:Y\rightarrow Y^{\vee}
such that
\ker\lambda\subseteq Y[\varpi]
and has order
q^{2r}
. Up to
O_{F}
-linear quasi-isogeny compatible with polarizations, there is a unique such triple
(\mathbb{Y},\iota_{\mathbb{Y}},\lambda_{\mathbb{Y}})
over
S=\Spec\bar{k}
such that
\mathbb{Y}
is isoclinic. Let
\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}
be the (relative)
unitary Rapoport–Zink space of maximal parahoric level
, which is a formal scheme over
\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
representing the functor sending each
S
to the set of isomorphism classes of tuples
(Y,\iota,\lambda,\rho)
, where the
framing

\rho:Y\times_{S}\bar{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{Y}\times_{\Spec\bar{k}}\bar{S}
is an
O_{F}
-linear quasi-isogeny of height 0 such that
\rho^{*}((\lambda_{\mathbb{Y}})_{\bar{S}})=\lambda_{\bar{S}}
.

The Rapoport–Zink space
\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}
is formally locally of finite type, regular, of relative dimension
n-1
and of semistable reduction over
\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
(
[
22
]
,
[
4
]
,
[
2
, Thm. 1.2]
). By definition
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\simeq\mathcal{N}_{n}
for
r=0
.

Analogous to §
3.2
, denote by
\mathbb{W}_{n}=\mathbb{W}_{n}^{[r]}=\Hom_{O_{F}}^{\circ}({\overline{\mathbb{E}}},\mathbb{Y})
the space of special quasi-homomorphisms. Then
\mathbb{W}_{n}
carries a
F/F_{0}
-hermitian form: for
x,y\in\mathbb{W}_{n}
, the pairing
(x,y)\in F
is given by

({\overline{\mathbb{E}}}\xrightarrow{x}\mathbb{Y}\xrightarrow{\lambda_{\mathbb{Y}}}{\mathbb{Y}^{\vee}}\xrightarrow{y^{\vee}}{\overline{\mathbb{E}}}^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\lambda_{\mathbb{E}}^{-1}}{\overline{\mathbb{E}}})\in\End_{O_{F}}^{\circ}({\overline{\mathbb{E}}})=\iota_{\overline{\mathbb{E}}}(F)\simeq F.

The hermitian space
\mathbb{W}_{n}^{[r]}
is the unique (up to isomorphism) non-degenerate split (resp. nonsplit)
F/F_{0}
-hermitian space of dimension
n
if
r
is odd (resp.
r
is even). Analogous to §
3.2
, the unitary group
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{W}_{n})(F_{0})
acts on the framing hermitian
O_{F}
-module
(\mathbb{Y},\iota_{\mathbb{Y}},\lambda_{\mathbb{Y}})
and hence acts on the Rapoport–Zink space
\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}
via
g(Y,\iota,\lambda,\rho)=(Y,\iota,\lambda,g\circ\rho)
for
g\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{W})(F_{0})
. Note that
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}=\mathcal{N}_{n}
and
\mathbb{W}_{n}^{[0]}=\mathbb{V}_{n}
.

3.4.
Rapoport–Zink spaces
\mathcal{N}^{[r,s]}_{n}
of parahoric level

Let
0\leq s\leq r\leq n
be integers of the same parity. Let
\mathbb{Y}^{[r]}
(resp.
\mathbb{Y}^{[s]}
) be the framing
p
-divisible groups of
\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}
(resp.
\mathcal{N}^{[s]}_{n}
). Fix an
O_{F}
-linear isogeny
\alpha:\mathbb{Y}^{[r]}\rightarrow\mathbb{Y}^{[s]}
compatible with polarizations such that
\ker\alpha\subseteq\mathbb{Y}^{[r]}[\varpi]
and has degree
q^{r-s}
. The existence of
\alpha
follows from Dieudonné theory.

Consider the functor sending a
\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
-scheme
S
to the set of isomorphism classes of tuples
(Y^{[r]},\iota^{[r]},\lambda^{[r]},\rho^{[r]},Y^{[s]},\iota^{[s]},\lambda^{[s]},\rho^{[s]})
, where

•

(Y^{[r]},\iota^{[r]},\lambda^{[r]},\rho^{[r]})\in\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}(S)
,

•

(Y^{[s]},\iota^{[s]},\lambda^{[s]},\rho^{[s]})\in\mathcal{N}^{[s]}_{n}(S)
,

such that
(\rho^{[s]})^{-1}\circ\alpha\circ\rho^{[r]}:Y^{[r]}\times_{S}\bar{S}\rightarrow Y^{[s]}\times_{S}\bar{S}
lifts to an isogeny
\widetilde{\alpha}:Y^{[r]}\rightarrow Y^{[s]}
. Note that if
\widetilde{\alpha}
exists then it is unique and
\ker\widetilde{\alpha}\subseteq Y^{[r]}[\varpi]
and has degree
q^{r-s}
. This functor is represented by a formal scheme
\mathcal{N}^{[r,s]}_{n}
known as the (relative)
unitary Rapoport–Zink space of parahoric level
. The Rapoport–Zink space
\mathcal{N}^{[r,s]}_{n}
is formally locally of finite type, regular, of relative dimension
n-1
and of semistable reduction over
\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
(
[
22
]
,
[
4
]
). By definition there are natural projections

(3.4.1)

\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{[r,s]}_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{[s]}_{n}.}

3.5.
The space
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}

Let
0\leq r\leq n
. Set

(3.5.1)

\varepsilon=\begin{cases}0,&r\text{ is even},\\
1,&r\text{ is odd}.\end{cases}

Let
\mathbb{Y}
(resp.
\mathbb{X}
) be the framing
p
-divisible group of
\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}
(resp.
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
). Then we may choose
(\mathbb{Y}\times{\overline{\mathbb{E}}},\iota_{\mathbb{Y}}\times\iota_{\overline{\mathbb{E}}},\lambda_{\mathbb{Y}}\times\varpi^{\varepsilon}\lambda_{{\overline{\mathbb{E}}}})
(resp.
(\mathbb{X},\iota_{\mathbb{X}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})
) as a framing object of
\mathcal{N}^{[r+\varepsilon]}_{n+1}
(resp.
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
). We have a natural closed immersion

(3.5.2)

\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{N}^{[r+\varepsilon]}_{n+1},\quad(Y,\iota,\lambda,\rho)(Y\times\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{S},\iota\times\iota_{\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{S}},\lambda\times\varpi^{\varepsilon}\lambda_{\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{S}},\rho\times\rho_{\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}).

Consider
\mathcal{N}^{[r+\varepsilon,0]}_{n+1}
by fixing an
O_{F}
-linear isogeny

(3.5.3)

\alpha\colon\mathbb{Y}\times{\overline{\mathbb{E}}}\mathbb{X},

such that
\ker\alpha\subset(\mathbb{Y}\times{\overline{\mathbb{E}}})[\varpi]
and
\alpha^{*}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})=\lambda_{\mathbb{Y}}\times\varpi^{\varepsilon}\lambda_{{\overline{\mathbb{E}}}}
. We define the formal scheme
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
by the cartesian diagram

(3.5.4)

By construction,
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
is the closed formal subscheme of
\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
parameterizing tuples

(Y,\iota_{Y},\lambda_{Y},\rho_{Y},X,\iota_{X},\lambda_{X},\rho_{X})

such that
\rho_{X}^{-1}\circ\alpha\circ(\rho_{Y}\times\rho_{\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{S}})
lifts to an isogeny
\widetilde{\alpha}\colon Y\times\overline{\mathcal{E}}X
. Then
\widetilde{\alpha}
is uniquely determined, has degree
q^{r+\varepsilon}
and
\ker\widetilde{\alpha}\subset(Y\times\overline{\mathcal{E}})[\varpi]
. We denote by
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}
the two natural projections,

(3.5.5)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 84.64845pt\raise-26.0456pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 42.48384pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 79.73965pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{n+1}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Here
\pi_{1}
, resp.
\pi_{2}
, maps the tuple
(Y,\iota_{Y},\lambda_{Y},\rho_{Y},X,\iota_{X},\lambda_{X},\rho_{X})
to the first half, resp. the second half, of the tuple. Both projection maps
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}
are proper.

The structure of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
is quite mysterious in general. The following conjecture concerns the local structure.

Conjecture 3.5.1
.

The formal scheme
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
is regular, with special fiber a
tame divisor with normal crossings
, i.e. for every
x\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}(\bar{k})
, there exists a regular system of parameters
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
for the complete local ring
\hat{}\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},x}
such that
\varpi=\prod X_{1}^{m_{1}}\cdot\ldots\cdot X_{n}^{m_{n}}
, where the
m_{i}
are prime to
p
.

Proposition 3.5.2
.

Conjecture
3.5.1
holds when
r
is even.

Proof.

In this case
\varepsilon=0
and we have
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\simeq\mathcal{N}^{[r,0]}_{n}
is regular and has semistable reduction (using the local model diagram which connects the question to the analogous problem for the local model for
(\mathrm{GL}_{n},\varpi_{1}^{\vee})
, solved in
[
4
]
).
∎

Conjecture
3.5.1
seems more difficult when
r
is odd. We will study the special case
r=1
in more detail and prove in Theorem
14.6.2
a more precise result regarding the structure of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
. This was conjectured by Kudla and Rapoport, cf.
[
11
]
, see also
[
15
, Conj. 10.4.1]
. In particular, when
r=1
we will prove Conjecture
3.5.1
in Theorem
16.6.1
.

3.6.
Arithmetic intersection number for AT of type
(r,0)

The morphisms
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
in (
3.5.5
) combine to give a closed embedding,

{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}.

Since
\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}
is regular and
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
is formally smooth over
\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
, we know that
\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
is regular. Hence it makes sense to define arithmetic intersection numbers of closed formal subschemes on the ambient space
\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
,

(3.6.1)

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}:=\chi(\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1},{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\cap^{\mathbb{L}}g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}),

where
g\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{W}_{n}^{[r]})(F_{0})\times\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V}_{n+1})(F_{0})
. The arithmetic intersection number is finite as long as
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\cap g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
is a proper scheme over
\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
, which is the case if
g
is regular semisimple by the standard argument, cf.
[
16
, proof of Lem. 6.1]
. We will formulate arithmetic transfer conjectures (Conjectures
9.2.1
,
11.2.1
and
13.1.1
) relating (
3.6.1
) to the derivative of certain orbital integrals.

3.7.
Arithmetic intersection number for the graph variant of AT of type
(r,0)

We also consider a variant of the arithmetic intersection number (
3.6.1
) by replacing the factor
\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}
in the regular ambient space
\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
by the related space
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
. We have the natural closed immersion

{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\hookrightarrow{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1},

given by the graph of
\pi_{2}
.

Let
g\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{W}_{n}^{[r]})(F_{0})\times\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V}_{n+1})(F_{0})
. The variant of (
3.6.1
) we consider is

(3.7.1)

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}:=\chi({\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1},{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\cap^{\mathbb{L}}g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}).

Note that the diagonal action of the group
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{W}_{n}^{[r]})(F_{0})
on
\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
stabilizes
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
and hence
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{W}_{n}^{[r]})(F_{0})
acts on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
. Thus the group
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{W}_{n}^{[r]})(F_{0})\times\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V}_{n+1})(F_{0})
naturally acts on the product
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
.

Assuming that
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
is regular (comp. Conjecture
3.5.1
), and since
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
is formally smooth over
\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
, the ambient space
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
is regular. Hence under this assumption the arithmetic intersection number above makes sense, and is finite when the formal scheme-theoretic intersection
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\cap g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
is a proper scheme over
\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
. We will give a formula, relating (
3.7.1
) to the derivative of orbital integrals, cf. Theorem
8.1.1
.

3.8.
Relation of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
with the special divisor
\mathcal{Z}(u)

Let
{u}\in\mathbb{V}_{n+1}=\Hom^{\circ}_{O_{F}}({\overline{\mathbb{E}}},\mathbb{X})
be the restriction of
\alpha
in (
3.5.3
) to the second factor
{\overline{\mathbb{E}}}
. Let
\mathcal{Z}(u)\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
be the special divisor associated to
{u}
. By construction, the projection
\pi_{2}:{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
factors through
\mathcal{Z}(u)
. Thus we have a (not necessarily cartesian) commutative diagram

(3.8.1)

) and (

) we obtain a commutative diagram,

3.9.
Arithmetic intersection number for the quasi-canonical AFL

Consider the natural closed immersion

\mathcal{Z}(u)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}

given by the graph of the inclusion of the special divisor
\mathcal{Z}(u)
in
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
Note that
(u,u)=\varpi^{\varepsilon}
and hence
\mathcal{Z}(u)
is the quasi-canonical divisor of level zero (when
\varepsilon=0
), resp. one (when
\varepsilon=1
). Since
({u},{u})
has valuation
\varepsilon\in\{0,1\}
, we know by
[
23
]
that
\mathcal{Z}(u)
is regular (note that
\mathcal{Z}(u)
is its own difference divisor). Since
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
is formally smooth over
\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
, the ambient space
\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
is regular. Let
\mathbb{W}_{n}
be the orthogonal complement of
u\in\mathbb{V}_{n+1}
. Then the action of the group
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{W}_{n})(F_{0})\subset\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V}_{n+1})(F_{0})
on
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
leaves
\mathcal{Z}(u)
invariant. Hence
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{W}_{n})(F_{0})\times\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V}_{n+1})(F_{0})
acts on the product
\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
. Therefore we may consider the intersection number of the quasi-canonical divisor
\mathcal{Z}(u)
with its translate under
g\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{W}_{n})(F_{0})\times\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V}_{n+1})(F_{0})
,

(3.9.1)

\left\langle\mathcal{Z}(u),g\mathcal{Z}(u)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}:=\chi(\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1},\mathcal{Z}(u)\cap^{\mathbb{L}}g\mathcal{Z}(u)).

When
\varepsilon=0
, (
3.9.1
) is, under the identification of
\mathcal{Z}(u)
with
\mathcal{N}_{n}
, the intersection number occurring in the AFL. When
\varepsilon=1
, we consider (
3.9.1
) as the intersection number occurring in the
quasi-canonical
AFL. This number is finite when the formal scheme-theoretic intersection
\mathcal{Z}(u)\cap g\mathcal{Z}(u)
is a proper scheme over
\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
. We will give a formula, relating (
3.9.1
) to the derivative of orbital integrals, cf. Theorem
7.2.1
. When
\varepsilon=0
, this formula reduces to the AFL.

3.10.
The space
{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}

Let
0\leq r\leq n+1
. Let
\varepsilon\in\{0,1\}
be of the same parity as
r
so that the space
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r,\varepsilon]}
is defined.
We have a natural embedding (cf. (
3.5.2
))

\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[\varepsilon]},\quad(Y,\iota,\lambda,\rho)(Y\times\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{S},\iota\times\iota_{\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{S}},\lambda\times\varpi^{\varepsilon}\lambda_{\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{S}},\rho\times\rho_{\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{S}}).

Using this embedding, similar to (
3.5.4
) but using the other projection
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r,\varepsilon]}\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[\varepsilon]}
(which maps the tuple
(Y^{[r]},\iota^{[r]},\lambda^{[r]},\rho^{[r]},Y^{[\varepsilon]},\iota^{[\varepsilon]},\lambda^{[\varepsilon]},\rho^{[\varepsilon]})
to the second half of the tuple), we form another Cartesian product, denoted by
{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}
,

(3.10.1)

Now, instead of (
3.5.5
) we can consider

(3.10.2)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern
