3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.5342pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.5342pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 38.5342pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{n+1}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

By the projection formula for the morphism
\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{n+1}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
, we can relate the intersection numbers,

\left\langle\mathcal{Z}(u),(1\times g)\mathcal{Z}(u)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}=\left\langle\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n+1}},(1\times g)\mathcal{Z}(u)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n+1}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}.

By the semi-Lie algebra version of AFL, Theorem
5.2.1
part
c
, we have

\left\langle\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n+1}},(1\times g)\mathcal{Z}(u)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n+1}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}\log q=-\operatorname{\partial Orb}((\gamma,w_{0}),{\bf 1}_{K^{\prime}\times\Lambda^{\prime}}).

Now we apply Lemma
6.2.5
and take the first derivative. By Leibniz’s rule, we have

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\Big|_{s=0}\Orb(\gamma,\phi^{\prime}_{s},s)=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\Big|_{s=0}\Orb(\gamma,\phi^{\prime}_{s=0},s)+(-1)^{n+1}\Orb(\gamma,{\bf 1}_{S_{n+1}(O_{F_{0}})})\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\Big|_{s=0}q^{-(n+1)s}.

Recall that, when we make the bijection of the orbits in (
4.1.3
), we need to rescale the Hermitian form, cf. Remark
4.1.1
. Here, after we scale the Hermitian space
W_{0}
by a factor
\varpi
, the new Hermitian space has Hasse invariant
(-1)^{n+1}
. Now we distinguish two cases according to the parity of
n
. If
n
is odd, then for any
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, we have

\Orb(\gamma,{\bf 1}_{S_{n+1}(O_{F_{0}})})=0
(by the “easy” part of the Jacquet–Rallis fundamental lemma), hence the second summand vanishes. If
n
is even, then the second summand does not identically vanish. We have

(-1)^{n+1}\Orb(\gamma,{\bf 1}_{S_{n+1}(O_{F_{0}})})\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\Big|_{s=0}q^{-(n+1)s}=(n+1)\log q\Orb(\gamma,{\bf 1}_{S_{n+1}(O_{F_{0}})}).

This proves the inhomogeneous version.

The homogeneous version follows. Indeed, we obtain by Lemma
6.2.2
that

\varphi^{\prime\natural}-\phi^{\prime}=(q^{2(n+1)}-1)((-1)^{n}{\bf u^{\prime}}\ast{\bf 1}_{S_{n+1}(O_{F_{0}})}-\,{\bf u}\ast{\bf 1}_{S_{n+1}(O_{F_{0}})})

and
(\varphi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{corr}})^{\natural}=\phi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{corr}}
.
Note that by (
6.2.7
) we have for
\gamma
matching
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
,

(7.2.1)

\displaystyle\operatorname{\partial Orb}(\gamma,{\bf u^{\prime}}\ast{\bf 1}_{S_{n+1}(O_{F_{0}})})=(-1)^{n}\operatorname{\partial Orb}(\gamma,{\bf u}\ast{\bf 1}_{S_{n+1}(O_{F_{0}})}).

Therefore, we get from Corollary
4.2.4

\Orb(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{corr}})=\Orb(\mathfrak{r}(\gamma),\phi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{corr}}),\quad\operatorname{\partial Orb}(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime})=2\operatorname{\partial Orb}(\mathfrak{r}(\gamma),\phi^{\prime}),\quad\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}.

On the other hand, we have, for
(g_{1},g_{2})\in(\mathrm{U}(W_{1}^{\flat})\times\mathrm{U}(W_{1}))(F_{0})
,

\left\langle\mathcal{Z}(u),(g_{1},g_{2})\mathcal{Z}(u)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}=\left\langle\mathcal{Z}(u),(1,g_{1}^{-1}g_{2})\mathcal{Z}(u)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}.

The result follows because, if
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
matches
(g_{1},g_{2})\in(\mathrm{U}(W_{1}^{\flat})\times\mathrm{U}(W_{1}))(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then
\mathfrak{r}(\gamma)\in S_{n+1}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
matches
g_{1}^{-1}g_{2}\in\mathrm{U}(W_{1})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, comp. §
4.1
.
∎

8.
The graph version of the AT conjecture of type
(r,0)

Let
n\geq 1
and let
0\leq r\leq n
. Assuming that
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
is regular, we may consider the intersection number
\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}
of §
3.7
. Recall that Conjecture
3.5.1
implies the required regularity; it holds if
r=1
(Theorem
14.6.2
) or
r
is even (Proposition
3.5.2
).

The Haar measures on
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(F)
and on
\mathrm{U}(W_{0}^{\flat})(F_{0})
are chosen as in (
6.1.10
) for the given
r
.

8.1.
Reduction to the (quasi-canonical) AFL

Theorem 8.1.1
.

Assume that
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
is regular.

(i)
(Inhomogeneous version)
When
r
is odd, let
\phi_{r}^{\prime}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(S_{n+1})
as in (
6.2.5
). When
r
is even, let
\phi_{r}^{\prime}={\bf 1}_{S_{n+1}(O_{F_{0}})}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(S_{n+1})
. If
\gamma\in S_{n+1}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in\mathrm{U}(W_{1})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},(1,g){\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}\log q=-\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,c_{r}\phi_{r}^{\prime}\big)-\Orb\big(\gamma,c_{r}\phi^{\prime}_{r,\mathrm{corr}}\big)

where

\phi^{\prime}_{r,\mathrm{corr}}=\begin{cases}(n+1)\,{\bf 1}_{S_{n+1}(O_{F_{0}})}\cdot\log q,&$n$\text{ is even, and $r$ is odd},\\
0,&$n$\text{ is odd, or $r$ is even}.\end{cases}

(ii)
(Homogeneous version)

When
r
is odd, let
\varphi^{\prime}_{r}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
as in (
6.2.6
). When
r
is even, let
\varphi^{\prime}_{r}=c_{r}c^{\prime}_{r}\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{K}_{n}^{\prime[r]}\times K_{n+1}^{\prime}}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
(then
\varphi^{\prime}_{r}
is a transfer of
(c_{r}^{2}\cdot{\bf 1}_{\widetilde{K}_{n}^{[r]}\times K_{n+1}},0)
). If
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}_{r}\big)-\Orb\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}_{r,\mathrm{corr}}\big)

where

\varphi^{\prime}_{r,\mathrm{corr}}=\begin{cases}c_{r}\cdot(n+1)\,{\bf 1}_{G^{\prime}(O_{F_{0}})}\cdot\log q,&$n$\text{ is even, and $r$ is odd},\\
0,&$n$\text{ is odd, or $r$ is even}.\end{cases}

Again, as was the case for the quasi-canonical AFL, we obtain the following corollary. Recall that the choice of Haar measures depends on
r
, which is why we indicate
r
in the statement of the corollary.

Corollary 8.1.2
.

Assume the density conjecture
[
18
, Conj. 5.16]
, and
assume that
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
is regular.

(i)
(Inhomogeneous version)
Let
\phi^{\prime}_{r}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(S_{n+1})
be any transfer of
({\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}},0)
. Then there exists
a function
\phi^{\prime}_{r,\mathrm{corr}}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
such that, if
\gamma\in S_{n+1}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in\mathrm{U}(W_{1})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},(1,g){\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}\cdot\log q=-\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,c_{r}\phi^{\prime}_{r}\big)-\Orb\big(\gamma,\phi^{\prime}_{r,\mathrm{corr}}\big).

(ii)
(Homogeneous version)
Let
\varphi^{\prime}_{r}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
be any transfer of
(c_{r}^{2}\cdot{\bf 1}_{\widetilde{K}_{n}^{[r]}\times K_{n+1}},0)
. Then there exists
a function
\varphi^{\prime}_{r,\mathrm{corr}}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
such that, if
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}_{r}\big)-\Orb\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}_{r,\mathrm{corr}}\big).

∎

Proof.

(of Theorem
8.1.1
)
We first note that part (ii) follows from part (i). In fact, we have
\varphi^{\prime\natural}_{r}=c_{r}\phi_{r}^{\prime}
, as follows from Lemma
6.2.2
when
r
is odd, and from the similar identity
(\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{K}_{n}^{\prime[r]}\times K_{n+1}^{\prime}})^{\natural}=\vol(\widetilde{K}_{n}^{\prime[r]}){\bf 1}_{S_{n+1}(O_{F_{0}})}=\frac{1}{c^{\prime}_{r}}{\bf 1}_{S_{n+1}(O_{F_{0}})}
, when
r
is even.

We now show part (i).
Recall that the group
G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})
naturally acts on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
and
\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
, where
\mathcal{Z}(u)
denotes the special divisor for the vector
u
of length
\varpi^{\varepsilon}
. We have a cartesian diagram

(8.1.1)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 12.80675pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.25581pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.25581pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.85765pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-16.34473pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.64328pt\raise-16.34473pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.60417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\square}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 41.85765pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 67.53656pt\raise-16.34473pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.93056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 67.53656pt\raise-24.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-12.80675pt\raise-32.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}(u)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.80675pt\raise-32.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.80675pt\raise-32.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 36.80675pt\raise-32.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

The morphism
\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id}:{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
is
G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})
-equivariant.

In view of the AFL (for
r
even, see Theorem
5.2.1
), resp. the quasi-canonical AFL (for
r
odd, see Theorem
7.2.1
), it suffices to prove the identity

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}=c_{r}\left\langle\mathcal{Z}(u),g\mathcal{Z}(u)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}},\quad g\in{G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})}_{\mathrm{rs}}.

This identity follows from the projection formula for the proper morphism
\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id}:{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
and the following Lemma.
∎

Lemma 8.1.3
.

Let
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})
.

(i)

The identity
(\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}(g\mathcal{Z}(u))=g\widetilde{}\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r]}
holds in
K_{0}^{\prime}(\widetilde{}\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1})
.

(ii)

The identity
(\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id})_{*}(g\widetilde{}\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r]})=c_{r}g\mathcal{Z}(u)
holds in
\Gr^{n}K_{0}^{g\mathcal{Z}(u)}(\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1})
.

Proof.

By the
G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})
-equivariance of
\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id}
, it suffices to consider the case
g=1
.

(i)

Let
\tilde{z}
be a point of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
and let
z=\pi_{2}(\tilde{z})
be the image point in
\mathcal{Z}(u)
. Let
R=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1},z}
and
S=\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1},\tilde{z}}
. Since
\mathcal{Z}(u)
and
\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
are both regular, we know that
\mathcal{Z}(u)
is locally defined by a regular sequence
f_{1},\ldots,f_{n}
in
R
. Thus the
R
-module
O_{\mathcal{Z}(u),z}
has a free resolution given by the Koszul complex
K(f_{1},\ldots,f_{n})
. Let
\tilde{f}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{f}_{n}
be the images of
f_{1},\ldots,f_{n}
under the morphism
R\rightarrow S
induced by
\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id}
. Since the diagram (
8.1.1
) is cartesian, and both
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
and
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
are regular by our assumption on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
so that
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\hookrightarrow{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
is a regular immersion of pure codimension
n
, we know that
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
is locally defined by the regular sequence
\tilde{f}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{f}_{n}
in
S
. Thus the
S
-module
O_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},\tilde{z}}
has a free resolution given by the Koszul complex
K(\tilde{f}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{f}_{n})
. We have an isomorphism of complexes of
S
-modules

K(f_{1},\ldots f_{n})\otimes_{R}S\simeq K(\tilde{f}_{1},\ldots\tilde{f}_{n}),

which gives the desired identity
(\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}(\mathcal{Z}(u))={\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}
at
\tilde{z}
by the definition of
(\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}
.

(ii)

The formal scheme
\mathcal{Z}(u)
is regular of dimension
n
and the morphism
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\mathcal{Z}(u)
is finite in its generic fiber. Let
m_{r}
be its degree. Then the coherent sheaf
\pi_{2,*}(\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}})
coincides with a free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Z}(u)}
-sheaf of rank
m_{r}
up to coherent sheaves with support of dimension strictly smaller than
n
. It follows that the difference
m_{r}\mathcal{Z}(u)-(\pi_{2}\times\mathrm{id})_{*}({\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}})
of elements of
{\rm Fil}^{n}K_{0}^{\mathcal{Z}(u)}(\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1})
has zero image in
\Gr^{n}K_{0}^{\mathcal{Z}(u)}(\mathcal{Z}(u)\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1})
. The result follows because
m_{r}=[\tilde{K}_{n}^{[\varepsilon]}:\tilde{K}_{n}^{[r]}]=c_{r}
.

∎

9.
AT conjecture of type
(r,0)
: the case
r
even

In the next sections, we will be concerned with the AT conjectures, i.e., the arithmetic intersection number of §
3.6
, resp. of §
3.11
. In this section we consider the AT conjecture of type
(r,0)
in the case when
r
is even. We will reduce the problem to the FL and AFL for certain (non-unit) elements in the spherical Hecke algebra.

9.1.
An explicit transfer: an application of FL for the whole Hecke algebra

Since
r
is even, i.e.,
\varepsilon=0
, we have a direct sum decomposition
\Lambda_{0}=\Lambda^{\flat}_{0}\obot\langle u_{0}\rangle
where
\Lambda_{0}^{\flat}\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(W^{\flat}_{0})
, which necessarily satisfies
\Lambda^{\flat}\subset\Lambda^{\flat}_{0}
, cf. (
6.1.1
).
Recall from (
6.1.8
) and (
6.1.5
)

(9.1.1)

\displaystyle K_{n}^{\prime}=K_{n+1}^{\prime}\cap\mathrm{GL}_{n}(F)=\mathrm{GL}_{O_{F}}(\Lambda^{\flat}_{0}).

We also define
K_{n+1}=\mathrm{U}(\Lambda_{0})
and

(9.1.2)

\displaystyle K_{n}:=K_{n+1}\cap\mathrm{U}(W_{0}^{\flat})(F_{0})=\mathrm{U}(\Lambda_{0}^{\flat}),

(in terms of (
6.1.2
), we have
K_{n}=K_{n}^{[0]}
).
We continue with the choice of the Haar measures such that

\vol(K_{n})=1,\quad\vol(K_{n}^{\prime})=1,

(this normalization is consistent with (
6.1.10
) since
K_{n}^{[0]}=\widetilde{K}_{n}^{[0]}
, cf. Remark
6.1.2
(i)).
Recall from
[
14
, §4]
the atomic Hecke function in the spherical Hecke algebra
\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}}
, defined as the convolution,

(9.1.3)

\varphi_{r}:=\frac{1}{\vol(K_{n}^{[r]})}{\bf 1}_{K_{n}K_{n}^{[r]}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}K_{n}}.

We also recall from
[
14
, §3.6]
the base change homomorphisms between spherical Hecke algebras,

\mathrm{BC}_{n}:\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}},\quad\mathrm{BC}_{n+1}:\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n+1}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}},

and

\mathrm{BC}=\mathrm{BC}_{n}\otimes\mathrm{BC}_{n+1}:\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n+1}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}}.

Note that all of them are surjective. Recall from Remark
6.1.2
, (ii) that
\tilde{K}_{n}^{[r]}=K_{n}\cap K_{n}^{[r]}=K_{n}^{[r,0]}
.

Proposition 9.1.1
.

Recall that
r
is even. Let
\varphi^{\prime}
be any element in
\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n+1}}
such that

\mathrm{BC}(\varphi^{\prime})=\varphi_{r}\otimes{\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}}\in\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}}.

Then the function
\varphi^{\prime}
is a transfer of
(c_{r}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}\times K_{n+1}},0)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{0}})\times C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{1}})
.

Proof.

Taking into account that
c_{r}=\vol(\widetilde{K}_{n}^{[r]})^{-1}=\vol(K_{n}^{[r,0]})^{-1},
this follows from Lemma
9.1.2
below and the Jacquet–Rallis fundamental lemma for the full Hecke algebra due to Leslie
[
13
]
, cf.
[
14
, Thm. 3.7.1]
.
∎

Lemma 9.1.2
.

For every
g\in G_{W_{0}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
,

\displaystyle\Orb(g,\frac{1}{\vol(K_{n}^{[r,0]})^{2}}{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}\times K_{n+1}})=\Orb(g,\varphi_{r}\otimes{\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}}).

Proof.

We recall the definition (
4.2.9
) of the orbital integral,

\Orb(g,f)=\int_{H(F_{0})\times H(F_{0})}f(h_{1}^{-1}gh_{2})\,dh_{1}\,dh_{2}.

It follows that, for any
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(H)
,

\Orb(g,\phi_{1}\ast f\ast\phi_{2})=c(\phi_{1})c(\phi_{2})\Orb(g,f),

where

c(\phi):=\int_{H(F_{0})}\phi(h)\,dh.

Here the convolution is defined in the usual way induced by the two actions of
H(F_{0})
on
G(F_{0})
: for any
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(H)
and
f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G)
,

(\phi\ast f)(g):=\int_{H(F_{0})}\phi(h)f(h^{-1}g)\,dh,

and

(f\ast\phi)(g):=\int_{H(F_{0})}\phi(h)f(gh)\,dh.

In particular, we have

(9.1.4)

\Orb(g,f)=\Orb(g,{\bf 1}_{\Delta(K_{n})}\ast f\ast{\bf 1}_{\Delta(K_{n})}),

where we have used the fact that
\vol(K_{n})=1
. Here
\Delta(K_{n})
is the image of
K_{n}
under the inclusion
\mathrm{U}(W^{\flat}_{0})\simeq H\subset G=\mathrm{U}(W^{\flat}_{0})\times\mathrm{U}(W_{0})
.

We apply the consideration to
f={\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}\times K_{n+1}}
. Using (
9.1.2
), we see that
K_{n+1}
is bi-
K_{n}
-invariant. It follows from a substitution in the integral defining the convolution that we have

(9.1.5)

\displaystyle{\bf 1}_{\Delta(K_{n})}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}\times K_{n+1}}\ast{\bf 1}_{\Delta(K_{n})}=({\bf 1}_{K_{n}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n}})\otimes{\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}}.

Here the convolution is the usual one defined for any two functions in
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathrm{U}(W^{\flat}_{0}))
.
To compute the triple convolution on the RHS, we note

{\bf 1}_{K_{n}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}}=\vol(K_{n}^{[r,0]}){\bf 1}_{K_{n}K_{n}^{[r]}},\quad{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n}}=\vol(K_{n}^{[r,0]}){\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}K_{n}}.

In fact, the first convolution is left-
K_{n}
-invariant and right-
K_{n}^{[r]}
-invariant and has support in
K_{n}K_{n}^{[r]}
. Therefore it suffices to compare the values of both sides at
g=1
: the left hand side gives the volume of
K_{n}\cap K_{n}^{[r]}=K_{n}^{[r,0]}
, which verifies the first identity. The argument works for the second identity as well.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle{\bf 1}_{K_{n}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n}}

\displaystyle=\vol(K_{n}^{[r]})^{-1}{\bf 1}_{K_{n}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n}}

\displaystyle=\vol(K_{n}^{[r,0]})^{2}\vol(K_{n}^{[r]})^{-1}{\bf 1}_{K_{n}K_{n}^{[r]}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}K_{n}}

\displaystyle=\vol(K_{n}^{[r,0]})^{2}\varphi_{r},

where the last equality follows from the definition of
\varphi_{r}
, see (
9.1.3
). The lemma follows.

∎

We present an alternative proof by the “lattice counting interpretation” of orbital integrals, for the reason that the latter gives the heuristics on how to formulate the arithmetic transfer conjectures and will appear repeated in later sections. Following the notation of
[
14
, §4.1]
, we have
a diagram (analogous to the one for the RZ spaces in the next section), which is an analog of (
3.4.1
),

(9.1.6)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 11.22803pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 35.22803pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{N}^{[r,0]}_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 88.25003pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 11.22804pt\raise-25.79121pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 100.42665pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-11.22803pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{N}^{[r]}_{n}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 46.3658pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 87.50357pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{N}^{[0]}_{n+1}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Here
\mathbb{N}^{[0]}_{n+1}
, resp.
\mathbb{N}^{[r]}_{n}
, denotes the set of vertex lattices of type
0
in
W_{0}
, resp. of type
r
in
W_{0}^{\flat}
, and
\mathbb{N}^{[r,0]}_{n}
consists of pairs
(\Lambda^{\flat},\Lambda^{\flat}_{0})\in\mathbb{N}_{n}^{[r]}\times\mathbb{N}_{n}^{[0]}
such that
\Lambda^{\flat}\subset\Lambda^{\flat}_{0}
, and the two maps record
\Lambda^{\flat}
and
\Lambda^{\flat}_{0}\oplus\langle{u_{0}}\rangle
respectively. Combining the two maps above, we obtain an injective map,

\mathbb{N}^{[r,0]}_{n}\mathbb{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathbb{N}^{[0]}_{n+1}.

We also have the Hecke correspondence
\mathbb{T}_{\leq r}
which consists of the triples
(\Lambda^{\flat},\Lambda^{\flat}_{0},\Lambda^{\prime\flat}_{0})\in\mathbb{N}_{n}^{[r]}\times\mathbb{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathbb{N}_{n}^{[0]}
such that
\Lambda^{\flat}\subset\Lambda^{\flat}_{0}\cap\Lambda^{\prime\flat}_{0}
. In other words,
\mathbb{T}_{\leq r}
is the composition of
the obvious correspondence with its transpose (comp.
[
14
, (4.1.5)]
),

(9.1.7)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 11.25697pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 46.61002pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 87.96306pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{T}_{\leq r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern We form the cartesian product
\mathbb{N}_{n}^{[r]}(g)
,

(9.1.8)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 25.06947pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.68117pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 24.396pt\raise-26.8789pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{id},g)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 49.06947pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 49.06947pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\,\,(\mathbb{N}^{[0]}_{n}\times\mathbb{N}^{[0]}_{n+1})\times(\mathbb{N}^{[0]}_{n}\times\mathbb{N}^{[0]}_{n+1}).}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

The lower horizontal map in (
9.1.8
) maps
(\Lambda^{\flat}_{0},\Lambda^{\flat\prime}_{0})
to
\big((\Lambda^{\flat}_{0},\Lambda^{\flat}_{0}\oplus\langle u_{0}\rangle),g(\Lambda^{\flat\prime}_{0},\Lambda^{\flat\prime}_{0}\oplus\langle u_{0}\rangle)\big)
. The right vertical map maps
\big((\Lambda^{\flat},\Lambda^{\flat}_{0},\Lambda^{\flat\prime}_{0}),\Lambda_{0}\big)
to
\big((\Lambda^{\flat}_{0},\Lambda_{0}),(\Lambda^{\flat\prime}_{0},\Lambda_{0})\big)
.

Lemma 9.1.3
.

Let
g\in G_{W_{0}}(F_{0})
be regular semisimple.

(i)

We have

(9.1.9)

\Orb(g,\vol(K_{n}^{[r,0]})^{-2}\,{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}\times K_{n+1}})=\#(\mathbb{N}^{[r,0]}_{n}\cap g\mathbb{N}^{[r,0]}_{n})=\#\mathbb{N}_{n}^{[r]}(g),

where the second term is the cardinality of the intersection of two subsets of
\mathbb{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathbb{N}^{[0]}_{n+1}
.

(ii)

We have

(9.1.10)

\Orb(g,\varphi_{r}\otimes{\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}})=\#\mathbb{N}_{n}^{[r]}(g).

Proof.

It obviously suffices to consider elements
g
of the form
g=(1,g^{\sharp})
, with
g^{\sharp}\in\mathrm{U}_{W_{0}}(F_{0})
.

In part (i), the first identity is an easy exercise, by unfolding the orbital integral.
Unpacking the definitions, the intersection
\mathbb{N}^{[r,0]}_{n}\cap g\mathbb{N}^{[r,0]}_{n}
is in bijection with the set of triples
(\Lambda^{\flat},\Lambda^{\flat}_{0},\Lambda^{\flat\prime}_{0})\in\mathbb{N}_{n}^{[r]}\times\mathbb{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathbb{N}_{n}^{[0]}
such that
\Lambda^{\flat}\subset\Lambda^{\flat}_{0}\cap\Lambda^{\flat\prime}_{0}
and

g^{\sharp}(\Lambda^{\flat\prime}_{0}\oplus\langle{u_{0}}\rangle)=\Lambda^{\flat}_{0}\oplus\langle{u_{0}}\rangle
. On the other hand, this last set of triples is easily seen to be in bijection with the cartesian product
\mathbb{N}_{n}^{[r]}(g)
in (
9.1.8
), and this proves part (i).

For part (ii), we note that for any spherical Hecke function of the form
\varphi=\varphi_{r}\otimes\varphi_{r^{\prime}}
on
G_{W_{0}}(F_{0})
,
we have

\Orb((1,g^{\sharp}),\varphi)=\sum_{h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathrm{U}(W_{0}^{\flat})/K_{n}}\varphi(h_{1}^{-1}(1,g^{\sharp})h_{2}).

We may naturally identify
\mathrm{U}(W_{0}^{\flat})/K_{n}
with the set
{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(W_{0}^{\flat})
. Then the index set in the above sum is in bijection with the set of pairs
(\Lambda_{0}^{\flat},\Lambda_{0}^{{}^{\prime}\flat})\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(W_{0}^{\flat})\times{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(W_{0}^{\flat})
such that
the relative position of
\Lambda_{0}^{\flat}
and
\Lambda_{0}^{{}^{\prime}\flat}
(resp., of
\Lambda_{0}^{\flat}\oplus\langle u_{0}\rangle
and
g^{\sharp}(\Lambda_{0}^{{}^{\prime}\flat}\oplus\langle u_{0}\rangle)
) is stipulated by
\varphi_{r}
(resp. by
\varphi_{r^{\prime}}
). Taking now
\varphi_{r^{\prime}}={\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}}
, it is easy to see that the set of such pairs is bijective to the image of the left vertical map in (
9.1.8
). Moreover, the weight factor
\varphi(h_{1}^{-1}(1,g^{\sharp})h_{2})
is exactly the size of the fiber of this map. This concludes the proof of part (ii).

∎

Remark 9.1.4
.

The natural-looking candidate
{\bf 1}_{{K}_{n}^{\prime[r]}\times K_{n+1}^{\prime}}
appears to fail to be a transfer (up to a constant multiple) of
{\bf 1}_{{K}_{n}^{[r]}\times K_{n+1}}
. We can show this at least when
n=r=2
and the method below should work in general. Indeed, by the method of the proof of the lemma, we have the identity for
G^{\prime}
,

\Orb(\gamma,{\bf 1}_{{K}_{n}^{\prime[r]}\times K_{n+1}^{\prime}})=\vol(K_{n}^{\prime[r,0]})\Orb(\gamma,{\bf 1}_{K^{\prime}_{n}{K}_{n}^{\prime[r]}\times K_{n+1}^{\prime}}),

where
K_{n}^{\prime[r,0]}=K_{n}^{\prime}\cap K_{n}^{\prime[r]}
. (Note that we only use the action of
H_{1}^{\prime}
from the left.)
Note that the function
{\bf 1}_{K^{\prime}_{n}{K}_{n}^{\prime[r]}\times K^{\prime}_{n+1}}
is not in the spherical Hecke algebra but that the image
r^{\eta^{n-1}}({\bf 1}_{K^{\prime}_{n}K_{n}^{\prime[r]}})
lies in
\mathcal{H}_{S_{n}}
(in
[
14
, §3.7]
, the definition of the map
r^{\eta^{n-1}}
makes sense for any function in
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathrm{U}(W_{0}^{\flat}))
; in particular we can apply it to the function
{\bf 1}_{K^{\prime}_{n}{K}_{n}^{\prime[r]}}
). Hence,
using the isomorphism
\mathrm{BC}^{\eta}_{S_{n}}
in the commutative diagram from
[
14
]
,

(9.1.11)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 13.05688pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.05688pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.64938pt\raise 7.34224pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.34224pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{r^{\eta^{n-1}}_{\ast}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.05688pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.82715pt\raise-22.28745pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.89168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{BC}_{n}}$}}}\kern 52.63115pt\raise-16.80334pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.70752pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{BC}^{\eta}_{S_{n}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.63115pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 42.02626pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern it suffices to compare

\mathrm{BC}^{\eta}_{S_{n}}(r^{\eta^{n-1}}({\bf 1}_{K^{\prime}_{n}K_{n}^{\prime[r]}}))
and
\varphi_{r}

(up to a constant multiple). One can show that the function
{\bf 1}_{K^{\prime}_{n}K_{n}^{\prime[r]}}
is equivalent to the spherical function
{\bf 1}_{K^{\prime}_{n}\varpi^{(1^{r/2},0^{n-r/2})}K^{\prime}_{n}}
(i.e., has the same regular semi-simple orbital integrals, cf. Definition
4.2.1
). The question is now to compare

\mathrm{BC}({\bf 1}_{K^{\prime}_{n}\varpi^{(1^{r/2},0^{n-r/2})}K^{\prime}_{n}})
with
\varphi_{r}
(up to a constant multiple).
Let us consider the special case
n=r=2
. Using notation and results from
[
14
, §7]
, the Satake transform of
{\bf 1}_{K^{\prime}_{2}\varpi^{(1,0)}K^{\prime}_{2}}
is
q(X+X^{-1})
, and the Satake transform of
\varphi_{2}
is

(q+1)+\phi_{1}=(q+1)+(q(X+1+X^{-1})-1)=q(X+2+X^{-1}).

They do not match!

9.2.
The AT conjecture

We continue to assume that
r
is even. We identify
W_{1}
with
\mathbb{V}_{n+1}
defined in §
3.2
and choose the special vector in
W_{1}
to be
{u}\in\mathbb{V}_{n+1}
defined in §
3.8
. Then we may identify the hermitian space
\mathrm{U}(W_{1})
, resp.
\mathrm{U}(W_{1}^{\flat})
, with
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V}_{n+1})
, resp.
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{W}^{[r]}_{n})
. Then
G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})
acts on
\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
via this identification and hence the arithmetic intersection number
\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}
defined in §
3.6
makes sense for
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})
.

Conjecture 9.2.1
.

Recall that
r
is even. Let
\varphi^{\prime}
be any element in
\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n+1}}
such that

\mathrm{BC}(\varphi^{\prime})=\varphi_{r}\otimes{\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}}\in\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}}.

(Then
\varphi^{\prime}
is a transfer of
(c_{r}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}\times K_{n+1}},0)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{0}})\times C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{1}})
.)

(i)

If
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}\big).

(ii)

For any
\tilde{\varphi}^{\prime}\sim\varphi^{\prime}
(i.e.,
\tilde{\varphi}^{\prime}
and
\varphi^{\prime}
have identical regular semi-simple orbital integrals, cf. Definition
4.2.1
), there exists
\varphi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{corr}}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
such that if
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\tilde{\varphi}^{\prime}\big)-\Orb\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{corr}}\big).

Remark 9.2.2
.

(i) When
r=0
, Conjecture
9.2.1
(i) recovers the (homogeneous version of) arithmetic fundamental lemma. It seems hard to formulate an inhomogeneous version of Conjecture
9.2.1
beyond the case
r=0
.

(ii) Part (ii) of Conjecture
9.2.1
follows from part (i) and the density conjecture
[
18
, Conj. 5.16]
.

It turns out that
Conjecture
9.2.1
is a consequence of the AFL conjecture for the Hecke correspondence formulated in
[
14
]
. We recall the statement of the latter.

Conjecture 9.2.3
.

(AFL for the spherical Hecke algebra, homogeneous version
[
14
, Conj. 6.1.4]
.)
Let
\varphi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n+1}}
, and let
\varphi={\rm BC}(\varphi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}}
. Then

\left\langle\mathbb{T}_{\varphi}(\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}}),g\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[0]}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}\big),

whenever
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
.

Here
\mathbb{T}_{\varphi}
is the Hecke operator on K-theory defined in
[
14
, §5]
. The full definition of
\mathbb{T}_{\varphi}
is delicate; for our purpose, the case of
\varphi_{r}\otimes{\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}}
is sufficient.
We
recall from
[
14
, §5.5]
that the Hecke correspondence on
\mathcal{N}_{n}
associated to
\varphi_{r}
is defined as

\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\leq r}=\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0,r]}\circ\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r,0]}.

More precisely we have the following diagram (analogous to (
9.1.7
)) (in which, as explained in
[
14
, §5]
, the cartesian square has to be interpreted in the framework of
derived algebraic geometry
),

(9.2.1)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 46.61002pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 87.96306pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\leq r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-33.15305pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
