3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0,r]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 88.68527pt\raise-59.09778pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 11.25697pt\raise-58.95769pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 96.9133pt\raise-33.15305pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 135.86354pt\raise-33.15305pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r,0]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 188.13908pt\raise-58.23311pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 111.14134pt\raise-59.05391pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 197.78494pt\raise-33.15305pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-11.25697pt\raise-66.53195pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 46.61002pt\raise-66.53195pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 88.68527pt\raise-66.53195pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r]}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 147.00131pt\raise-66.53195pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 188.13908pt\raise-66.53195pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

By abuse of notation, we also denote by
\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\leq r}
the Hecke correspondence
\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\leq r}\times\Delta_{\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n+1}}
on
\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n+1}
. Then
\mathbb{T}_{\varphi_{r}\otimes{\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}}}
is the Hecke operator induced by the Hecke correspondence
\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\leq r}
in K-theory, cf.
[
14
, §9]
.

Lemma 9.2.4
.

For every
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
,

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[0]}}=\left\langle\mathbb{T}_{\varphi_{r}\otimes{\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}}}(\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}}),g\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n+1}}.

Proof.

This is modeled on the “alternative proof” of Lemma
9.1.2
, but we replace the sets
\mathbb{N}^{[r]}_{n},\mathbb{N}^{[0]}_{n},
etc. by the respective RZ spaces. Recall that
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}=\mathcal{N}^{[r,0]}_{n}
, comp. the proof of Proposition
3.5.2
. Consider the following correspondence

(9.2.2)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 26.68007pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 50.68007pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{[r,0]}_{n}\times\Delta_{\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n+1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 121.83322pt\raise-9.32834pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.86111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi}_{2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 145.22653pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.14561pt\raise-9.32834pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.86111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi}_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.57553pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 166.17758pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-26.68007pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n+1}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 80.90881pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 141.13756pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n+1}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Then for
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}
viewed as a class
\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}}
in
K_{0}^{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}}(\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n+1})
, we have

(9.2.3)

(\widetilde{\pi}_{2})_{\ast}\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{\ast}(\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}})=\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r,0]},

as classes in
\Gr^{n}K_{0}^{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[r,0]}}(\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n+1})
.
It therefore follows from the projection formula that

\displaystyle\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[0]}}=

\displaystyle\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},(\widetilde{\pi}_{2})_{\ast}\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{\ast}(g\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}})\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[0]}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle(\widetilde{\pi}_{1})_{\ast}\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{\ast}({\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}),g\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[0]}},

where we note that the action of
g
commutes with the action of the correspondences.
Using (
9.2.3
) again, we obtain

(\widetilde{\pi}_{1})_{\ast}\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{\ast}({\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}})=(\widetilde{\pi}_{1})_{\ast}\widetilde{\pi}_{2}^{\ast}((\widetilde{\pi}_{2})_{\ast}\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{\ast}(\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}}))=(\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\leq r})_{*}(\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}})

by the definition of the Hecke correspondence
\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\leq r}
. Therefore we arrive at the desired assertion

\displaystyle\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[0]}}=\left\langle\mathbb{T}_{\varphi_{r}\otimes{\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}}}(\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}}),g\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[0]}}.

∎

Corollary 9.2.5
.

Recall that
r
is even. Conjecture

9.2.3
(for
\varphi=\varphi_{r}\otimes{\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}}
) implies Conjecture
9.2.1
(i).

Unfortunately, we do not know of any instance in which Conjecture

9.2.3
is known, if
n\geq 2
.

10.
AT conjecture of type
(0,r)
: the case
r
even

We continue to denote by
W_{0}
(resp.
W_{1}
) a split (resp. non-split) Hermitian space of dimension
n+1
.

10.1.
Open compact subgroups

Let
0\leq r\leq n+1
. In this subsection
r
can be even or odd. We first define open compact subgroups on the unitary group side.

Recall the parity
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(r)\in\{0,1\}
of
r
, defined in (
3.5.1
). Fix a special vector
u_{0}
of norm
\varpi^{\varepsilon}
in
W_{\varepsilon}
. Then
W^{\flat}:=\langle{u_{0}}\rangle^{\perp}
is a split hermitian space. We fix a self-dual lattice
\Lambda^{\flat}_{0}\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(W^{\flat})
. Let
\Lambda
be a lattice of type
r
in
W_{\varepsilon}
. We impose the following condition

(10.1.1)

\Lambda_{0}^{\flat}\obot\langle u_{0}\rangle\supseteq\Lambda.

We let
\Lambda^{+}
be such a lattice satisfying the additional condition

(10.1.2)

u_{0}\in\Lambda,

(if it exists) and let
\Lambda^{-}
be such a lattice (if it exists) satisfying

(10.1.3)

u_{0}\notin\Lambda.

We denote by
K_{n}=K_{n}^{[0]}\subset\mathrm{U}(W^{\flat})(F_{0})
the stabilizer of the self-dual lattice
\Lambda_{0}^{\flat}
, resp.
K_{n+1}^{[\varepsilon]}\subset\mathrm{U}(W_{\varepsilon})(F_{0})
the stabilizer of the vertex lattice
\Lambda_{0}^{\flat}\obot\langle u_{0}\rangle
of type
\varepsilon
, resp.
K_{n+1}^{[r],+}\subset\mathrm{U}(W_{\varepsilon})(F_{0})
and
K_{n+1}^{[r],-}\subset\mathrm{U}(W_{\varepsilon})(F_{0})
the stabilizers of the type
r
vertex lattices

\Lambda^{+}
and
\Lambda^{-}
, respectively.

Lemma 10.1.1
.

Consider the action of the group
\mathrm{U}(W^{\flat})
on the set of pairs
(\Lambda_{0}^{\flat},\Lambda)
satisfying (
10.1.1
).

(i)

When
r
is odd or
r=0
, there is exactly one orbit with the representative given by
(\Lambda_{0}^{\flat},\Lambda^{+})
.

(ii)

When
r
is even and
r=n+1
, there is exactly one orbit with the representative given by
(\Lambda_{0}^{\flat},\Lambda^{-})
.

(iii)

When
r
is even and
1\leq r\leq n
,
there are exactly two orbits with representatives given by
(\Lambda_{0}^{\flat},\Lambda^{+})
and
(\Lambda_{0}^{\flat},\Lambda^{-})
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma
6.1.1
. We can assume
\Lambda_{0}^{\flat}
is the standard vertex lattice of type
0
. Let
\overline{W}=\varpi^{-1}(\Lambda_{0}^{\flat}\obot\langle u_{0}\rangle)/(\Lambda_{0}^{\flat}\obot\langle u_{0}\rangle)^{\vee}
be the
\mathbb{F}_{q^{2}}/\mathbb{F}_{q}
-hermitian space of dimension
n+1-\varepsilon
with the induced (non-degenerate) hermitian form. Then the set of type
r
lattices
\Lambda
contained in
\Lambda_{0}^{\flat}\obot\langle u_{0}\rangle
corresponds to the set of isotropic subspaces of
\overline{W}
of dimension
(r-\varepsilon)/2
(sending
\Lambda
to
\Lambda^{\vee}/(\Lambda_{0}^{\flat}\obot\langle u_{0}\rangle)^{\vee}
).
We need to consider the action of
K_{n}
on the set of such
\Lambda
.
We have a subspace
\varpi^{-1}\langle\overline{u}_{0}\rangle/\langle\overline{u}_{0}\rangle^{\vee}\subset\overline{W}
generated by the class
\overline{u}_{0}
of
\varpi^{-1}u_{0}
in
\overline{W}
. Note that this subspace is zero in the first two cases and non-degenerate in the third case. We let
\overline{W}^{\flat}
be the orthogonal complement of
\langle{\overline{u}_{0}}\rangle
. Then the reduction of
K_{n}
is the subgroup
\mathrm{U}(\overline{W}^{\flat})
of the finite unitary group
\mathrm{U}(\overline{W})
. We are reduced to considering the action of
\mathrm{U}(\overline{W}^{\flat})
on the set of isotropic subspaces
N
of
\overline{W}
of dimension
(r-\varepsilon)/2
.

If
\varepsilon=1
, then
\overline{W}^{\flat}=\overline{W}^{\flat}
and Witt’s theorem implies that
\mathrm{U}(\overline{W})
acts transitively. Now assume
\varepsilon=0
so that
\langle{\overline{u}_{0}}\rangle
is an anisotropic line. Then Witt’s theorem implies that there are at most two orbits, depending on whether
\langle{\overline{u}_{0}}\rangle\perp N
or not. Only one orbit exists when
r=0
(then we necessarily have
\langle{\overline{u}_{0}}\rangle\perp N
) or when
r=n+1\equiv 0\mod 2
(then
\langle{\overline{u}_{0}}\rangle\perp N
cannot happen since
N
is maximal isotropic in an even dimensional hermitian space
\overline{W}
while
\langle{\overline{u}_{0}}\rangle
is anisotropic). In the remaining cases, both can happen and we obtain exactly two orbits. Now the desired assertion follows.
∎

Remark 10.1.2
.

A more direct argument for part (i) in the case
r
odd is that we have then
\Lambda_{0}\supset\varpi(\Lambda_{0}^{\flat}\obot\langle u_{0}\rangle)^{\vee}\ni u_{0}
and hence
\langle{u_{0}}\rangle
is an orthogonal direct summand of
\Lambda_{0}
.

Corollary 10.1.3
.

(i)

When
r
is odd or
r=0
, then

K_{n+1}^{[\varepsilon]}K_{n+1}^{[r],+}=K_{n}K_{n+1}^{[r],+},\quad K_{n+1}^{[r],+}K_{n+1}^{[\varepsilon]}=K_{n+1}^{[r],+}K_{n}.

(ii)

When
r
is even and
r=n+1
, then

K_{n+1}^{[0]}K_{n+1}^{[r],-}=K_{n}K_{n+1}^{[r],-},\quad K_{n+1}^{[r],-}K_{n+1}^{[0]}=K_{n+1}^{[r],-}K_{n}.

(iii)

Let
r
be even and
1\leq r\leq n
. In this case, both
\Lambda^{+}
and
\Lambda^{-}
exist. There is an element
h\in K_{n+1}^{[0]}
such that

(10.1.4)

K_{n+1}^{[r],-}=hK_{n+1}^{[r],+}h^{-1}.

There are disjoint sum decompositions,

\displaystyle K_{n+1}^{[0]}K_{n+1}^{[r],+}

\displaystyle=K_{n}K_{n+1}^{[r],+}\sqcup K_{n}hK_{n+1}^{[r],+}

\displaystyle K_{n+1}^{[0]}K_{n+1}^{[r],-}

\displaystyle=K_{n}K_{n+1}^{[r],-}\sqcup K_{n}h^{-1}K_{n+1}^{[r],-}.

There are also similar decompositions for
K_{n+1}^{[r],+}K_{n+1}^{[0]}
and
K_{n+1}^{[r],-}K_{n+1}^{[0]}
, etc., by taking the inverses of the two sides of the equations.

Proof.

We only prove part (iii), as the others can be proved similarly.
Following the notation in the proof of Lemma
10.1.1
, we have bijections

K_{n+1}^{[\varepsilon]}K_{n+1}^{[r],+}/K_{n+1}^{[r],+}\simeq K_{n+1}^{[\varepsilon]}/(K_{n+1}^{[\varepsilon]}\cap K_{n+1}^{[r],+})\simeq\mathrm{U}(\overline{W})/\overline{P}

where
\overline{P}
is the parabolic stabilizing the isotropic subspace corresponding to
\Lambda^{+}
.
The action of
K_{n}
on
K_{n+1}^{[\varepsilon]}K_{n+1}^{[r],+}/K_{n+1}^{[r],+}
corresponds to the action of
\mathrm{U}(\overline{W}^{\flat})
on
\mathrm{U}(\overline{W})/\overline{P}
, where, we recall,
\overline{W}^{\flat}
is the orthogonal complement of the anisotropic line generated by
\overline{u}_{0}\in\overline{W}
.
By Lemma
10.1.1
, there are exactly two
\mathrm{U}(\overline{W}^{\flat})
orbits, corresponding to the two isotropic subspaces corresponding to
\Lambda^{+}
and
\Lambda^{-}
respectively. The base point in
\mathrm{U}(\overline{W})/\overline{P}
corresponds to
\Lambda^{+}
. Pick any representative of the other orbit, say
\overline{h}\in\mathrm{U}(\overline{W})
, and lift it to
h\in K_{n+1}^{[\varepsilon]}
. Then
h
satisfies (
10.1.4
) and the decomposition
K_{n+1}^{[\varepsilon]}K_{n+1}^{[r],+}=K_{n}K_{n+1}^{[r],+}\sqcup K_{n}hK_{n+1}^{[r],+}
holds. This proves the existence of
h
and the first equation in (iii). The second equation is proved in a similar way.
∎

Remark 10.1.4
.

Note that, for any two
h,h^{\prime}
satisfying (
10.1.4
), we have
h^{\prime}h^{-1}\in K_{n+1}^{[r],+}
so that the truth of the assertion in (iii) is independent of the choice of
h
(a maximal parahoric is its own normalizer). The existence of
h
implies

K_{n+1}^{[0]}K_{n+1}^{[r],+}K_{n+1}^{[0]}=K_{n+1}^{[0]}K_{n+1}^{[r],-}K_{n+1}^{[0]}.

10.2.
Orbital integrals

Let us consider the analogous diagram to (
3.10.1
)

(10.2.1)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 14.83916pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.83916pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.83916pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.22804pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.25612pt\raise-16.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.60417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\square}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 40.22804pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{N}_{n+1}^{[r,\varepsilon]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.76224pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-11.25697pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{N}_{n}^{[0]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 11.25697pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.83916pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 38.83916pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{N}_{n+1}^{[\varepsilon]}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Explicitly the set
\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r]}
consists of
(\Lambda^{\flat},\Lambda)\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(W^{\flat}_{0})\times{\mathrm{Vert}}^{r}(W_{\varepsilon})
such that
\Lambda^{\flat}\obot\langle u_{0}\rangle\supseteq\Lambda
holds. According to whether
u_{0}\in\Lambda
or not, the set
\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r]}
is partitioned into a disjoint union of two subsets
\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r],+}
and
\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r],-}
. Note that
\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r],-}
is empty if
r
is odd or
r=0
, and that
\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r],+}
is empty if
r
is even and
r=n+1
, cf. Lemma
10.1.1
. Then
\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r],+}
(if non-empty) is naturally bijective to
\mathbb{N}^{[r,0]}
.
We have

(10.2.2)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 52.4203pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-52.4203pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r]}=\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r],+}\sqcup\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r],-}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 76.4203pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 76.4203pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathbb{N}^{[r]}_{n+1}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Define the analogous function of
\varphi_{r}
in (
9.1.3
),

(10.2.3)

\varphi_{r}^{[\varepsilon]}:=\frac{1}{\vol(K_{n+1}^{[r]})}{\bf 1}_{K^{[\varepsilon]}_{n+1}K_{n+1}^{[r]}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}^{[r]}K^{[\varepsilon]}_{n+1}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{U}(W_{\varepsilon})}.

Here we normalize the Haar measure so that
\vol(K^{[\varepsilon]}_{n+1})=1
.

We have an analog of Lemma
9.1.3
, interpreting the orbital integral as a suitable lattice counting.

Lemma 10.2.1
.

For any regular semi-simple
g\in(\mathrm{U}(W^{\flat})\times\mathrm{U}(W_{\varepsilon}))(F_{0})
, we have

\displaystyle\Orb(g,{\bf 1}_{K_{n}}\otimes\varphi^{[\varepsilon]}_{r})

\displaystyle=\#(\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r]}\cap g\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r]})

\displaystyle\Orb(g,{\bf 1}_{K_{n}\times K^{[r],+}_{n+1}})

\displaystyle=\vol(K_{n}\cap K^{[r],+}_{n+1})^{2}\#(\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r],+}\cap g\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r],+}),

\displaystyle\Orb(g,{\bf 1}_{K_{n}\times K^{[r],-}_{n+1}})

\displaystyle=\vol(K_{n}\cap K^{[r],-}_{n+1})^{2}\#(\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r],-}\cap g\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r],-}),

\displaystyle\Orb(g,{\bf 1}_{K_{n}\times hK^{+}_{n+1}})

\displaystyle=\vol(K_{n}\cap K^{[r],+}_{n+1})\vol(K_{n}\cap K^{[r],-}_{n+1})\#(\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r],+}\cap g\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r],-}).

Here the intersection is taken inside
\mathbb{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathbb{N}^{[r]}_{n+1}
and in the last three identities
r
is even with
2\leq r\leq n
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma
9.1.3
, and we omit the details. For the first equality, we will see an analog in the context of RZ spaces in the proof of Lemma
10.3.3
below.
∎

Remark 10.2.2
.

We do not know the explicit transfer of the functions appearing on the LHS above and therefore the analogue of Proposition
9.1.1
is still missing.

10.3.
The AT conjecture

We assume that
r
is even, and hence
\varepsilon=0
.
We now consider the intersection number defined by (
3.11.1
)

(10.3.1)

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r]}}:=\chi(\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r]},{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}\cap^{\mathbb{L}}g{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}),\quad g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}.

Conjecture 10.3.1
.

Recall that
r
is even. Let
\varphi^{\prime}
be any element in
\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n+1}}
such that

\mathrm{BC}(\varphi^{\prime})={\bf 1}_{K_{n}}\otimes\varphi_{r}\in\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}}.

If
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r]}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}\big).

Remark 10.3.2
.

In contrast to Conjecture
9.2.1
, we could formulate an inhomogeneous version of Conjecture
10.3.1
.

Similar to the AT conjecture of type
(r,0)
with even
r
, Conjecture
10.3.1
is a consequence of the AFL conjecture
9.2.3
. We have the following analog of Lemma
9.2.4
.

Lemma 10.3.3
.

For every
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, we have

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r]}}=\left\langle\mathbb{T}_{{\bf 1}_{K_{n}}\otimes\varphi_{r}}(\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}}),g\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n+1}}.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma
9.2.4
and we only indicate the differences.
Consider the correspondence analogous to (
9.2.2
),

(10.3.2)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 26.68007pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 50.68007pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Delta_{\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n}}\times\mathcal{N}^{[0,r]}_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 116.7752pt\raise-9.32834pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.86111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi}_{2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 139.28188pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.27364pt\raise-9.32834pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.86111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi}_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 22.60466pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 159.26205pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-26.68007pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n+1}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 77.43658pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 134.1931pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n+1}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Then, unpacking the definition of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}
, we have

(10.3.3)

(\widetilde{\pi}_{2})_{\ast}\widetilde{\pi}_{1}^{\ast}(\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}})={\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}},

as classes in
\Gr^{n}K_{0}^{{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}}(\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n+1})
.
The rest of the proof is similar to that of Lemma
9.2.4
, applying the projection formula.

∎

Corollary 10.3.4
.

Recall that
r
is even. Conjecture

9.2.3
(for
\varphi={\bf 1}_{K_{n}}\otimes\varphi_{r}
) implies Conjecture
10.3.1
.

Remark 10.3.5
.

There is no analogous relation to Conjecture

9.2.3
for the variants for
\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[r],+},\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[r],-}
and the mixed case, cf. §
13.2
, Conjecture
13.2.2
.

11.
AT conjectures of type
(n,0)
and
(0,n+1)
with
n
odd.

In this section we consider the AT conjecture of type
(r,0)
when
r=n
is odd. For this, we use an idea similar to the one in the last section: reduce the problem to the FL and AFL for certain (non-unit) elements in the spherical Hecke algebra. We then use the exceptional isomorphism (
3.10.5
) to deduce the AT conjecture of type
(0,n+1)
, where
n
is odd.

11.1.
An explicit transfer: an application of the FL for the whole Hecke algebra

We first return to the situation in §
6.1
.
We pick a basis
e_{1},e_{2},\cdots,e_{n}
of the
n
-dimensional Hermitian space
W_{0}^{\flat}
such that
(e_{i},e_{i})=\varpi
for all
i
, and add the special vector
u_{0}
with
(u_{0},u_{0})=\varpi
. Then, because
n=r
is odd,
W_{0}=W_{0}^{\flat}\oplus\langle{u_{0}}\rangle_{F}
is a split Hermitian space. Set
\Lambda^{\flat}=\langle{e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}}\rangle\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{n}(W_{0}^{\flat})
and
\Lambda=\Lambda^{\flat}\oplus\langle{u_{0}}\rangle\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{n+1}(W_{0})
. Then
\Lambda
is selfdual up to a scalar. Let
\Lambda_{0}\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(W_{0})
be such that
\Lambda_{0}\supset\Lambda
. Let
K_{n+1}=K_{n+1}^{[0]}=\mathrm{U}(\Lambda_{0})
and
K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}=\mathrm{U}(\Lambda)
be the stabilizer of
\Lambda_{0}
and
\Lambda
respectively. Both are hyperspecial compact open subgroups of
\mathrm{U}(W_{0})
. Let
K_{n}^{[r]}=\mathrm{U}(\Lambda^{\flat})
.

Let
W_{0}^{\prime}
be the same space as
W_{0}
but with a rescaling of the hermitian form by a factor
\varpi^{-1}
. Then there exists an isometry between
W_{0}
and
W_{0}^{\prime}
that induces a bijection between the vertex lattices of type
(n+1)
in
W_{0}
and the vertex lattices of type
0
in
W_{0}^{\prime}
. This isometry also induces an isomorphism between the unitary groups and hence also isomorphisms of the Hecke algebras
\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}}
with the spherical Hecke algebras, so that the base change homomorphisms
\mathrm{BC}:\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n}}\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}
and
\mathrm{BC}:\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n+1}}\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}}
make sense.

We continue with the Haar measures fixed in (
6.1.10
), so that

\vol(K_{n}^{[n]})=1,\quad\vol(K_{n}^{\prime})=1,

(note that
\vol(K_{n}^{[n]})=\vol(K_{n}^{[0]})=\vol(\widetilde{K}_{n}^{[0]})
).
We also define the atomic function analogous to (
9.1.3
) but for the current hyperspecial compact open
K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}
,

(11.1.1)

\varphi_{0}^{[n+1]}:={\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}K_{n+1}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}}\in\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}}.

Note that
\vol(K_{n+1}^{[n+1]})=\vol(K_{n+1})
which is taken to be one to normalize our choice of measure. Here we use the notation
\varphi_{0}^{[n+1]}
to be consistent with
\varphi_{r}^{[\varepsilon]}
in (
10.2.3
). In particular, the function
\varphi_{r}
in (
9.1.3
) could be renamed as
\varphi_{r}^{[0]}
.

We have the following result similar to Proposition
9.1.1
.

Proposition 11.1.1
.

Let
r=n
be odd. Let
\varphi^{\prime}
be any element in
\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n+1}}
such that

\mathrm{BC}(\varphi^{\prime})={\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}\otimes\varphi_{0}^{[n+1]}\in\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}}.

Then
\varphi^{\prime}
is a transfer of
(c_{n}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[n]}\times K_{n+1}},0)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{0}})\times C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{1}})
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
11.1.2
below and the Jacquet–Rallis fundamental lemma for the full Hecke algebra due to Leslie
[
13
]
, cf.
[
14
, Thm. 3.7.1]
. Note that, even though the theorem of Leslie
[
13
]
was formulated for the hyperspecial compact open associated to the self-dual lattices, it can be easily translated into the version for the hyperspecial compact open in the current set-up.
∎

Lemma 11.1.2
.

Assume that
n=r
is odd. Then we have for
g\in G_{W_{0}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
,

\displaystyle\Orb(g,{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[n]}\times K_{n+1}})

\displaystyle=\vol(K_{n}^{[n]}\cap K_{n+1})^{2}\,\Orb(g,{\bf 1}_{K^{[n]}_{n}}\otimes\varphi_{0}^{[n+1]}),

\displaystyle\Orb(g,{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[0]}\times K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}})

\displaystyle=\vol(K_{n}^{[0]}\cap K_{n+1}^{[n+1]})^{2}\,\Orb(g,{\bf 1}_{K^{[n]}_{n}}\otimes\varphi_{0}^{[n+1]}).

Proof.

Let us prove the first identity and the second identity is proved the same way.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma
9.1.2
.
We only indicate the changes needed. As in (
9.1.4
), using
\vol(K_{n}^{[n]})=1
, we have

(11.1.2)

\Orb(g,f)=\Orb(g,{\bf 1}_{\Delta(K_{n}^{[n]})}\ast f\ast{\bf 1}_{\Delta(K_{n}^{[n]})}).

Similar to (
9.1.5
), we obtain

{\bf 1}_{\Delta(K_{n}^{[n]})}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[n]}\times K_{n+1}}\ast{\bf 1}_{\Delta(K_{n}^{[n]})}={\bf 1}_{K^{[n]}_{n}}\otimes({\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}).

Now it suffices to note that, by Lemma
11.1.3
below,

{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}}=\vol(K_{n}^{[n]}\cap K_{n+1}){\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}K_{n+1}},\quad{\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}}\ast{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}=\vol(K_{n}^{[n]}\cap K_{n+1}){\bf 1}_{K_{n+1}K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}}.

∎

The following lemma, used in the previous proof, essentially follows from Corollary
10.1.3
.

Lemma 11.1.3
.

We have an equality of subsets of
\mathrm{U}(W_{0})(F_{0})
,

K_{n}^{[n]}K_{n+1}^{[0]}=K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}K_{n+1}^{[0]},\quad K_{n+1}^{[0]}K_{n}^{[n]}=K_{n+1}^{[0]}K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}.

Proof.

By rescaling the hermitian form on both hermitian spaces
W_{0},W_{0}^{\flat}
by a factor
\varpi^{-1}
, the two equations become those in Case (ii) of Corollary
10.1.3
, noting that there the group
K_{n+1}^{[r],-}
is equal to
K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}
when
r=n+1
.

Remark 11.1.4
.

Similar to Lemma
9.1.2
, we have an alternative proof of Lemma
11.1.2
using the lattice counting interpretation of orbital integrals in Lemma
10.2.1
. Now, we have
\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r]}=\widetilde{}\mathbb{M}_{n}^{[r],-}\simeq\mathbb{N}^{[n,0]}
. Hence the right hand sides of the first and the third equations in Lemma
10.2.1
are equal (up to the desired constants). It follows that their left hand sides are also equal, as desired.

∎

11.2.
AT conjecture of type
(r,0)
with
r=n
odd

The following conjecture is analogous to Conjecture
9.2.1
.

Conjecture 11.2.1
.

Let
n=r
be odd. Let
\varphi^{\prime}
be any element in
\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n+1}}
such that

\mathrm{BC}(\varphi^{\prime})={\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}\otimes\varphi_{0}^{[n+1]}\in\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}}.

If
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}\big).

Similarly to the even
r
case,
Conjecture
11.2.1
is a consequence of Conjecture
9.2.3
(AFL for the full Hecke algebra). This is based on the following analog of Lemma
9.2.4
.

Let
\mathcal{T}_{n+1}^{\leq(n+1)}
be the Hecke correspondence associated to the spherical Hecke function
{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}\otimes\varphi_{0}^{[r+1]}\in\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}}
, and let
\mathbb{T}_{{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}\otimes\varphi_{0}^{[n+1]}}
be the corresponding Hecke operator in K-theory, cf.
[
14
, §9]
.

Lemma 11.2.2
.

Let
n=r
be odd.
For every
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
,

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}=\left\langle\mathbb{T}_{{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}\otimes\varphi_{0}^{[n+1]}}(\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[n]}}),g\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[n]}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[n]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}^{[n+1]}_{n+1}}.

The proof is analogous to that of Lemma
9.2.4
, and we leave the details to the reader.

Corollary 11.2.3
.

Conjecture

9.2.3
(for
\varphi={\bf 1}_{K_{n}}\otimes\varphi_{n+1}
) implies Conjecture
11.2.1
.

Proof.

Let us rescale the hermitian spaces
W_{0}
and
W_{0}^{\flat}
by the factor
\varpi^{-1}
to have the natural isomorphism
\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}}\simeq\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}^{[0]}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}^{[0]}}
. Under this isomorphism, the function
\varphi_{0}^{[n+1]}\in\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}}
(resp.
{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}\in\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}
) corresponds to the function
\varphi_{n+1}\in\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}}
(resp.
{\bf 1}_{K_{n}}\in\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}}
). Then the isomorphism
\mathcal{N}^{[n]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}^{[n+1]}_{n+1}\simeq\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n+1}
also induces an isomorphism of correspondences so that we have an equality of intersection numbers

\left\langle\mathbb{T}_{{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}\otimes\varphi_{0}^{[n+1]}}(\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[n]}}),g\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[n]}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[n]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}^{[n+1]}_{n+1}}=\left\langle\mathbb{T}_{{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[0]}}\otimes\varphi_{n+1}}(\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}}),g\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}^{[0]}_{n+1}}.

The corollary nows follows from Lemma
11.2.2
.

Alternatively, this corollary also follows from Lemma
11.3.1
below together with Corollary
10.3.4
.
∎

Since Conjecture

9.2.3
is known in the case
n=1

[
14
, Thm. 7.5.1]
, we deduce the following statement.

Theorem 11.2.4
.

The AT conjecture of type
(1,0)
holds for
n=1
, i.e., Conjecture
11.2.1
holds when
n=r=1
. ∎

Remark 11.2.5
.

We point out that in the special case
n=r=1
the natural looking function
\mathbf{1}_{K^{\prime[1]}_{1}\times K_{2}^{\prime}}
is not a transfer of
{\bf 1}_{K_{1}^{[1]}}\otimes\varphi_{0}^{[2]}
(up to a constant multiple). Using the rescaling isomorphism in the proof of Corollary (
11.2.3
), we need to consider
{\bf 1}_{K_{1}}\otimes\varphi_{2}
. But we have

\varphi_{2}=(q+1){\bf 1}_{K_{2}}+{\bf 1}_{K_{2}\varpi^{(1,-1)}K_{2}},

cf.
[
14
, (7.1.5)]
.
The function
\mathbf{1}_{K^{\prime[1]}_{1}\times K_{2}^{\prime}}

can be shown to be equivalent to (up to a constant multiple)

{\bf 1}_{\mathrm{GL}_{1}(O_{F})}\otimes({\bf 1}_{K_{2}^{\prime}\varpi^{(1,0)}K_{2}^{\prime}}-{\bf 1}_{K_{2}^{\prime}})\in\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{1}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{2}}.

In particular, using the explicit calculation in
[
14
, §7]
, we see that it does not transfer to
{\bf 1}_{K_{1}}\otimes\varphi_{2}
on the unitary side.

11.3.
AT conjecture of type
(0,r+1)
with
r=n
odd

The following lemma shows the equivalence of the AT conjectures of type
(n,0)
and
(0,n+1)
with
n
odd.

Lemma 11.3.1
.

Let
n
be odd. For every
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
,
we have

\left\langle\widetilde{}\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[n]},g\widetilde{}\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[n]}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[n]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[0]}}=\left\langle\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[n+1]},g\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[n+1]}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[n+1]}}.

Proof.

The rescaling isomorphism
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[n]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[0]}\simeq\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[n+1]}
is
G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})
-equivariant, and induces an isomorphism
\widetilde{}\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[n]}\simeq\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[n+1]}
, cf. (
3.10.5
).
∎

We therefore may formulate the following conjecture.

Conjecture 11.3.2
.

Let
n
be odd. Let
\varphi^{\prime}
be any element in
\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K^{\prime}_{n+1}}
such that

\mathrm{BC}(\varphi^{\prime})={\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}\otimes\varphi_{0}^{[n+1]}\in\mathcal{H}_{K_{n}^{[n]}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{H}_{K_{n+1}^{[n+1]}}.

If
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[n+1]},g\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[n+1]}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[n+1]}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}\big).

We again have the following corollary, whose proof is similar to that of Corollary
11.2.3
and will be omitted.

Corollary 11.3.3
.

Let
n
be odd. Conjecture

9.2.3
(for
\varphi={\bf 1}_{K_{n}}\otimes\varphi_{n+1}
) implies Conjecture
11.3.2
.

Again, as in Theorem
11.2.4
, we deduce the AT conjecture of type
(0,2)
for
n=1
.

12.
AT conjecture of type
(0,1)

In this section we consider the AT conjecture of type
(0,r)
when
r=1
. In fact, this case was considered in
[
19
, §10, §14]
, where we reduced the AT conjecture to the (now known) AFL conjecture (for the unit element), at least in the artinian case. It was then revisited independently by Z. Zhang
[
30
]
, who gave a direct proof (as a special case of a more general result).

We are now in the set-up of §
10.1
, specialized to the case
r=1
. We have a special vector
u_{0}
of norm
\varpi
in
W_{1}
, and
W^{\flat}=\langle{u_{0}}\rangle^{\perp}
is split. (Note that in
[
19
]
, the special vector is denoted by
u_{1}
.) We have
\Lambda_{0}^{\flat}\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(W^{\flat})
and
\Lambda=\Lambda_{0}^{\flat}\obot\langle u_{0}\rangle\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{1}(W_{1})
, with corresponding parahoric subgroups
K_{n}
and
K_{n+1}^{[1]}
. Let
K_{n}^{\prime}\subset\mathrm{GL}(W^{\flat})
be the stabilizer of
\Lambda_{0}^{\flat}
and let
K^{\prime}_{n+1}(\varpi)\subset\mathrm{GL}(W_{1})
be the joint stabilizer of
\Lambda
and
\Lambda^{\vee}
(the latter group was denoted by
K_{0}(\varpi)
in
[
19
, §10]
). Let
K_{S}(\varpi)\subset S_{n+1}(F_{0})
denote the intersection
K^{\prime}_{n+1}(\varpi)\cap S_{n+1}(O_{F_{0}})
.

The following explicit transfer theorem was
[
19
, Conj. 10.3]
, and is now a theorem.

Theorem 12.0.1
.

(i)

(Homogeneous version)
The function
(-1)^{n-1}\frac{q^{n}-1}{q-1}\mathbf{1}_{K_{n}^{\prime}\times K^{\prime}_{n+1}}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
transfers to the pair of functions
(0,\mathbf{1}_{K_{n}\times K_{n+1}^{[1]}})\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{0}})\times C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{1}})
.

(ii)

(Inhomogeneous version)
The function
(-1)^{n-1}\mathbf{1}_{K_{S}(\varpi)}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(S)
transfers to the pair of functions
(0,\mathbf{1}_{K_{n+1}^{[1]}})\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathrm{U}(W_{0}))\times C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathrm{U}(W_{1}))
.

Proof.

Recall that
q
is odd.
In
[
19
, Thm. 14.1]
, the above statement was reduced to the FL for the Lie algebra, at least when
q\geq n
. The FL for Lie algebra is now known for any (odd) residue characteristic, see §
5.1
. Therefore the theorem follows, at least when
q\geq n
.

On the other hand, Z. Zhang
[
30
, Thm. 1.1]
also provided a direct proof of the explicit transfer for any maximal parahoric subgroup, which implies the claim for all odd
q
. Note that there is a sign difference between
[
19
]
and
[
30
]
.
∎

The following AT theorem was
[
19
, Conj. 10.4]
, and is now also a theorem. For the sake of brevity we only consider the inhomogeneous version.

Theorem 12.0.2
.

Suppose that
\gamma\in S_{n+1}({F_{0}})_{\mathrm{rs}}
matches an element
g\in\mathrm{U}(W_{0})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
. Then

\bigl\langle\mathcal{N}_{n},(1\times g)\mathcal{N}_{n}\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n+1}}\cdot\log q=-\operatorname{\partial Orb}\bigl(\gamma,(-1)^{n-1}\mathbf{1}_{K_{S}(\varpi)}\bigr).

Proof.

In
[
19
, Thm. 14.9, and Thm. 14.10]
, the above statement was reduced to the AFL conjecture (for the unit element), at least when
q\geq n
and when the intersection is artinian. The AFL is now known by
[
29
]
(for
q\geq n
) and
[
30
]
(for all odd
q
). Therefore the theorem follows, at least when
q\geq n
and the intersection is artinian.

On the other hand, Z. Zhang
[
30
, Thm. 1.4]
also provided a direct proof of the arithmetic transfer conjecture for any maximal parahoric subgroup (comp. Introduction), which implies our assertion for all odd
q
.
∎

Remark 12.0.3
.

The reduction argument in
[
19
, Thm. 14.9, and Thm. 14.10]
was done only when the intersection is artinian. However, the same idea should apply in general, so that Theorem
12.0.2
can be reduced to the AFL (for the unit element), at least when
q\geq n
. We leave this enhancement of
[
19
]
to interested readers.

13.
AT conjectures: the remaining cases

In the last sections
we have stated several cases of AT conjectures in which we have an explicit test function (at least with the help of the base change homomorphism). In the remaining cases, we do not have an explicit test function. Instead, similar to
[
18
, Conj. 5.3]
, we can only formulate an AT conjecture where we postulate the existence of a test function with an explicit transfer. Assuming the density conjecture
[
18
, Conj. 5.16]
, this conjecture implies that then any test function with the given transfer yields an AT identity, but with a correction function, analogous to part (ii) in Conjecture
9.2.1
.

13.1.
Type
(r,0)
and
r
odd

Let
r
be an odd integer such that
0\leq r\leq n
, not necessarily equal to
n
(the latter case is considered in §
11
).

Recall from (
3.6.1
) that we have defined the arithmetic intersection number

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}
.

Conjecture 13.1.1
.

Let
r
be odd such that
0\leq r\leq n
.

(i)

There exists
\varphi^{\prime}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
with transfer
(c_{r}^{2}\cdot{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}\times K_{n+1}},0)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{0}})\times C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{1}})
such that, if
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}\big).

(ii)

For any
\varphi^{\prime}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
transferring to
(c_{r}^{2}\cdot{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[r]}\times K_{n+1}},0)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{0}})\times C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{1}})
, there exists
\varphi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{corr}}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
such that, if
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}},g{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[r]}_{n}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}^{[r]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}\big)-\Orb\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{corr}}\big).

Note that by the density conjecture
[
18
, Conj. 5.16]
, part (ii) follows from part (i). Something analogous holds for all further conjectures in this section; in the interest of brevity, we have omitted these variants of these conjectures in the statements below.

Let us comment on Conjecture
13.1.1
. The case
r=n=1
has been treated in the last section (Theorem
11.2.4
). In the next simplest case when
r=1,n=2
, we can show that the natural candidate
\mathbf{1}_{{K}_{2}^{\prime[1]}\times K_{3}^{\prime}}
is a transfer of
({\bf 1}_{K_{2}^{[1]}\times K_{3}},0)
(up to a constant multiple). However, even in this case we did not prove the AT conjecture above.
In fact, beyond the case (
r=1,n=2
), we know nothing about the AT conjecture at the moment. We will pursue this direction in a future paper.

13.2.
Type
(0,r)

The heuristics for the explicit transfer in this case comes from Lemma
10.2.1
. We keep the notation
K_{n+1}^{[r]}
and
K_{n+1}^{[r],+}
and
K_{n+1}^{[r],-}

and
hK_{n+1}^{[r],+}
of that lemma.

Conjecture 13.2.1
.

Let
r
be such that
0\leq r\leq n+1
, with parity
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(r)
. Recall from §
10.1
the hermitian space
W_{\varepsilon}
; we denote by
W_{\varepsilon+1}
the hermitian space of the same dimension
n+1
and with opposite invariant. As before, the perp-spaces
W_{\varepsilon}^{\flat}
and
W_{\varepsilon+1}^{\flat}
are formed using special vectors of length
\varpi^{\varepsilon}
. Also, recall the function
\varphi_{r}^{[\varepsilon]}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{U}(W_{\varepsilon})}
from (
10.2.3
).

There exists
\varphi^{\prime}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
with transfer
({\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[0]}}\otimes\varphi_{r}^{[\varepsilon]},0)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{\varepsilon}})\times C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{\varepsilon+1}})
such that, if
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{\varepsilon+1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[r]},g\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[r]}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r]}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}\big).

Note that in the case
r
even we have the more precise Conjecture
10.3.1
, in that we can give
\varphi^{\prime}
explicitly. However, when
r
is even and
2\leq r\leq n
, there is the following refinement of Conjecture
10.3.1
, in which we cannot give the test function explicitly.

We recall from §
3.10
that, when
r
is even and
2\leq r\leq n
, the space
{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{[r]}_{n}}
has two closed formal schemes,
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{n}^{[r],+}=\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0,r]}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{n}^{[r],-}
(see (
3.10.4
) and the paragraph after it).
As the notation suggests, the generic fiber of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{n}^{[r],+}
is the member of the RZ tower associated with the compact open subgroup
K_{n+1}^{[r],+}=\mathrm{U}(\Lambda^{+})
. To be parallel to the formulation of Conjecture
13.1.1
,
we introduce the volume constants analogous to (
6.1.11
),

(13.2.1)

c_{r}^{\pm}:=\vol(K_{n}\cap K^{[r],\pm}_{n+1})^{-1}=[K_{n}:K_{n}\cap K^{[r],\pm}_{n+1}].

We then have the following AT conjectures for the plus space and the minus space and the mixed case respectively. We know nothing about them at this moment.

Conjecture 13.2.2
.

Let
r
be even such that
2\leq r\leq n
.

(i)

There exists
\varphi^{\prime}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
with transfer
((c_{r}^{+})^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[0]}\times K_{n+1}^{[r],+}},0)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{0}})\times C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{1}})
such that, if
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[r],+},g\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[r],+}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r]}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}\big).

(ii)

There exists
\varphi^{\prime}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
with transfer
((c_{r}^{-})^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[0]}\times K_{n+1}^{[r],-}},0)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{0}})\times C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{1}})
such that, if
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[r],-},g\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[r],-}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r]}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}\big).

(iii)

There exists
\varphi^{\prime}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G^{\prime})
with transfer
(c_{r}^{+}c_{r}^{-}\,{\bf 1}_{K_{n}^{[0]}\times hK_{n+1}^{[r],+}},0)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{0}})\times C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{W_{1}})
such that, if
\gamma\in G^{\prime}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
is matched with
g\in G_{W_{1}}(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then

\left\langle\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[r],+},g\widetilde{}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{[r],-}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[r]}}\cdot\log q=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\partial Orb}\big(\gamma,\varphi^{\prime}\big).

Part II
The geometry of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}

In this part we specialize the situation of §
3
to
r=1
. Our aim is to prove Theorem
1.0.1
from the Introduction. In §
14.1
and §
14.2
, we give a description of the
\bar{k}
-points of
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}
and its Bruhat-Tits stratification (using Dieudonné theory) and determine the singularities (using the theory of local models).
In §
14.3
and §
14.4
, we do the same for
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n+1}
. In §
14.5
, we describe the singularities of the special cycle
\mathcal{Z}(u)
. In §
14.6
, we formulate the main result Theorem
14.6.2
on the structure of
\widetilde{}\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1]}
. The remaining sections §
15
and §
16
are devoted to the proofs of Theorem
14.6.2
and of Conjecture
3.5.1
.

Write
\mathbb{V}=\mathbb{V}_{n+1}
and
\mathbb{W}=\mathbb{W}_{n}^{[1]}
for short.

14.
The space
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}

14.1.
\bar{k}
-points of
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}

Let
(Y,\iota,\lambda,\rho)\in\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}(\bar{k})
. Let
\mathbb{D}(Y)
be the (relative) Dieudonné crystal of
Y
. The (relative) Dieudonné module
\mathbb{D}(Y)({O_{\breve{F}}})
is a free
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-module of rank
2n
, equipped with the action of the
\sigma
-linear Frobenius
\mathbf{F}
and the
\sigma^{-1}
-linear Verschiebung
\mathbf{V}
. The almost principal polarization
\lambda
induces a non-perfect alternating
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-bilinear form on the Dieudonné module

\langle\ ,\ \rangle:\mathbb{D}(Y)({O_{\breve{F}}})\times\mathbb{D}(Y)({O_{\breve{F}}})\rightarrow{O_{\breve{F}}}.

It satisfies
\langle\mathbf{F}x,y\rangle=\langle x,\mathbf{V}y\rangle^{\sigma}
for any
x,y\in\mathbb{D}(Y)({O_{\breve{F}}})
. The
O_{F}
-action
\iota
induces a
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
-grading

\mathbb{D}(Y)({O_{\breve{F}}})=\mathbb{D}(Y)({O_{\breve{F}}})_{0}\oplus\mathbb{D}(Y)({O_{\breve{F}}})_{1},

where each
\mathbb{D}(Y)({O_{\breve{F}}})_{i}
is a free
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-module of rank
n
. Then
\mathbf{F}
(resp.
\mathbf{V}
) is of degree 1 with respect this
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
-grading. The compatibility of
\iota
with the polarization
\lambda
gives an
O_{F}
-action on
\mathbb{D}(Y)({O_{\breve{F}}})
commuting with
\mathbf{F},\mathbf{V}
such that
\langle\iota(a)x,y\rangle=\langle x,\iota(\sigma(a))y\rangle
for any
x,y\in\mathbb{D}(Y)({O_{\breve{F}}})
and
a\in O_{F}
.

Let
\tau=\mathbf{V}^{-1}\mathbf{F}
, a
\sigma^{2}
-linear operator on the
{\breve{F}}
-isocrystal
\mathbb{D}(Y)({O_{\breve{F}}})\otimes{{\breve{F}}}
which is of degree 0 with respect to the
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
-grading. The space of
\tau
-invariants
C(Y):=(\mathbb{D}(Y)({O_{\breve{F}}})_{0}\otimes{\breve{F}})^{\tau=1}
is a
F
-vector space of dimension
n
. Define a pairing on the
{\breve{F}}
-isocrystal

(\ ,\ ):\mathbb{D}(Y)({O_{\breve{F}}})\otimes{\breve{F}}\times\mathbb{D}(Y)({O_{\breve{F}}})\otimes{\breve{F}}\rightarrow{\breve{F}},\quad(x,y):=(\varpi\delta)^{-1}\langle x,\mathbf{F}y\rangle.

It satisfies

(14.1.1)

(x,y)=(y,\tau^{-1}(x))^{\sigma}

and so
(\ ,\ )
restricts to an
F/F_{0}
-hermitian form on
C(Y)
. Via the quasi-isogeny
\rho
we may identify
C(Y)
with the hermitian space
C(\mathbb{Y})
, which we further identify with the hermitian space
\mathbb{W}
, cf.
[
10
, Lem. 3.9]
.

For any
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-lattice
\mathsf{A}\subseteq\mathbb{W}_{{\breve{F}}}\simeq C(Y)_{{\breve{F}}}
of rank
n
, define the dual lattice

\mathsf{A}^{\vee}:=\{x\in\mathbb{W}_{{\breve{F}}}:(x,\mathsf{A})\subseteq{O_{\breve{F}}}\}.

Then by (
14.1.1
) we have

(\mathsf{A}^{\vee})^{\vee}=\tau(A).

Definition 14.1.1
.

A pair
(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B})
of
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-lattices
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}\subseteq\mathbb{W}_{{\breve{F}}}
of rank
n
is
special
if

\mathsf{B}^{\vee}\subseteq^{1}\mathsf{A}\subseteq^{1}\mathsf{B},\quad\mathsf{B}^{\vee}\subseteq^{1}\mathsf{A}^{\vee}\subseteq^{1}\mathsf{B}.

By
[
3
, Prop. 2.4,
h=1
]
3
3

3

Note that the dual lattice in
[
3
]
is taken with respect to the form
\{\ ,\ \}=\varpi\delta(\ ,\ )
and thus
\varpi\mathsf{A}^{\vee}
(resp.
\varpi\mathsf{B}^{\vee}
) in
[
3
]
is our
\mathsf{A}^{\vee}
(resp.
\mathsf{B}^{\vee}
).
, the pair
(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B})
of
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-lattices of
\mathbb{W}_{{\breve{F}}}
given by

\mathsf{A}:=\mathbb{D}(Y)({O_{\breve{F}}})_{0},\quad\mathsf{B}:=(\mathbf{V}(\mathbb{D}(Y)({O_{\breve{F}}})_{1}))^{\vee}\cong\mathbb{D}(Y^{\vee})({O_{\breve{F}}})_{0}

is special, and the association
(Y,\iota,\lambda,\rho)(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B})
gives a bijection

\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}(\bar{k})\simeq\{(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B})\text{ special}:\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}\subseteq\mathbb{W}_{{\breve{F}}}\}.

14.2.
Bruhat-Tits stratification of
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}

By
[
3
, Thm. 1.1]
, we have a
Bruhat-Tits stratification
with closed strata

\mathcal{N}^{[1],\mathrm{red}}_{n}=\bigcup_{\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}(\mathbb{W})}\mathcal{V}(\Lambda).

\bullet

For
\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})
, by
[
3
, Rem. 2.15]
we have

\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)(\bar{k})=\{(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B})\text{ special}:\mathsf{A}=\Lambda_{{O_{\breve{F}}}}\}=\{(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B})\text{ special}:\mathsf{A}=\mathsf{A}^{\vee}\}.

In this case
\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)\simeq\mathbb{P}^{n-1}=\mathbb{P}(\Lambda_{\bar{k}})=\mathbb{P}(\varpi^{-1}\mathsf{A}/\mathsf{A})
, where
(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B})
corresponds to the line given by the image of
\mathsf{B}
in
\varpi^{-1}\mathsf{A}/\mathsf{A}
. Moreover
\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)
’s for
\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})
are all disjoint.

\bullet

For
\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{t}(\mathbb{W})
(
2\leq t\leq n
, necessarily even), by
[
3
, Def. 2.9 (2)]
we have

\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)(\bar{k})=\{(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B})\text{ special}:\Lambda\subseteq\mathsf{B}^{\vee}\},

and by
[
3
, Prop. 3.9 (1)]
,
\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)
is a closed generalized Deligne–Lusztig variety for the finite even unitary group
\mathrm{U}(\Lambda^{\vee}/\Lambda)
(for the hermitian form induced by
(\,,\,)
), which has dimension
t/2
. It has an open Deligne–Lusztig subvariety
\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)^{\circ}\subseteq\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)
with

\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)^{\circ}(\bar{k})=\{(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B})\text{ special}:\Lambda\subseteq\mathsf{B}^{\vee},\mathsf{A}\neq\mathsf{A}^{\vee}\}.

In particular, for
(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B})\in\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)^{\circ}(\bar{k})
, we have
\mathsf{B}^{\vee}=\mathsf{A}\cap\mathsf{A}^{\vee}
and thus
\mathsf{B}
is uniquely determined by
\mathsf{A}
. For
\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{2}(\mathbb{W})
, we have
\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)\simeq\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

\bullet

For
\Lambda_{0}\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})
and
\Lambda_{t}\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{t}(\mathbb{W})
with
t\geq 2
, by
[
3
, Prop. 2.18]
we have
\mathcal{V}(\Lambda_{0})\cap\mathcal{V}(\Lambda_{t})\neq\varnothing
if and only if
\Lambda_{t}\subseteq\Lambda_{0}
. In this case
[
3
, Rem. 2.19]
we have an isomorphism

\mathcal{V}(\Lambda_{0})\cap\mathcal{V}(\Lambda_{t})\simeq\mathbb{P}(\Lambda_{t}^{\vee}/\Lambda_{0})\simeq\mathbb{P}^{t/2-1},

which at the level of
\bar{k}
-points is given by

(14.2.1)

\mathcal{V}(\Lambda_{0})(\bar{k})\cap\mathcal{V}(\Lambda_{t})(\bar{k})=\{(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B})\text{ special}:\mathsf{A}=\Lambda_{0,{O_{\breve{F}}}},\ \mathsf{A}\subseteq^{1}\mathsf{B}\subseteq\Lambda_{t,{O_{\breve{F}}}}^{\vee}\}=\mathbb{P}(\Lambda_{t}^{\vee}/\Lambda_{0})(\bar{k}).

In particular, when
t=2
, we have

\mathcal{V}(\Lambda_{0})(\bar{k})\cap\mathcal{V}(\Lambda_{2})(\bar{k})=\{(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B})=(\Lambda_{0,{O_{\breve{F}}}},\Lambda_{2,{O_{\breve{F}}}}^{\vee})\}

consisting of a single
\bar{k}
-point.

\bullet

For
\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{t}(\mathbb{W})
with
t\geq 2
, by (
14.2.1
) we have

\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)^{\circ}=\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)\setminus\bigcup_{\Lambda^{\prime}\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})}(\mathcal{V}(\Lambda^{\prime})\cap\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)).

Figure 2.
\mathcal{N}_{4}^{[1]}

Definition 14.2.1
.

For
\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})
, define

F_{\Lambda}:=\{\ell\in\mathbb{P}(\Lambda_{\bar{k}}):\ell\subseteq\ell^{\perp}\}\subseteq\mathcal{V}(\Lambda),

a Fermat hypersurface of degree
q+1
, comp.
[
25
, Ex. 5.6]
. By (
14.2.1
) we know that for
\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})
,

\bigcup_{\Lambda^{\prime}\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{\geq 2}(\mathbb{W})}(\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)\cap\mathcal{\mathcal{V}}(\Lambda^{\prime}))\subset F_{\Lambda}.

The following terminology is due to Kudla
[
11
]
.

Definition 14.2.2
.

The
closed balloon locus
of
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}
is the reduced closed subscheme of
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}\otimes\bar{k}
given by

\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}:=\bigsqcup_{\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})}\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)\simeq\bigsqcup_{\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})}\mathbb{P}^{n-1}.

Define the
link stratum
to be

\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\dagger}=\bigsqcup_{\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})}F_{\Lambda},

a
(n-2)
-dimensional reduced closed subscheme of
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}
. Define the open
balloon stratum
to be

\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\circ}:=\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}\setminus\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\dagger},

a reduced locally closed subscheme of
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}
. Define the
non-special locus
to be

\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],{\mathrm{ns}}}:=\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}\setminus\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet},

an open formal subscheme of
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}
. Note that

\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\circ}=\bigsqcup_{\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})}(\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)\setminus F_{\Lambda}),\quad(\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],{\mathrm{ns}}})_{\mathrm{red}}=\bigcup_{\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{\geq 2}(\mathbb{W})}\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)^{\circ}.

Remark 14.2.3
.

We emphasize that the loci
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}
,
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\dagger}
and
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\circ}
are schemes contained in the underlying reduced scheme of
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}
. In
[
30
, §5.3]
, Z. Zhang represents the special fiber
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}\otimes_{O_{\breve{F}}}\bar{k}
as the union of two formal Cartier divisors
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}
and
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\circ}
of
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}
and introduces their intersection
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\dagger}
. The dictionary between his definitions and ours is as follows: his balloon stratum
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\circ}
is a scheme and equals our
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}
; his ground stratum
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}
is not a scheme but has as underlying reduced scheme the union of
\bigcup_{\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{\geq 2}(\mathbb{W})}\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)^{\circ}
and
\bigsqcup_{\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})}F_{\Lambda}
; his link stratum
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\dagger}
is a scheme and coincides with our
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\dagger}
.

Proposition 14.2.4

(Singularities of
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}
)
.

The complete local ring of
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}
at
z\in\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}(\bar{k})
is isomorphic to
{O_{\breve{F}}}[[X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}]]/(X_{1}X_{2}-\varpi)
if
z\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\dagger}(\bar{k})
, resp. to
{O_{\breve{F}}}[[X_{1},\ldots,X_{n-1}]]
if
z\not\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\dagger}(\bar{k})
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{loc}}
be the standard Drinfeld local model over
\Spec{O_{\breve{F}}}
for the periodic lattice chain associated to two adjacent lattices: for an
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-scheme
S
,
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{loc}}(S)
is the set of isomorphism classes of commutative diagrams of locally free
O_{S}
-modules

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 12.49377pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.49377pt\raise
