97.9751pt\raise-8.3611pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where

(i)

\Lambda_{0}=\langle e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}\rangle
,
\Lambda_{1}=\langle\varpi^{-1}e_{1},e_{2},\ldots e_{n}\rangle
are free
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-modules of rank
n
,

(ii)

\phi_{0}=\mathrm{id}
,
\phi_{1}=\varpi
,

(iii)

\mathcal{F}_{i}
are locally free
O_{S}
-submodules of rank
n-1
which Zariski-locally on
S
are direct summands of
\Lambda_{i,S}
.

For
z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{k})
, the complete local ring
\hat{O}_{z^{\prime}}
is isomorphic to
{O_{\breve{F}}}[[X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}]]/(X_{1}X_{2}-\varpi)
if
e_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{0}
and
\Lambda_{1,\bar{k}}/\mathcal{F}_{1}
is generated by
\varpi^{-1}e_{1}
, and is isomorphic to
{O_{\breve{F}}}[[X_{1},\ldots,X_{n-1}]]
otherwise (
[
4
, Thm. 4.12, §4.4.5 with
\kappa=1
,
r=n-1
]
). The local model of
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}
over
{O_{\breve{F}}}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{loc}}
(
[
19
, §5]
).

Let
z\in\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}(\bar{k})
. The corresponding
\bar{k}
-point
z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\bar{k})
with isomorphic complete local ring
\hat{O}_{z}\simeq\hat{O}_{z^{\prime}}
is given by the diagram

\textstyle{\mathbb{D}(X_{z})(\bar{k})_{0}=\mathsf{A}/\varpi\mathsf{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{0}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{D}(X_{z}^{\vee})(\bar{k})_{0}=\mathsf{B}/\varpi\mathsf{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{A}/\varpi\mathsf{A}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{V}(\mathbb{D}(X_{z})(\bar{k})_{1})=\tau^{-1}(\mathsf{B}^{\vee})/\varpi\mathsf{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{V}(\mathbb{D}(X_{z}^{\vee})(\bar{k})_{1})=\tau^{-1}(\mathsf{A}^{\vee})/\varpi\mathsf{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau^{-1}(\mathsf{B}^{\vee})/\varpi\mathsf{A}.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

At a singular point
z
(equivalently at a singular point
z^{\prime}
), the condition that
\Lambda_{1,\bar{k}}/\mathcal{F}_{1}
is generated by
\varpi^{-1}e_{1}
becomes the condition that
\mathsf{B}/\tau^{-1}(\mathsf{A}^{\vee})
is generated by
\varpi^{-1}e_{1}
, and the condition
e_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{0}
becomes the condition that
\tau^{-1}(\mathsf{B}^{\vee})/\varpi\mathsf{A}
contains
e_{1}
. Since
\mathsf{B}/\mathsf{A}
is generated by
\varpi^{-1}e_{1}
, we know that
\tau^{-1}(\mathsf{A}^{\vee})=\mathsf{A}
, and hence
\tau(\mathsf{A})=\mathsf{A}=\Lambda_{{O_{\breve{F}}}}
for some
\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})
. Moreover, as
e_{1}\in\tau^{-1}(\mathsf{B}^{\vee})/\varpi\mathsf{A}
, the
\bar{k}
-line
\langle e_{1}\rangle=\varpi\mathsf{B}/\varpi\mathsf{A}\subseteq\Lambda_{\bar{k}}
satisfies
\langle e_{1}\rangle\subseteq\langle e_{1}\rangle^{\perp}
, and hence corresponds to a point on the Fermat hypersurface
F_{\Lambda}
. Hence
\hat{O}_{z}\simeq{O_{\breve{F}}}[[X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}]]/(X_{1}X_{2}-\varpi)
exactly when
z\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\dagger}(\bar{k})
.
∎

14.3.
\bar{k}
-points of
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[1]}

Let
(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)\in\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[1]}(\bar{k})
. The (relative) Dieudonné module
\mathbb{D}(X)({O_{\breve{F}}})
is a free
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-module of rank
2(n+1)
, equipped with the action of the
\sigma
-linear Frobenius
\mathbf{F}
and the
\sigma^{-1}
-linear Verschiebung
\mathbf{V}
. The principal polarization
\lambda
induces a perfect alternating
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-bilinear form on the Dieudonné module

\langle\ ,\ \rangle:\mathbb{D}(X)({O_{\breve{F}}})\times\mathbb{D}(X)({O_{\breve{F}}})\rightarrow{O_{\breve{F}}}.

It satisfies
\langle\mathbf{F}x,y\rangle=\langle x,\mathbf{V}y\rangle^{\sigma}
for any
x,y\in\mathbb{D}(X)({O_{\breve{F}}})
. The
O_{F}
-action
\iota
induces a
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
-grading

\mathbb{D}(X)({O_{\breve{F}}})=\mathbb{D}(X)({O_{\breve{F}}})_{0}\oplus\mathbb{D}(X)({O_{\breve{F}}})_{1},

where each
\mathbb{D}(X)({O_{\breve{F}}})_{i}
is a free
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-module of rank
n
. Then
\mathbf{F}
(resp.
\mathbf{V}
) is of degree 1 with respect this
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
-grading. The compatibility of
\iota
with the polarization
\lambda
gives an
O_{F}
-action on
\mathbb{D}(X)({O_{\breve{F}}})
commuting with
\mathbf{F},\mathbf{V}
such that
\langle\iota(a)x,y\rangle=\langle x,\iota(\sigma(a))y\rangle
for any
x,y\in\mathbb{D}(X)({O_{\breve{F}}})
and
a\in O_{F}
.

Let
\tau=\mathbf{V}^{-1}\mathbf{F}
, a
\sigma^{2}
-linear operator on the
{\breve{F}}
-isocrystal
\mathbb{D}(X)({O_{\breve{F}}})\otimes{{\breve{F}}}
which is of degree 0 with respect to the
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
-grading. The space of
\tau
-invariants
C(X):=(\mathbb{D}(X)({O_{\breve{F}}})_{0}\otimes{\breve{F}})^{\tau=1}
is a
F
-vector space of dimension
n
. Define a pairing on the
{\breve{F}}
-isocrystal

(\ ,\ ):\mathbb{D}(X)({O_{\breve{F}}})\otimes{\breve{F}}\times\mathbb{D}(X)({O_{\breve{F}}})\otimes{\breve{F}}\rightarrow{\breve{F}},\quad(x,y):=(\varpi\delta)^{-1}\langle x,\mathbf{F}y\rangle.

It satisfies

(14.3.1)

(x,y)=(y,\tau^{-1}(x))^{\sigma}

and so
(\ ,\ )
restricts to an
F/F_{0}
-hermitian form on
C(X)
. Via the quasi-isogeny
\rho
we may identify
C(X)
with the hermitian space
C(\mathbb{X})
, which we further identify with the hermitian space
\mathbb{V}
, cf.
[
10
, Lemma 3.9]
.

For any
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-lattice
A\subseteq\mathbb{W}_{{\breve{F}}}\simeq C(X)_{{\breve{F}}}
of rank
n
, define the dual lattice

A^{\vee}:=\{x\in\mathbb{W}_{{\breve{F}}}:(x,A)\subseteq{O_{\breve{F}}}\}.

Then by (
14.3.1
) we have

(A^{\vee})^{\vee}=\tau(A).

Definition 14.3.1
.

An
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-lattice
A\subseteq\mathbb{V}_{{\breve{F}}}
of rank
n+1
is
special
if

\varpi A\subseteq^{n}A^{\vee}\subseteq^{1}A.

For
(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)\in\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[1]}(\bar{k})
, by
[
24
, Prop. 1.10]
the
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-lattice

A:=\mathbb{D}(X)({O_{\breve{F}}})_{0}\subseteq\mathbb{V}_{{\breve{F}}}

is special, and the association
(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)A
gives a bijection

(14.3.2)

\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[1]}(\bar{k})\simeq\{\text{special lattices }A\subseteq\mathbb{V}_{{\breve{F}}}\}.

14.4.
Bruhat–Tits stratification of
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[1]}

By
[
25
, Thm. B]
, we have a
Bruhat–Tits stratification
of
\mathcal{N}^{[1],\mathrm{red}}
with closed strata

\mathcal{N}^{[1],\mathrm{red}}=\bigcup_{\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}(\mathbb{V})}\mathcal{V}(\Lambda).

Each closed Bruhat–Tits stratum
\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)
is a generalized Deligne–Lusztig variety associated to the finite odd unitary group
\mathrm{U}(\Lambda^{\vee}/\Lambda)
, which has dimension
(t(\Lambda)-1)/2
. It has
\bar{k}
-points

\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)(\bar{k})=\{A\text{ special}:\Lambda\subseteq A^{\vee}\}.

In particular, for
\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{1}(\mathbb{V})
, we have
A\in\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)(\bar{k})
if and only if

A^{\vee}=\Lambda_{{O_{\breve{F}}}}
.

Definition 14.4.1
.

Define the
superspecial locus

\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{\mathrm{ss}}
of
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[1]}
to be

\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{\mathrm{ss}}:=\bigsqcup_{\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{1}(\mathbb{V})}\mathcal{V}(\Lambda),

a closed reduced subscheme of dimension zero of
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[1]}
.

14.5.
The special divisor
\mathcal{Z}(u)
on
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[1]}

Let
\mathcal{Z}(u)\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[1]}
be the special divisor considered in §
3.8
. The bijection (
14.3.2
) restricts to a bijection (see
[
10
, Prop. 3.1]
)

\mathcal{Z}({u})(\bar{k})\simeq\{\text{special lattices }A\subseteq\mathbb{V}_{{\breve{F}}}:{u}\in A^{\vee}\}.

Under the identification
\mathbb{W}=\langle u\rangle^{\perp}
, each vertex lattice
\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{t}(\mathbb{W})
gives rise to a vertex lattice

\Lambda({u}):=\Lambda\obot\langle{u}\rangle\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{t+1}(\mathbb{V}).

Definition 14.5.1
.

Define the
center point locus
of
\mathcal{Z}(u)
to be

\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{cent}}:=\bigsqcup_{\Lambda\subseteq{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})}\mathcal{V}(\Lambda({u})),

a 0-dimensional closed reduced subscheme of
\mathcal{Z}(u)
. In particular,
\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{cent}}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}(u)\cap\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{\mathrm{ss}}
. Note that
z\in\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{cent}}
if and only if
A^{\vee}=\Lambda({u})_{{O_{\breve{F}}}}
, for some
\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})
.

Define the
non-special locus

\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{ns}}:=\mathcal{Z}(u)\setminus\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{cent}},

an open formal subscheme of
\mathcal{Z}(u)
.

The proof of the following theorem is given in §
15.2
.

Theorem 14.5.2
.

The formal scheme
\mathcal{Z}(u)
is regular of dimension
n
, and formally smooth over
\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
outside the zero-dimensional closed subset
\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{cent}}
of
\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\rm red}
.

14.6.
Geometry of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}

We first introduce the following loci in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
.

Definition 14.6.1
.

Define the
exceptional locus

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[1],{\rm exc}}:=\pi_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{cent}})
, a closed formal subscheme of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
. Define the
non-special locus

{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1],\mathrm{ns}}_{n}}:={\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}\setminus\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[1],{\rm exc}}
, an open formal subscheme of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
.

Recall the projection morphisms
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}
from (
3.8.2
). The following Theorem was conjectured in
[
11
]
. The proof will occupy us in the next sections. The end of the proof is in §
16.5
.

Theorem 14.6.2

(Geometry of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
)
.

(i)

The formal scheme
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
is regular of dimension
n
.

(ii)

The morphism
\pi_{1}
is finite flat of degree
q+1
, étale away from
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}
, and totally ramified along
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}
.

(iii)

The morphism
\pi_{2}
is proper. Its restriction to
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1],\mathrm{ns}}_{n}}
induces an isomorphism
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1],\mathrm{ns}}_{n}}\simeq\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{ns}}
.

(iv)

The closed formal subscheme
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[1],{\rm exc}}
of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
is a reduced Cartier divisor and isomorphic to
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}
under
\pi_{1}
. In particular, for
\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})
,
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}:=\pi_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{V}(\Lambda({u})))
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)\simeq\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
under
\pi_{1}
and we have a decomposition

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[1],{\rm exc}}=\bigsqcup_{\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})}\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}.

(v)

For
\Lambda_{1},\cdots,\Lambda_{n}\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})
, we have

(14.6.1)

\displaystyle\chi\bigl({\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}},\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{1}}\cap^{\mathbb{L}}\cdots\cap^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{n}}\bigr)=\begin{cases}(-1)^{n-1},&\Lambda_{1}=\cdots=\Lambda_{n},\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Example 14.6.3
.

When
n=2
, we have

•

\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}
is isomorphic to the Drinfeld half plane, whose special fiber is a union of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
’s with dual graph a
(q+1)
-valent tree. The
\mathbb{P}^{1}
’s are either of the form
\mathcal{V}(\Lambda_{0})
for a
\Lambda_{0}\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})
or of the form
\mathcal{V}(\Lambda_{2})
for a
\Lambda_{2}\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{2}(\mathbb{W})
.

•

\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}
consists of the
\mathcal{V}(\Lambda_{0})
for a
\Lambda_{0}\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})
, with nonreduced preimage
\pi_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{V}(\Lambda_{0}))
(a “fat”
\mathbb{P}^{1}
with multiplicity
q+1
). The preimage
\pi_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{V}(\Lambda_{2}))
of
\mathcal{V}(\Lambda_{2})
is a Fermat curve of degree
q+1
.

•

The special fiber of
\mathcal{Z}(u)
consists of Fermat curves of degree
q+1
intersecting at points in
\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{cent}}
. Each Fermat curve contains
q+1
intersection points and
q+1
Fermat curves pass through each intersection point.

•

The preimage of
z=\mathcal{V}(\Lambda_{0}({u}))\in\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{cent}}
under
\pi_{2}
is an exceptional divisor
\pi_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{V}(\Lambda_{0}))^{\mathrm{red}}\simeq\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

Figure
1
in the Introduction illustrates the morphisms
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
in (
3.8.2
) (for
n=2
and
r=1
) on the special fibers locally around a superspecial point of
\mathcal{Z}(u)
.

14.7.
The morphisms
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}
on
\bar{k}
-points

As a first step towards proving Theorem
14.6.2
, we study the properties of
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
at the level of
\bar{k}
-points.

Lemma 14.7.1
.

The following assertions hold.

(i)

\pi_{2}
induces a bijection
\pi_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],{\mathrm{ns}}})(\bar{k})\simeq\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{ns}}(\bar{k})
.

(ii)

\pi_{2}
maps
\pi_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet})(\bar{k})
onto
\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{cent}}(\bar{k})
with fibers isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}(\bar{k})
.

(iii)

\pi_{1}
induces a bijection
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[1],{\rm exc}}(\bar{k})\simeq\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}(\bar{k})
.

(iv)

\pi_{1}
maps
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1],\mathrm{ns}}_{n}}(\bar{k})
onto
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],{\mathrm{ns}}}(\bar{k})
with fibers of size
q+1
.

Proof.

Let
z=((\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}),A)\in(\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}\times\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[1]})(\bar{k})
. Then
z\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}(\bar{k})
if and only if

\mathsf{A}({u})\subseteq^{1}A,\quad\mathsf{B}^{\vee}({u})\subseteq^{1}A^{\vee}.

In this case, we have
\mathsf{A}=A\cap\mathbb{W}_{\breve{F}}
and
\mathsf{B}^{\vee}=A^{\vee}\cap\mathbb{W}_{\breve{F}}
. For
z\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}(\bar{k})
, by definition we have
z\in\pi_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],{\mathrm{ns}}})
if and only if
\mathsf{A}\neq\mathsf{A}^{\vee}
.

On the other hand, we have

\mathcal{Z}(u)(\bar{k})=\{A\text{ special}:{u}\in A^{\vee}\},

and in this case by definition
A\in\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{ns}}(\bar{k})
if and only if
A^{\vee}\cap\mathbb{W}_{\breve{F}}\text{ does not contain any }\Lambda_{0}\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})
, if and only if
A\cap\mathbb{W}_{\breve{F}}
is not
\tau
-invariant.

(i)

Let
z=((\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}),A)\in\pi_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],{\mathrm{ns}}})(\bar{k})
. Then
A\cap\mathbb{W}_{{\breve{F}}}=\mathsf{A}
satisfies
\mathsf{A}\neq\mathsf{A}^{\vee}
, hence
A\cap\mathbb{W}_{{\breve{F}}}
is not
\tau
-invariant and thus
\pi_{2}(z)\in\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{ns}}(\bar{k})
. Conversely, let
A\in\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{ns}}(\bar{k})
. Then
A\cap\mathbb{W}_{\breve{F}}
is not
\tau
-invariant. Let
\mathsf{A}=A\cap\mathbb{W}_{{\breve{F}}}
. Then
\mathsf{A}\neq\mathsf{A}^{\vee}
and hence determines a unique
z=((\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}),A)\in\pi_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],{\mathrm{ns}}})(\bar{k})
such that
\pi_{2}(z)=A
.

(ii)

If
z\in\pi_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet})(\bar{k})
, then
\mathsf{A}=\mathsf{A}^{\vee}=\Lambda_{{O_{\breve{F}}}}
for some
\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})
. Hence
\mathsf{A}({u})=\Lambda_{{O_{\breve{F}}}}({u})\subseteq^{1}A
and
A^{\vee}\subseteq^{1}A
implies that
A^{\vee}=\Lambda_{{O_{\breve{F}}}}({u})
and thus
\pi_{2}(z)=A\in\mathcal{V}(\Lambda({u}))(\bar{k})\subseteq\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{cent}}(\bar{k})
. Conversely, for a fixed
A\in\mathcal{V}(\Lambda({u}))(\bar{k})
, we have
\pi_{2}(z)=A
if and only if
A^{\vee}=\Lambda_{{O_{\breve{F}}}}({u})
, if and only if
\mathsf{A}=\Lambda_{{O_{\breve{F}}}}
, and thus
\pi_{2}^{-1}(A)(\bar{k})\simeq\{\mathsf{B}:\mathsf{A}\subseteq^{1}\mathsf{B}\}=\mathbb{P}(\Lambda_{\bar{k}})(\bar{k})
.

(iii)

This follows from the description of the fibers of
\pi_{2}
in (ii).

(iv)

Let
(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B})\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],{\mathrm{ns}}}(\bar{k})
. Then
\mathsf{A}\neq\mathsf{A}^{\vee}
and
\mathsf{B}^{\vee}=\mathsf{A}\cap\mathsf{A}^{\vee}
. We have
\pi_{1}(z)=(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B})
if and only if
\mathsf{B}^{\vee}({u})\subseteq A^{\vee}
. Since

\mathsf{B}^{\vee}({u})\subseteq^{1}A^{\vee}\subseteq^{1}A\subseteq^{1}\mathsf{B}(\varpi^{-1}{u}),\quad\mathsf{B}^{\vee}({u})\subseteq^{1}\mathsf{A}({u})\subseteq^{1}A\subseteq^{1}\mathsf{B}(\varpi^{-1}{u}),

we know that the choices of
A
such that
z=((\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}),A)\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
are in bijection with isotropic lines
\ell:=A^{\vee}/\mathsf{B}^{\vee}({u})
in the 3-dimensional space
\mathsf{B}(\varpi^{-1}{u})/\mathsf{B}^{\vee}({u})
such that
\ell
is orthogonal to the anisotropic line
\ell^{\prime}:=\mathsf{A}({u})/\mathsf{B}^{\vee}({u})
. Hence the number of choices of
A
is equal to the number of isotropic lines
\ell
in the 2-dimensional space
(\ell^{\prime})^{\perp}
, which is equal to
q+1
. ∎

15.
Deformation theory

In this section we collect necessary deformation theoretic facts needed for the study of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
. Let
R
be a local noetherian
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-algebra on which
\varpi
is nilpotent. Let
I\subseteq R
be an ideal such that
I^{2}=0
. Let
S=R/I
. Then
R
is a thickening of
S
, equipped with the trivial nilpotent divided power structure on
I
.

15.1.
The spaces
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}
and
\mathcal{Z}(u)

Let
(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)\in\mathcal{N}_{n+1}(S)
. Then
\mathbb{D}(X)(S)
is a free
S
-module of rank
2(n+1)
. We have the Hodge filtration
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(S)\subseteq\mathbb{D}(X)(S)
, a free
S
-module of rank
n+1
with free factor module. The polarization
\lambda
induces an
S
-alternating pairing on
\mathbb{D}(X)(S)
such that
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(S)
is totally isotropic.

Denote by
\{\iota_{F}^{i}:O_{F}\rightarrow{O_{\breve{F}}},i\in\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\}
the two conjugate embeddings that are the identity on
O_{F_{0}}
. Define
\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{i}\subseteq\mathbb{D}(X)(S)
to be the maximal
S
-submodule on which
\iota(O_{F})
acts via the the map
O_{F}\xrightarrow{\iota_{F}^{i}}{O_{\breve{F}}}\rightarrow S
, a free
S
-module of rank
n+1
. We have a Hodge exact sequence of free
S
-modules

0\rightarrow\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{i}\rightarrow\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{i}\rightarrow\Lie X(S)_{i}\rightarrow 0.

By the signature condition we know that
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{i}
has rank
n
for
i=0
and rank 1 for
i=1
. The principal polarization
\lambda
induces a perfect
S
-bilinear pairing

\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{i}:\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{i}\times\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{i+1}\rightarrow S,

which induces a perfect pairing

\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{i}\times\Lie X(S)_{i+1}\rightarrow S.

In particular,
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{0}
determines
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{1}
and vice versa.

Note that
\mathbb{D}(X)(R)
is a free
R
-module of rank
2(n+1)
and
\mathbb{D}(X)(S)=\mathbb{D}(X)(R)\otimes_{R}S
. By Grothendieck–Messing theory, a lifting of
(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)\in\mathcal{N}_{n+1}(S)
to
\mathcal{N}_{n+1}(R)
corresponds to a free
R
-module

\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0}\subseteq\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0}

lifting
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{0}
.

For
(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)\in\mathcal{Z}(u)(S)
, the special homomorphism
{u}\in\Hom_{O_{F}}(\overline{\mathcal{E}},X)
induces a homomorphism of free
R
-modules

{u}_{*}:\mathbb{D}(\overline{\mathcal{E}})(R)_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0},

which preserves the Hodge filtration when base changing to
S
,

{u}_{*}(\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(\overline{\mathcal{E}})(S)_{0})\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{0},

where the source and target have dimensions 1 and
n
respectively. The lifting
(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)\in\mathcal{N}_{n+1}(R)
lies in
\mathcal{Z}(u)(R)
if and only if
{u}_{*}
preserves the Hodge filtration:

{u}_{*}(\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(\overline{\mathcal{E}})(R)_{0})\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0}.

Lemma 15.1.1
.

Let
z\in\mathcal{Z}(u)(\bar{k})
. Then the dimension of the tangent space is given by

\dim_{\bar{k}}T_{z}\mathcal{Z}(u)_{\bar{k}}=\begin{cases}n-1,&z\in\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{ns}}(\bar{k}),\\
n,&z\in\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{cent}}(\bar{k}).\end{cases}

In particular,
\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{ns}}
is formally smooth over
\Spf{O_{\breve{F}}}
.

Proof.

Let
z\in\mathcal{Z}(u)(\bar{k})
. Let
\hat{O}_{z}
be the complete local ring of
\mathcal{Z}(u)
at
z
. Let
A\subseteq\mathbb{V}_{{\breve{F}}}
be the special lattice associated to
z
. Recall that the reduction
A_{\bar{k}}=A/\varpi A
is equipped with the Hodge filtration
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}A_{\bar{k}}\subseteq A_{\bar{k}}
, a hyperplane given by the image of
A^{\vee}
. By Grothendieck–Messing theory, for any local Artinian
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-algebra
R
such that the kernel
R\rightarrow\bar{k}
is equipped with a nilpotent divided power structure, the set
\Hom_{{O_{\breve{F}}}}(\hat{O}_{z},R)
is in bijection with
R
-hyperplanes
H_{R}\subseteq A_{R}
lifting
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}A_{\bar{k}}
such that
{u}\in H_{R}
. Let
\mathfrak{m}
be the maximal ideal of
R
.

Since
z\in\mathcal{Z}(u)(\bar{k})
, we know that
{u}\in A^{\vee}
. Since
A^{\vee}\subseteq A
and
{\mathrm{val}}({u})=1
, we know that
\varpi^{-1}{u}\not\in A^{\vee}
. We distinguish two cases.

(i)

If
\varpi^{-1}{u}\in A
, then we know that the line
A/A^{\vee}
is generated by the image of
\varpi^{-1}{u}
, and hence

A=\Lambda_{0}\obot\langle\varpi^{-1}{u}\rangle,\quad A^{\vee}=\Lambda_{0}\obot\langle{u}\rangle,

for
\Lambda_{0}\subseteq\mathbb{V}_{{\breve{F}}}
a self-dual lattice of rank
n
. By

A^{\vee}=\Lambda_{0}\obot\langle{u}\rangle\subseteq^{1}\tau(A)=\tau(\Lambda_{0})\obot\langle\varpi^{-1}{u}\rangle,

we obtain that
\Lambda_{0}=\tau(\Lambda_{0})
, so
A=\tau(A)
, and hence
z\in\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{ss}}
. In this case, we may extend
\varpi^{-1}{u}
to an
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-basis
\{e_{0}=\varpi^{-1}{u},e_{1},\ldots e_{n}\}
of
A
. The hyperplane
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}A_{\bar{k}}
is given by the equation

e_{0}^{*}=0.

Then a hyperplane
H_{R}\subseteq A_{R}
lifting
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}A_{\bar{k}}
is given by an equation of the form

(15.1.1)

e_{0}^{*}+X_{1}e_{1}^{*}\cdots+X_{n}e_{n}^{*}=0,\quad X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\in\mathfrak{m}.

Since
e_{0}^{*}({u})=e_{0}^{*}(\varpi e_{0})=\varpi
,
e_{i}^{*}({u})=0
(
i\geq 1
), the condition
{u}\in H_{R}
becomes

\varpi=0,

and it follows that

\Hom_{{O_{\breve{F}}}}(\hat{O}_{z},R)=\Hom_{O_{\breve{F}}}(\bar{k}[[X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}]],R).

(This is not enough information to determine
\hat{O}_{z}
since the kernel
R\rightarrow k
is required to have a nilpotent divided power structure). In particular, taking
R=k[\varepsilon]/\varepsilon^{2}
we obtain the tangent space of the special fiber has dimension

\dim T_{z}\mathcal{Z}(u)_{\bar{k}}=n.

(ii)

If
\varpi^{-1}{u}\not\in A
, then
z\not\in\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{cent}}
. We may extend
{u}
to an
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-basis
\{e_{0}={u},e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}\}
of
A
. After changing basis we may assume that the hyperplane
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}A_{\bar{k}}
is given by

e_{n}^{*}=0.

Then a hyperplane
H_{R}\subseteq A_{R}
lifting
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}A_{\bar{k}}
is given by an equation of the form

(15.1.2)

X_{0}e_{0}^{*}+X_{1}e_{1}^{*}\cdots+X_{n-1}e_{n-1}^{*}+e_{n}^{*}=0,\quad X_{0},\ldots,X_{n-1}\in\mathfrak{m}.

But now
e_{0}^{*}({u})=1
, so the condition
{u}\in H_{R}
becomes

X_{0}=0,

and it follows that

\Hom_{O_{\breve{F}}}(\hat{O}_{z},R)=\{(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n-1}),X_{i}\in\mathfrak{m}\}=\Hom_{O_{\breve{F}}}({O_{\breve{F}}}[[X_{1},\ldots,X_{n-1}]],R).

In particular, taking
R=k[\varepsilon]/\varepsilon^{2}
we obtain

\dim T_{z}\mathcal{Z}(u)_{\bar{k}}=n-1,

and hence the special fiber
\mathcal{Z}(u)_{\bar{k}}
is formally smooth at
z
and so
\hat{O}_{z}\simeq{O_{\breve{F}}}[[X_{1},\ldots,X_{n-1}]]
.∎

15.2.
Proof of Theorem
14.5.2

The regularity of
\mathcal{Z}(u)
follows from
[
23
]
, since
\mathcal{Z}(u)
is its own difference divisor. The second assertion follows from Lemma
15.1.1
and the fact that
\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{cent}}
is zero-dimensional, cf. Definition
14.5.1
.

15.3.
The space
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}

Let
(Y,\iota,\lambda,\rho)\in\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}(S)
. Then
\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)
is a free
S
-module of rank
2n
. We have a Hodge filtration
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)\subseteq\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)
, a free
S
-module of rank
n
. The polarization
\lambda
induces an
S
-alternating pairing on
\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)
such that
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)
is totally isotropic.

We have the Hodge exact sequence of free
S
-modules

0\rightarrow\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{i}\rightarrow\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{i}\rightarrow\Lie Y(S)_{i}\rightarrow 0.

By the signature condition we know that
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{i}
has rank
n-1
for
i=0
and rank 1 for
i=1
. The polarization
\lambda
induces an
S
-bilinear pairing

\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{i}:\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{i}\times\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{i+1}\rightarrow S.

When
\varpi=0
in
S
, let
\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{i}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{i+1}
be the orthogonal complement under
\langle\ ,\ \rangle
, which has rank 1 by the almost principal assumption on
\lambda
. Let
z=(Y,\iota,\lambda,\rho_{Y})\in\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}(S)
, then

(i)

z\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\circ}(S)
if and only if
\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}^{\perp}=\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{1}
and
\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{1}^{\perp}\not\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}
,

(ii)

z\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],{\mathrm{ns}}}(S)
if and only if
\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}^{\perp}\neq\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{1}
and
\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{1}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}
,

(iii)

z\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\dagger}(S)
if and only if
\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}^{\perp}=\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{1}
and
\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{1}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}
.

Note that
\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)
is a free
R
-module of rank
2n
and
\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)=\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)\otimes_{R}S
. By Grothendieck–Messing theory, a lifting of
(Y,\iota,\lambda,\rho_{Y})\in\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}(S)
to
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}(R)
corresponds to free
R
-modules

\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{i}\subseteq\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{i}

lifting
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{i}

i\in\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
, such that
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{0}
and
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{1}
are orthogonal under
\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{0}
.

15.4.
The space
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}

Let
(X,\iota_{X},\lambda_{X},\rho_{X},Y,\iota_{Y},\lambda_{Y},\rho_{Y})\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}(S)
. The isogeny
\widetilde{\alpha}:Y\times\overline{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow X
induces a homomorphism of free
R
-modules of rank
n+1

\alpha_{*}:\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{i}\oplus\mathbb{D}(\overline{\mathcal{E}})(R)_{i}\rightarrow\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{i},

whose cokernel is a free
R/\varpi R
-module of rank 1. The condition
\alpha^{*}\lambda_{X}=\lambda_{Y}\times\varpi\lambda_{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}
translates to the compatibility of
\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{i}
on
\mathbb{D}(Y\times\overline{\mathcal{E}})(R)
and
\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{i}
on
\mathbb{D}(X)(R)
under
\alpha_{*}
,

(15.4.1)

\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{i}}\oplus\varpi\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{\mathbb{D}(\overline{\mathcal{E}})(R)_{i}}=\langle\alpha_{*}(\ ),\alpha_{*}(\ )\rangle_{\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{i}}.

The homomorphism
\alpha_{*}
preserves Hodge filtrations when base changing to
S
:

\alpha_{*}(\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{i}\times\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(\overline{\mathcal{E}})(S)_{i})\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{i},

where both the source and target have rank
n
when
i=0
and rank 1 when
i=1
.

By Grothendieck–Messing theory, a lifting of
(X,\iota_{X},\lambda_{X},\rho_{X},Y,\iota_{Y},\lambda_{Y},\rho_{Y})\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}(S)
to
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}(R)
corresponds to liftings
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0}
,
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{i}
such that

(i)

\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{0}
and
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{1}
are orthogonal under
\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{0}
.

(ii)

\alpha_{*}
preserves Hodge filtrations:

\alpha_{*}(\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{i}\times\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(\overline{\mathcal{E}})(R)_{i})\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{i}.

Here
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{1}
is determined by
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0}
(see §
15.1
).

16.
Proof of Theorem
14.6.2
and Conjecture
3.5.1

16.1.
The exceptional divisor
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[1],{\rm exc}}
of
\pi_{2}

Proposition 16.1.1
.

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[1],{\rm exc}}
is a Cartier divisor in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
.

Proof.

We write
\mathcal{T}:=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[1],{\rm exc}}
for brevity. Let
z\in\mathcal{T}(\bar{k})
. Let
O_{z}
be the local ring of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
at
z
with maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}
. Let
J\subseteq O_{z}
be the ideal defining
\mathcal{T}
at
z
. Let
R=O_{z}/\mathfrak{m}J
and
I=J/\mathfrak{m}J
. Then
R
is a local noetherian
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-algebra on which
\varpi
is nilpotent and
I^{2}=0
. By Nakayama’s lemma, to show that
J
is principal it suffices to show that
I
is principal. It remains to show the following more general assertion: for any local noetherian
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-algebra
R
on which
\varpi
is nilpotent, a nonzero ideal
I\subseteq R
such that
I^{2}=0
and
S=R/I
, the condition that a lifting of
\tilde{z}\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}(R)
of
z\in\mathcal{T}(S)
lies in
\mathcal{T}(R)
is given by the vanishing of one nonzero element in
I
.

Let
z=(X,\iota_{X},\lambda_{X},\rho_{X},Y,\iota_{Y},\lambda_{Y},\rho_{Y})\in\mathcal{T}(S)
. Write
z_{i}=\pi_{i}(z)
. By the definition of
\mathcal{T}
, we know that
z_{2}=(X,\iota_{X},\lambda_{X},\rho_{X})\in\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{cent}}(S)
. Hence
\varpi=0
in
S
and
X=X_{\bar{k}}\times_{\bar{k}}S
for a unique
\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W})
and the unique point
(X_{\bar{k}},\iota_{X_{\bar{k}}},\lambda_{X_{\bar{k}}},\rho_{X_{\bar{k}}})\in\mathcal{V}(\Lambda({u}))(\bar{k})
. Therefore

\mathbb{D}(X)(R)=\mathbb{D}(X_{\bar{k}})({O_{\breve{F}}})\otimes_{{O_{\breve{F}}}}R,\quad\mathbb{D}(X)(S)=\mathbb{D}(X_{\bar{k}})({O_{\breve{F}}})\otimes_{O_{\breve{F}}}S=\mathbb{D}(X_{\bar{k}})(\bar{k})\otimes_{\bar{k}}S

and

\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{0}=\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(\bar{k})_{0}\otimes_{\bar{k}}S.

A lifting of
z\in\mathcal{T}(S)
to
\tilde{z}\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}(R)
corresponds to liftings
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0}
, resp.
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{i}
, of
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{0}
, resp.
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{i}
, as in §
15.4
. Note that
\tilde{z}\in\mathcal{T}(R)
if and only if
\varpi=0
in
R
and

\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0}=\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(\bar{k})_{0}\otimes_{\bar{k}}R.

We would like to show that the condition that
\tilde{z}\in\mathcal{T}(R)
is given by the vanishing of one nonzero element in
I
.

Let
\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}\}
be an
O_{F}
-basis of
\Lambda
and let
e_{0}=\varpi^{-1}{u}
. Then by the first case of the proof of Lemma
15.1.1
, we have

\mathbb{D}(X_{\bar{k}})({O_{\breve{F}}})_{0}=\langle e_{0},\ldots,e_{n}\rangle_{{O_{\breve{F}}}},\quad\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X_{\bar{k}})(\bar{k})_{0}=\langle e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}\rangle_{\bar{k}}.

Hence

\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0}=\langle e_{0},\ldots,e_{n}\rangle_{R},\quad\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{0}=\langle e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}\rangle_{S}.

A lifting of
z_{2}=(X,\iota_{X},\lambda_{X},\rho_{X})\in\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{cent}}(S)
to
\tilde{z}_{2}\in\mathcal{Z}(u)(R)
then corresponds to an
R
-hyperplane
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0}
in
\langle e_{0},\ldots,e_{n}\rangle_{R}
given by an equation

e_{0}^{*}+\lambda_{1}e_{1}^{*}+\cdots+\lambda_{n}e_{n}^{*}=0,\quad\lambda_{i}\in I,i=1,\ldots,n

such that
{u}\in\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0}
, i.e.,
\lambda_{i}\in I
for
i=1,\ldots,n
and
\varpi=0
in
R
.

Since
z_{1}=(Y,\iota_{Y},\lambda_{Y},\rho_{Y})\not\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],{\mathrm{ns}}}(S)
, we know that
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{1}=\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}^{\perp}
is determined by
\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}
. Since the cokernel of

\alpha_{*}:\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{0}\times\mathbb{D}(\overline{\mathcal{E}})(R)_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0}

is a free
R/\varpi R
-module of rank
1
, we know that
\alpha_{*}
induces an isomorphism

\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{0}\cong\langle e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}\rangle_{R}.

After changing the basis
\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}\}
we may assume that

\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{1}\cong\langle f_{1},\ldots,f_{n}\rangle_{R}

with
\langle e_{1},f_{1}\rangle_{0}=\varpi
and
\langle e_{i},f_{j}\rangle_{0}=\delta_{ij}
for
(i,j)\neq(1,1)
. Then
\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{1}^{\perp}=\langle e_{1}\rangle_{S}
, and
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{1}=\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}^{\perp}=\langle f_{1}\rangle_{S}
.

(i)

First consider the case that
z_{1}=(Y,\iota_{Y},\lambda_{Y},\rho_{Y})\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\circ}(S)
lies in the balloon stratum, cf. Definition
14.2.2
. Then
\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{1}^{\perp}=\langle e_{1}\rangle_{S}\not\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}
. We may assume that the
S
-hyperplane
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}
in
\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}=\langle e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}\rangle_{S}
is given by the equation

e_{1}^{*}+a_{2}e_{2}^{*}+\cdots+a_{n}e_{n}^{*}=0,\quad a_{i}\in S.

A lifting of
z_{1}=(Y,\iota_{Y},\lambda_{Y},\rho_{Y})\in\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}(S)
to
\tilde{z}_{1}\in\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}(R)
then corresponds to an
R
-hyperplane
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{0}
in
\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{0}=\langle e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}\rangle_{R}
given by an equation

(16.1.1)

e_{1}^{*}+\mu_{2}e_{2}+\cdots+\mu_{n-1}e_{n-1}^{*}+\mu_{n}e_{n}^{*}=0,\quad\mu_{i}\in a_{i}+I,

and an
R
-line
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{1}
in
\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{1}
generated by

f_{1}+\nu_{2}f_{2}+\cdots+\nu_{n}f_{n},\quad\nu_{i}\in I,i=2,\ldots,n

such that
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{0}
and
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{1}
are orthogonal under
\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{0}
. The orthogonality condition is equivalent to the
R
-hyperplane (
16.1.1
) being contained in the
R
-subspace defined by

\varpi e_{1}^{*}+\nu_{2}e_{2}^{*}+\cdots+\nu_{n-1}e_{n-1}^{*}+\nu_{n}e_{n}^{*}=0.

Hence

\nu_{2}=\varpi\mu_{2},\cdots,\nu_{n-1}=\varpi\mu_{n-1},\nu_{n}=\varpi\mu_{n},

and the liftings
\tilde{z}_{1}\in\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}(R)
are parametrized by
\mu_{2},\ldots,\mu_{n}\in I
.

For
\tilde{z}_{1}\in\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}(R)
lifting
z_{1}
and
\tilde{z}_{2}\in\mathcal{Z}(u)(R)
lifting
z_{2}
, we have
\tilde{z}=(\tilde{z}_{1},\tilde{z}_{2})\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}(R)
if and only if
\alpha_{*}
preserves the Hodge filtrations, namely
\alpha_{*}(\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{i})
is contained in
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{i}
for
i\in\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
. For
i=0
, the preservation of the Hodge filtrations means that

\lambda_{1}e_{1}^{*}+\cdots+\lambda_{n}e_{n}^{*}=\lambda_{1}(e_{1}^{*}+\mu_{2}e_{2}^{*}+\cdots+\mu_{n-1}e_{n-1}^{*}+\mu_{n}e_{n}^{*}).

It follows that

\lambda_{2}=\lambda_{1}\mu_{2},\ldots,\lambda_{n-1}=\lambda_{1}\mu_{n-1},\lambda_{n}=\lambda_{1}\mu_{n}.

For
i=1
, the preservation of the Hodge filtrations means that the
R
-line
\varpi f_{1}+\nu_{2}f_{2}+\cdots+\nu_{n}f_{n}
is contained in the
R
-line
f_{0}+\lambda_{1}f_{1}+\cdots+\lambda_{n}f_{n}
. Hence
\varpi=0
in
R
and

\nu_{2}=\cdots=\nu_{n}=0.

Therefore the liftings of
z\in\mathcal{T}(S)
to
\tilde{z}\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}(R)
are parametrized by
\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n-1},\lambda_{1}\in I
.

Now for
\tilde{z}=(\tilde{z}_{1},\tilde{z}_{2})\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}(R)
lifting
z\in\mathcal{T}(S)
, we have
\tilde{z}\in\mathcal{T}(R)
if and only if
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0}
is given by the equation
e_{0}^{*}=0
, which is cut out by one equation
\lambda_{1}=0
, as desired.

(ii)

Next consider the case that
z_{1}\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\dagger}(S)
lies in the link stratum, cf. Definition
14.2.2
. Then
\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{1}^{\perp}=\langle e_{1}\rangle_{S}\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}
. Without loss of generality we may assume that the
S
-hyperplane
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}
in
\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}=\langle e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}\rangle_{S}
is given by the equation
e_{n}^{*}=0
. A lifting of
z_{1}=(Y,\iota_{Y},\lambda_{Y},\rho_{Y})\in\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}(S)
to
\tilde{z}_{1}\in\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}(R)
then corresponds to an
R
-hyperplane
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{0}
in
\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{0}=\langle e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}\rangle_{R}
given by an equation

(16.1.2)

\mu_{1}e_{1}^{*}+\cdots+\mu_{n-1}e_{n-1}^{*}+e_{n}^{*}=0,\quad\mu_{i}\in I,i=1,\ldots,n-1

and an
R
-line
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{1}
in
\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{1}
generated by

f_{1}+\nu_{2}f_{2}+\cdots+\nu_{n}f_{n},\quad\nu_{i}\in I,i=2,\ldots,n

such that
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{0}
and
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{1}
and orthogonal under
\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{0}
. The orthogonality condition is equivalent to the
R
-hyperplanes (
16.1.2
) being contained in the
R
-subspace

\varpi e_{1}^{*}+\nu_{2}e_{2}^{*}+\cdots+\nu_{n-1}e_{n-1}^{*}+\nu_{n}e_{n}^{*}=0,

which is equivalent to

\nu_{n}(\mu_{1}e_{1}^{*}+\cdots+\mu_{n-1}e_{n-1}^{*}+e_{n}^{*})=\varpi e_{1}^{*}+\nu_{2}e_{2}^{*}+\cdots+\nu_{n}e_{n}^{*},

i.e.,

\varpi=\nu_{n}\mu_{1},\nu_{2}=\nu_{n}\mu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{n-1}=\nu_{n}\mu_{n-1}.

Since
I^{2}=0
we know that
\varpi=0
in
R
and

\nu_{2}=\cdots=\nu_{n-1}=0.

Hence the liftings
\tilde{z}_{1}\in\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}(R)
are parametrized by
\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n-1},\nu_{n}\in I
.

For
\tilde{z}_{1}\in\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}(R)
lifting
z_{1}
, and
\tilde{z}_{2}\in\mathcal{Z}(u)(R)
lifting
z_{2}
, we have
\tilde{z}=(\tilde{z}_{1},\tilde{z}_{2})\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}(R)
if and only if
\alpha_{*}
preserves the Hodge filtrations. For
i=0
, the preservation of the Hodge filtrations means that

\lambda_{1}e_{1}^{*}+\cdots+\lambda_{n}e_{n}^{*}=\lambda_{n}(\mu_{1}e_{1}^{*}+\cdots+\mu_{n-1}e_{n-1}^{*}+e_{n}^{*}).

It follows that

\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{n}\mu_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n-1}=\lambda_{n}\mu_{n-1}.

For
i=1
, the preservation of the Hodge filtrations means that the
R
-line
\varpi f_{1}+\nu_{2}f_{2}+\cdots+\nu_{n}f_{n}
is contained in the
R
-line
f_{0}+\lambda_{1}f_{1}+\cdots+\lambda_{n}f_{n}
. Hence
\varpi=0
in
R
and

\nu_{2}=\cdots=\nu_{n}=0.

Therefore the liftings of
z\in\mathcal{Z}(u)(S)
to
\tilde{z}\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}(R)
are parametrized by
\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n-1},\lambda_{n}\in I
.

Now for
\tilde{z}=(\tilde{z}_{1},\tilde{z}_{2})\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}(R)
lifting
z\in\mathcal{Z}(u)(S)
, we have
\tilde{z}\in\mathcal{T}(R)
if and only if
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0}
is given by the equation
e_{0}^{*}=0
, which is cut out by one equation
\lambda_{n}=0
, as desired.
∎

Corollary 16.1.2
.

Let
z\in\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[1],{\rm exc}}(\bar{k})
. Then

\dim_{\bar{k}}T_{z}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[1],{\rm exc}}=n-1.

Proof.

By the proof of Proposition
16.1.1
applied to
R=\bar{k}[\varepsilon]/\varepsilon^{2}
,
I=(\varepsilon)
and
S=\bar{k}
, we know that the liftings of
z\in\mathcal{T}(\bar{k})
to
\mathcal{T}(\bar{k}[\varepsilon]/\varepsilon^{2})
are parametrized by
n-1
free variables in
I=(\varepsilon)
. Hence
\dim_{\bar{k}}T_{z}(\mathcal{T})=n-1
.
∎

Definition 16.1.3
.

For
\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W}_{n})
, write
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}:=\pi_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{V}(\Lambda({u})))
, a closed
\bar{k}
-subscheme of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
.

Proposition 16.1.4
.

For
\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W}_{n})
, the morphism
\pi_{1}
induces an isomorphism
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)\simeq\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
. In particular, there is a decomposition

(16.1.3)

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[1],{\rm exc}}=\bigsqcup_{\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W}_{n})}\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}\simeq\bigsqcup_{\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W}_{n})}\mathbb{P}^{n-1}.

Proof.

By Lemma
14.7.1
(
iii
), we know that
\pi_{1}
restricts to a morphism of
\bar{k}
-schemes
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)
which induces a bijection on
\bar{k}
-points. By working systematically with a Cohen ring instead of the Witt ring, we obtain that
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)
induces a bijection on
k^{\prime}
-points for any field extension
k^{\prime}/\bar{k}
. Thus
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)
is birational and universally bijective. Since
\pi_{1}
is finite (Proposition
16.3.1
below), we know that
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)
is proper and therefore a universal homeomorphism. Hence
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}
is irreducible of dimension
n-1
. It follows from Corollary
16.1.2
that
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}
is smooth and hence reduced. Now the morphism
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)
is a birational, bijective and proper morphism with an integral source and a normal target, hence it is an isomorphism by the Zariski main theorem.
∎

Corollary 16.1.5
.

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[1],{\rm exc}}=\pi_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet})^{\mathrm{red}}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
16.1.4
, we know that
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[1],{\rm exc}}
is a reduced closed subscheme of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
. The result then follows as it has the same set of
\bar{k}
-points as the reduced closed subscheme
\pi_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet})^{\mathrm{red}}
, by Lemma
14.7.1
(
iii
).
∎

16.2.
The morphism
\pi_{2}
and the regularity of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}

Proposition 16.2.1
.

The restriction of
\pi_{2}
to
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1],\mathrm{ns}}_{n}}
induces an isomorphism
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1],\mathrm{ns}}_{n}}\simeq\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{ns}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
14.7.1
(
i
,
ii
), we know that
\pi_{2}
induces a bijection
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1],\mathrm{ns}}_{n}}(\bar{k})\simeq\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{ns}}(\bar{k})
. Since
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
is formally locally of finite type, it remains to show that the restriction of
\pi_{2}
to
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1],\mathrm{ns}}_{n}}
is formally étale. Let
R
be a local noetherian
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-algebra on which
\varpi
is nilpotent. Let
I\subseteq R
be an ideal such that
I^{2}=0
. Let
S=R/I
. Let
z=(X,\iota_{X},\lambda_{X},\rho_{X},Y,\iota_{Y},\lambda_{Y},\rho_{Y})\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1],\mathrm{ns}}_{n}}(S)
and
z_{2}:=\pi_{2}(z)=(X,\iota_{X},\lambda_{X},\rho_{X})\in\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{ns}}(S)
. To show the formal étaleness, we need to show that for any lift
\tilde{z}_{2}\in\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{ns}}(R)
of
z_{2}
, there exists a unique lift
\tilde{z}\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1],\mathrm{ns}}_{n}}(R)
of
z
such that
\pi_{2}(\tilde{z})=\tilde{z}_{2}
. Without loss of generality we may assume that
R
has residue field
\bar{k}
.

By the second case of the proof of Lemma
15.1.1
, there exists a
\bar{k}
-basis
\{\bar{e}_{0},\bar{e}_{1},,\ldots,\bar{e}_{n}\}
of
\mathbb{D}(X_{\bar{k}})(\bar{k})_{0}
such that

\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X_{\bar{k}})(\bar{k})_{0}=\langle\bar{e}_{0},\bar{e}_{1},\ldots,\bar{e}_{n-1}\rangle_{\bar{k}},\quad{u}_{*}(\mathbb{D}(\overline{\mathcal{E}})(\bar{k})_{0})=\langle\bar{e}_{0}\rangle_{\bar{k}}.

Since

\alpha_{*}:\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{0}\oplus\mathbb{D}(\overline{\mathcal{E}})(R)_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0}

has cokernel a free
R/\varpi R
-module of rank 1, we may lift
\{\bar{e}_{0},\ldots,\bar{e}_{n}\}
to an
R
-basis
\{e_{0},e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}\}
of
\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0}
and find an
R
-basis
\{f_{1},\ldots,f_{n}\}
of
\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{0}
such that

\alpha_{*}(f_{i})=e_{i},\ i=1,\ldots,n-1,\quad\alpha_{*}(f_{n})=\varpi e_{n},\quad\alpha_{*}(\mathbb{D}(\overline{\mathcal{E}})(R)_{0})=\langle e_{0}\rangle_{R}.

Assume that the
S
-hyperplane
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}\subseteq\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}
is defined by an equation

\lambda_{1}f_{1}^{*}+\cdots+\lambda_{n-1}f_{n-1}^{*}+\lambda_{n}f_{n}^{*}=0,\ \lambda_{i}\in S.

Since
z\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1],\mathrm{ns}}_{n}}(S)
, we know by Lemma
14.7.1
(
iv
) that
\pi_{1}(z)\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],{\mathrm{ns}}}(S)
and hence

\mathbb{D}(Y_{\bar{k}})(\bar{k})_{1}^{\perp}=\langle f_{n}\rangle_{\bar{k}}\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y_{\bar{k}})(\bar{k})_{0}.

In particular, we know that
\lambda_{n}\not\in S^{\times}
and thus there exists some
1\leq i\leq n-1
such that
\lambda_{i}\in S^{\times}
. Without loss of generality we may assume that
\lambda_{1}=1
. The fact that
\alpha_{*}
preserves the Hodge filtrations over
S
implies that the
S
-hyperplane
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{0}\subseteq\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}
is defined by an equation

e_{1}^{*}+\lambda_{2}e_{2}^{*}+\cdots+\lambda_{n-1}e_{n-1}^{*}+\lambda^{\prime}_{n}e_{n}^{*}=0,\quad\lambda_{n}^{\prime}\in S,\varpi\lambda_{n}^{\prime}=\lambda_{n}.

The lift
\tilde{z}_{2}
of
z_{2}
corresponds a hyperplane
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0}\subset\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0}
lifting
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{0}
, defined by an equation

e_{1}^{*}+\mu_{2}e_{2}^{*}+\cdots+\mu_{n}e_{n}^{*}=0,\quad\mu_{i}\in\lambda_{i}+I,i=2,\ldots,n-1,\ \mu_{n}\in\lambda_{n}^{\prime}+I.

A lift
\tilde{z}
of
z
such that
\pi_{2}(\tilde{z})=\tilde{z}_{2}
corresponds to an
R
-hyperplane
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{0}\subseteq\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{0}
lifting
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(S)_{0}
defined by an equation

f_{1}^{*}+\nu_{2}f_{2}^{*}+\cdots+\nu_{n}f_{n}^{*}=0,\quad\nu_{i}\in\lambda_{i}+I,i=2,\ldots,n

and an
R
-line
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{1}
lifting
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)
such that
\alpha_{*}
preserves the Hodge filtrations over
R
. For
i=0
, the preservation of the Hodge filtrations means that

\nu_{i}=\mu_{i},i=2,\ldots,n-1,\ \nu_{n}=\varpi\mu_{n}.

Hence such a lift
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{0}
exists and is uniquely determined by
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{0}
. A similar argument shows that
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(Y)(R)_{1}
also exists and is uniquely determined by
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(X)(R)_{1}
. Thus such a lift
\tilde{z}
exists and is uniquely determined by
\tilde{z}_{2}
.
∎

Corollary 16.2.2
.

The formal scheme
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
is regular of dimension
n
.

Proof.

By (
16.1.3
), we know that
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[1],{\rm exc}}
is regular. It follows from Proposition
16.1.1
that
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
admits a regular Cartier divisor
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[1],{\rm exc}}
, hence
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
is regular at all points
z\in\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[1],{\rm exc}}(\bar{k})
. By Proposition
16.2.1
and the fact that
\mathcal{Z}(u)
is regular, we know that
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
is also regular at all points
z\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1],\mathrm{ns}}_{n}}(\bar{k})
. Therefore
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
is regular at all points
z\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}(\bar{k})
.
∎

16.3.
The morphism
\pi_{1}

Proposition 16.3.1
.

The morphism
\pi_{1}:{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}
is finite flat of degree
q+1
, étale along
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],{\mathrm{ns}}}
, and totally ramified along
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}
.

Proof.

Let
S
be a noetherian
{O_{\breve{F}}}
-algebra on which
\varpi
is nilpotent. Note that any
S
-point
z=(X,\iota_{X},\lambda_{X},\rho_{X},Y,\iota_{Y},\lambda_{Y},\rho_{Y})\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}(S)
is determined by
z_{1}=(Y,\iota_{Y},\lambda_{Y},\rho_{Y})\in\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}(S)
together with
\ker(\tilde{\alpha}:Y\times\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S}\rightarrow X)\subseteq(Y\times\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S})[\varpi]
. Moreover, the condition for a subscheme of the projective
S
-scheme
(Y\times\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S})[\varpi]
to appear as
\ker(\tilde{\alpha}:Y\times\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S}\rightarrow X)
for some
z\in\pi_{1}^{-1}(z_{1})
is a closed condition. Hence by the theory of Hilbert schemes
[
5
, Thm. 3.1]
, the morphism
\pi_{1}
is relatively representable by a projective scheme (hence proper). Since
\pi_{1}
is quasi-finite by Lemma
14.7.1
(
iii
)(
iv
), we know that
\pi_{1}
is finite.

Since
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
is regular (hence Cohen–Macaulay) by Corollary
16.2.2
and
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}
is regular by Proposition
14.2.4
, we know that
\pi_{1}
is flat by the miracle flatness theorem.

The generic degree of
\pi_{1}
is equal to the number of type 0 lattices containing a fixed type 2 lattice in an
F/F_{0}
-hermitian space of dimension
n+1
, which is
q+1
. Hence
\pi_{1}
is finite flat of degree
q+1
. Comparing the degree
q+1
with the size of fibers at
\bar{k}
-points in Lemma
14.7.1
(
iii
)(
iv
) it follows that
\pi_{1}
is étale along
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],{\mathrm{ns}}}
and totally ramified along
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}
.
∎

16.4.
The self-intersection number of the exceptional divisor

Recall from Definition
16.1.3
the closed subscheme
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}
of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
.

Proposition 16.4.1
.

For
\Lambda\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W}_{n})
, the normal bundle
N_{\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}/{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}}(-1)
. In particular, the
n
-fold self-intersection of
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}
in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
is equal to

\chi({\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}},\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}\cap^{\mathbb{L}}\cdots\cap^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda})=(-1)^{n-1}.

Proof.

By Proposition
16.1.1
, we know that the normal bundle
N_{\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}/{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}}
is a line bundle on
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}\simeq\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
, and hence
N_{\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}/{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}}(m)
for a unique integer
m
. Let
\Lambda^{\flat}\subseteq\Lambda
be a type 0 lattice of rank
n-2
. Then we have a closed immersion
\delta:\mathcal{N}_{3}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[1]}
(
[
15
, §2.11]
) which identifies
\mathcal{N}_{3}
with the Kudla–Rapoport cycle
\mathcal{Z}(\Lambda^{\flat})\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{n+1}^{[1]}
. Let
\mathcal{Z}^{\flat}({u})=\mathcal{Z}(u)\cap\mathcal{Z}(\Lambda^{\flat})\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{3}
be a valuation one Kudla–Rapoport divisor on
\mathcal{N}_{3}
and
\pi_{2}^{\flat}:\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{\flat}({u})
the natural projection. Then
\delta
induces a cartesian diagram

\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}^{\flat}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\delta}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}^{\flat}({u})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}(u)}

Let
\Lambda_{2}
be the orthogonal complement of
\Lambda^{\flat}
in
\Lambda
and
\mathbb{W}_{2}:=\Lambda_{2,F}
. Then
\Lambda_{2}\in{\mathrm{Vert}}^{0}(\mathbb{W}_{2})
and
\tilde{\delta}
identifies
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{2}}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{2}
with a projective line in
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}
. Hence

N_{\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{2}}/\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{1}_{2}}=\tilde{\delta}^{*}(N_{\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}/{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}})\simeq\tilde{\delta}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}}(m))=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{2}}}(m).

Thus to show that
m=-1
we are reduced to the case
n=2
.

Now assume that
n=2
. Since
\pi_{1}
is finite flat of degree
q+1
and totally ramified along
\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)
(Proposition
16.3.1
), by the projection formula we have

(q+1)\cdot\chi({\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}},\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}\cap^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda})=\chi(\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n},\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)\cap^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)).

Since
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}
is a regular formal surface, whose special fiber is a strict normal crossing divisor with exactly
q+1
irreducible curves intersecting
\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)
, we know that

\chi(\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n},\mathcal{V}(\Lambda)\cap^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{V}(\Lambda))=-(q+1).

Hence
\chi({\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}},\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda}\cap^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda})=-1
, which is equivalent to
m=-1
when
n=2
, as desired.
∎

16.5.
Proof of Theorem
14.6.2

Item (
i
) is proved in Corollary
16.2.2
. Item (
ii
) is proved in Proposition
16.3.1
. Item (
iii
) is proved in Proposition
16.2.1
. Item (
iv
) is proved in Proposition
16.1.1
and Proposition
16.1.4
. Item (
v
) is proved in Proposition
16.4.1
.

16.6.
Singularities of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
and proof of Conjecture
3.5.1

By Corollary
16.1.5
, we have
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1],\mathrm{ns}}_{n}}(\bar{k})=\pi_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],{\mathrm{ns}}})(\bar{k})
.
Also let
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}^{[1],\dagger}_{n}=\pi_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\dagger})
and
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}^{[1],\circ}_{n}=\pi_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\circ})
be the inverse images of the link stratum, resp. the balloon stratum, cf. Definition
14.2.2
.

Theorem 16.6.1
.

Let
\hat{O}_{\tilde{z}}
be the complete local ring of
{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}
at
\tilde{z}\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}}(\bar{k})
. Then

\hat{O}_{\tilde{z}}\simeq\begin{cases}{O_{\breve{F}}}[[T_{1},\ldots,T_{n-1}]],&\tilde{z}\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{[1],\mathrm{ns}}_{n}}(\bar{k}),\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt{O_{\breve{F}}}[[T_{1},\ldots,T_{n}]]/(T_{1}^{q+1}-\varpi),&\tilde{z}\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}^{[1],\circ}_{n}(\bar{k}),\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt{O_{\breve{F}}}[[T_{1},\ldots,T_{n}]]/(T_{1}^{q+1}T_{2}-\varpi),&\tilde{z}\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}^{[1],\dagger}_{n}(\bar{k}).\\
\end{cases}

In particular, Conjecture
3.5.1
holds when
r=1
.

Proof.

Let
z=\pi_{1}(\tilde{z})\in\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}(\bar{k})
. Let
\hat{O}_{z}
be the complete local ring of
\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}
at
z
. Then
\pi_{1}
induces a morphism
\hat{O}_{z}\rightarrow\hat{O}_{\tilde{z}}
. When
z\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],{\mathrm{ns}}}(\bar{k})
, the morphism
\hat{O}_{z}\rightarrow\hat{O}_{\tilde{z}}
is an isomorphism by Proposition
16.3.1
. Hence when
z\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],{\mathrm{ns}}}(\bar{k})
, we have
\hat{O}_{\tilde{z}}\simeq{O_{\breve{F}}}[[T_{1},\ldots,T_{n-1}]]
by Proposition
14.2.4
.

When
z\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}(\bar{k})
, by Proposition
14.2.4
we have
\hat{O}_{z}\simeq{O_{\breve{F}}}[[X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}]]/(X_{1}-\varpi)
(resp.
\hat{O}_{z}\simeq\allowbreak{O_{\breve{F}}}[[X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}]]/(X_{1}X_{2}-\varpi)
when
z\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\circ}(\bar{k})
(resp. when
z\in\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\dagger}(\bar{k})
). Here we choose
X_{1}=0
to be a local equation defining the Cartier divisor
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}\subseteq\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{n}
at
z
and
X_{2},\ldots,X_{n}
to be a regular system of parameters for
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[1],\bullet}
at
z
. Let
T_{i}\in\hat{O}_{\tilde{z}}
be the image of
X_{i}
under
\hat{O}_{z}\rightarrow\hat{O}_{\tilde{z}}
for
i\geq 2
. Let
T_{1}\in\hat{O}_{\tilde{z}}
such that the local equation
T_{1}=0
defines the Cartier divisor
\pi_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{ss}})
at
\tilde{z}
(cf. Proposition
16.1.1
). Then by Proposition
16.3.1
and Corollary
16.1.5
, we know that
T_{2},\ldots,T_{n}
form a regular system of parameters for
\pi_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}(u)^{\mathrm{ss}})
at
\tilde{z}
and the ideal
(T_{1}^{q+1})\subseteq\hat{O}_{\tilde{z}}
equals the image of
(X_{1})
. The result then follows.
∎
